## D212 — The foundation: who acts is a participant read from the records, its work a capability read by one rule and its role a configured record composed from capabilities; where a line's work goes next is one pure decision both loops call, its account by one rule and every move one durable record that owns its delivery; what is said is one closed vocabulary of events, minted once at the commit or at the judgement that owns the fact, every telling a projection of it; the desktop's state and configuration kept in one SQLite store; the driver cut into subdomains in one acyclic map; reached in rows, the first the account rule (2026-10-25)

**Decision (FOUNDATION1, proposed 2026-10-25).** **Status: Part A accepted 2026-10-25 and building; Part B accepted 2026-10-25 after round 8 (the note of Part B's acceptance below).** Part A was
accepted by the steward after both makers' reads of round 4 and their named changes (design §17, §18; the note of that date below); row 1a-i,
accepted alone earlier on the Opus read, takes the corrected description of where its walk is asked (design §18). Part B's round 5 was written
from main at `eced2688e` (design §19; the round-5 note below) and read by both makers; round 6 answers both reads (design §20; the round-6 note
below), round 7 both makers' reads of round 6 (design §21; the round-7 note below), round 8 both makers' reads of round 7 (design §22; the
round-8 note below), and both makers read round 8 before any of Part B is accepted. Part A is these
clauses and rows, which only reorganise:
- point 1 as the read, its binding read from today's `<s>.harness.json`;
- point 2's work read from the marks by one rule and pushed as `work`;
- point 7's rule as LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2 built it, each caller's earliest reset read over its own set as today;
- point 14;
- point 18's map and folder moves;
- rows 1a-i, 1b, 1c, 1mod-0, 1mod-i to 1mod-xxi and 1u.

Those points' other clauses are Part B's (design §17, O4-10): point 1's binding moved into the desktop store; point 2's `actor` as the seeded id,
the role and its revision on the binding, and the census against the catalogue; and point 7's recorded account pick, its settled selection and its
one earliest reset over the accounts a line may take. **Part B** (those clauses and every other point and row: the move record and its claim, the
open and the words' settlement across the two stores, the journal and the news, the desktop store and its families, the role catalogue's
authority, the downgrade table) stays proposed, in round 5 and rounds after it, its rows waiting (the round-4 and round-5 notes below). Round 1
was the synthesis of three sealed proposals (Claude's, Codex's, and an independent Opus reader's in Fable's place under D191's
amendment of 2026-10-23), each written from one brief and one survey. Both makers read round 1 (`docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md`):
the independent Opus reader as a foundation, 20 findings, 3 blocking, accept after named changes; Codex, 18 findings, 16 blocking, another
round; round 2 answered every finding in the design's §12 and carried the owner's direction on roles (point 16). Both makers read round 2:
the Opus reader, 14 findings, 6 blocking, accept after named changes; Codex, 14 findings, 10 blocking, another round. Round 3 answers every
finding in the design's §15 and carries two more directions of the owner's (points 17 and 18). Both makers read round 3: the Opus reader, 16
findings, 2 blocking, accept after named changes; Codex, 17 findings, 11 blocking, another round; round 4 answers every finding in the design's
§16. It changes a foundation (the records' data model, a protocol between processes, concurrency, and decided rules other designs rest on), and
some of its rows grant or refuse authority or delete records, so by D191's note of 2026-10-24 no part of it is accepted on one maker's read.
The contract is `docs/2026-10-25-foundation-design.md`; its §1 compares the proposals question by question and says what
each choice rejects, and its §0.2 corrects the brief, the survey and the proposals against the code on main at `92ac2bd7e`, whose `src/`
`c8eccf30e` keeps unchanged; round 3 checked the code at `2a08bde4a`, round 4 at `e8f1aed4f`. Nothing is built.

The owner directed on 2026-10-25 that Daoris, grown through many foundational designs each with a mechanism of its own, be given
a structure that names the Fabric, the actor roles and the event system, designed under D191 and never built as a big-bang
rewrite; and, the same day, that actors and roles not be hard-coded, each role configurable and set up into the company D193
pictures; that configuration and state move from JSON files to SQLite as they grow complex; and that the full update analyze the driver
into its subdomains. The owner left the line between JSON and SQLite, and the map's names and grouping, to the steward, and confirmed
*Roles* as one subdomain's name. Read at `92ac2bd7e` (the survey, `docs/2026-10-25-foundation-survey.md`, read at `0e195ee61`): thirteen kinds of
participant, each with its own rule, admission, choice and record; fourteen paths in two loops each choosing an agent, an
account, a conversation and a context its own way; fifteen channels each with its own kinds, ids, order and readers; and 51 of the
273 fix-log entries of 2026-10-15 to 2026-10-25 from two of them answering one question differently.

1. **A participant is a read, never a store** (design §2.1). One function builds it from the stores that hold each kind of subject,
   in the driver library, and its twin in the service answers the connector; its fields are its id (the record's, unchanged on every
   wire, or the identity Daoris already issues a brainstorm voice, a holder or a native child), its work, its role, whom it serves, its
   line, its runtime binding, its visibility and its authority. It is D173 ADR-B's `Participant` and `RuntimeBinding`. Its binding, today
   `<s>.harness.json`, moves into the desktop store as its one home (point 17); no table of issued identities is added until a subject needs one.
2. **Its work is a capability read from the marks by one rule, never stored** (§2.2), as D203 point 1 has it: `review`, `partner`,
   `help`, `intake`, `ask-daoris`, `chat`, `build` and `brainstorm`, with another machine's, a holder and a child placed with no work
   here; `design` is admitted as `build`. `actor` names the seeded always-on role of the start's work, so every build's continuation reads an
   id its run knows; the role a start took and its revision are kept on its binding, held to the work by a census. The page reads the work
   pushed as `work`, an additive field beside the wire's `kind` (driven or chat), judged in .NET, with an older host's fallback; it never
   travels. The cap's *the driver's own session* is a property of the work.
3. **Admission is four layers, a table each, keyed by capability** (§2.3): may it start (the driver), may it call (the connector), may
   it act (the pipe), how it is contained (the launcher, unchanged: a piece fails closed, an ordinary start runs as today where the
   launcher cannot contain it), sharing one classification and answering closed refusal codes; a session Daoris names that the
   connector cannot place is served reads and refused every mutation, a brainstorm voice placed first through its box and served its
   result tool. A work's policy is one shape read from today's keys unchanged, common fields and a typed requirement per work. The
   person sees a partner drawn as a partner, Ask Daoris refused `helper_ask`, and a reviewer's and a voice's calls beyond their handed
   rules refused at the server on every door.
4. **Every start goes through one start plan** (§2.4), which finds the role that takes its work in its place and whose answer is a
   settled selection carried to the open, its reservation counted at the selection and a probe uncounted; one walk, one set of pass
   codes, one re-ask; a continuation takes its record's role and asked choice. A role holder and a meeting are the two starts the census
   names outside it.
5. **An actor and an appointed role are one configured concept, the role, in two exclusive shapes** (§2.5, §2.7): a working role takes work
   and holds no authority, which an actor is; an appointed role holds acts and takes no work, which the supervisor and the steward are. A
   holder's authority is the appointment's, proven per act: an act is admitted only within the code's act table, the appointment's grant (the
   acts the build offered at *Appoint*, snapshotted, with the steward's set-up tick and its scope) and the role's configuration as it stands, at
   admission and again at its run; every narrowing lowers each live grant itself and nothing but *Appoint* raises it, so an edit narrows a live
   appointment and widens none, a restored act included; the steward's desk decline stays outside every grant. The two role doors become one act
   form and one proof; the appointments, the act judge and the act handlers stay in the shell's library, the act vocabulary moved down into the
   driver.
6. **Where a line's next words or work go is one pure decision**, `LineCourse.Decide` (§3.1), which the look calls for a driven line and
   the chat runner for a conversation, each executing it as today. Its answers are resume, move (when, where, how), start (a new line
   with no `from`, for start-from), wait, hold and never, each with the facts it read, the words' state among them, which the executor
   checks before it acts; its facts include the answers-alone hold and the boundary the line stands at; a move standing makes its old
   part's own go-on never; each automatic hand-over passes the gate its door has today. The transient retry stays inside the run.
7. **The account rule is stated once** (§3.2): a resume asks for its own account; where that account cools and an account the line may
   take is ready, the line goes on now in a new part there, for a driven record, a repository conversation and a closed quest's session
   (LIMITSWITCH1's decision, LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2 built inside it; the closed quest's session through the ended move, by the
   steward's reading that the decision's *ended record* includes it, which the owner may overrule); Ask Daoris waits for its own outside a limit, and a conversation
   whose own account is refused, signed out or gone keeps today's door, each an open question for the owner (design §8). Otherwise it waits
   for the earliest reset of exactly the accounts it may take and of its own account where it may resume, an account that cannot run adding
   none (*Part B's*, row 1a-iii: until it lands each caller reads its own set as today, row 1a-i; design §17, O4-3), and where none of them has
   a reset it holds naming the repair. An account the person picked and the start's binding keeps waits for its own, the person's hand-over press included; an
   agent pick walks its agent's accounts; a record with no kept account pick is an agent pick; the other doors' named accounts keep today's
   reading. A new part takes one settled selection, the briefed move and both limit moves included.
8. **Every move keeps one durable move record** (§3.3), a row of the desktop store, owning its delivery and recovery through its phases
   (from `old-ended` for a move from an ended record), executed by the executor of its kind: a conversation's by the shell whose chat runner
   runs it, a driven line's by the loop whose look runs it, the instance's or a `--share` loop's. It is claimed by that executor's process run
   and taken over only from a run dead by the lease's own liveness test, never because a role passed, so the claim holds through every effect a
   live run makes; every external effect is fenced by a mark written, under a claim check, before it, a dead run's begun effect held and never
   repeated by itself. The destination is opened atomically under the move's key in the service, which keeps the open's result in a table of its
   own and moves the old part's waiting words in the same step, so a lost answer never opens or moves twice; against an older host a lost open
   is held. No old box is written beside it: an older build recovers a newer build's move from the ledger alone, by a table phase by phase, the
   newer build reading the line's head before it delivers in every phase, and a box an older build writes is imported once the start's
   confirmation is settled. SWITCHCTX1's carry is its `how: carry` and its two carry phases.
9. **Words hold across a move by construction** (§3.4): a word keeps its whole identity wherever it is held; the line's head is judged
   inside the say door's one exclusive step by one service function across kinds; the open moves the old part's words under their producer
   keys with transfer ids, ahead of later words; every word is kept on `said` before anything holds it, once the course holds the moving
   record's old part; an attempt is acknowledged word by word before the wire, each attempt kept with one disposition (taken; refused at a
   limit, only where the refusal is conclusive, and carried once; uncertain), never reclassified, and a word's settlement kept by the service
   in the statement that takes it, by line and producer key, read inside every keeping door's own step, the helper's and partner's desks
   included, so a word once attempted is never sent again by itself, a producer's retry is answered as kept and an uncertain word is said; one
   predicate says which words a new part carries.
10. **D210's four kinds are the decision's failure input** for every door and kind of run (§3.5).
11. **An event is one envelope with two minters and three orders** (§4.1): a record event a raw transition journalled by trigger in the
    statement that writes the record, guarded by what changed, never by a mirror, with no logic that raises, carrying an allow-listed snapshot
    of the facts its coding needs, into a journal in the service's database bounded per row and per table, which never travels and wakes its
    readers by a family of its own, coded in .NET at projection from the row alone; a machine event minted by the
    hub of the process that runs the work, at the judgement that owns the fact, the lease's loop alone telling plugins and outside
    readers; every process reading the journal from its come-up. A store's position, a hub's run and sequence, and a slice's epoch and
    sequence order three things and are never one. Records stay the truth: nothing is replayed into state, and what a trim loses is said.
12. **One closed vocabulary**, `EventCodes` beside `NoteCodes` and extending EVENTHOOK1's grammar (§4.2): each code with its facts,
    privacy class and rendering family, subject-specific where facts differ; an end one event with its note's reason and one class,
    today's four sets of *ended* four declared filters of it. A sentence is composed per reader and language, never parsed back.
13. **Every channel that tells that something happened is a projection** (§4.3): pushed slices stay state, command and held answers stay
    answers, the event file, the lineage and the channel stay stores; the notices have one owner to which every teller submits, each with
    its own cursor; news, keyed by its occurrence (a record's id and its transition number, one key whichever path finds it), is told at most
    once by a durable said-set claimed before the showing, never replayed, the said-set and the news a teller may tell sharing one horizon so a
    forgotten key is never told again (§4.5).
14. **The names** (§6): *Fabric*, *participant*, *capability*, *move* and *event* are code and prose only, with no Chinese until one reaches
    the page; a participant's capability is its *work*, D203's helper, partner and reviewer among them; the role is **角色** on the page in both
    its shapes, and **任命的角色** where the appointed one must be said (the steward's decision of round 4 on both readers' reads), since a role is
    one concept in two shapes; seven shipped strings say 角色 for it, and the ten that say 职责 for the appointed role take 角色 in row 1u (design
    §17, O4-2); **职务**, round 3's word, is withdrawn, its reason having separated two screen titles rather than two concepts; *actor* stays a
    code and prose word for a working role's staffing, its glossary entry pointing at *role*, since two terms may not share one Chinese name; the configured set
    is the *role catalogue* and its subdomain *Roles*, *the company* staying D193's narrative; the line of work stays the session (会话) and
    `Entry` in code; the design's types are named apart from their subdomains' namespaces (`LineCourse`, `Participant.Of`, `ParticipantWork.Of`).
15. **Reached in rows** (§10), FOUNDATION1a-i to 1y-ii, the store's 1z-i to 1z-xiv and the map's 1mod-0 to 1mod-xxi, each its own branch
    behind today's tests, in two parts. **Part A** first: the account rule over LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2 as built (1a-i), a word's
    identity (1b), the participant's work (1c), the structure gate (1mod-0), the role's name (1u), and the driver's folders moved one subdomain a
    row with their cuts (1mod-i to 1mod-xxi), each changing no behaviour and none standing on a Part B contract. **Part B** after its own
    acceptance: the store and its families, the account pick's provenance and the briefed move's walk, attempts and the words' settlement, the
    keyed open (1d-i) before every row that moves words, the course, the move record, the journal and the news, the act vocabulary moved down
    (1w-0), the catalogue's rows after the start plan and the configuration's import. Rows q, r, t, v, w-0, y-i, y-ii, z-ii, z-iv, z-ix and z-xi
    grant, refuse or rehome authority or delete, and are built only after both makers' reads. The namespace rows come last, in neither part.
16. **Capabilities in code, roles in configuration** (§2.7; the owner's direction of 2026-10-25, the brief §8). The code keeps one closed
    vocabulary of capabilities: the work a participant can take, the acts an appointed role may make (each with its admissible scope kind
    and its offered flag), the doors each may use and the containment each runs under, with each capability's admission. A role is a record
    of the role catalogue, in the desktop store, judged by one judge for every door, in one of two exclusive shapes: a working role (its name
    in English and Chinese, the work it takes, its staffing, D205's ordered choices, and its scope) or an appointed role (its name, its
    authority from the act vocabulary, and its scope). A role carries no doors of its own. D205's eight actors and the supervisor and the
    steward are the seeded roles, read per id: each id the seed's until the catalogue holds a record of it, so an upgrade writes and changes
    nothing and a first change touches one id; a role of a work that may not be off cannot be removed. The person adds, changes or removes a
    working role, or Ask Daoris proposes and the person's press applies; an appointed role's authority is edited only in the window, and takes
    effect only at the next *Appoint*; an owner's act stays the owner's whatever a role says, and appointing stays the owner's press in the
    window alone. MODELROLE1l's Design actor becomes a configured *Designer* role taking `design`, the only code its capability and the
    quest's `weight`.
17. **Where state is kept: one desktop store** (§13; the owner's direction of 2026-10-25, the brief §9, the line the steward's by the owner's
    leave). SQLite holds what Daoris owns and more than one reader or writer reads and sets; JSON stays for formats others own, tracked
    repository records, build manifests and files read before the store opens, append-only streams, the credentials in `profiles/`, and
    `driver.json` as the configuration's export and import alone. One file, `data/desktop.db`, machine-local and never synced, owned by the
    driver library through one class, opened by the shell, by `daoris-driver` as the headless loop and as each verb (the Node CLI's management
    verbs reaching it by spawning it) and read-only by the GitDoor, the driver and the door taking `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite`, pinned as the
    service's, as their first package; never by the service, which keeps its own store for sessions, quests, asks, the channel and the event
    journal, and the tables this design adds there: the keyed open's record, a word's settlement and a record's transition number, none on the
    record. It holds the Fabric's records (the bindings, the appointments with their grants, the role catalogue), every setting of today's
    `driver.json`, the machine-local state more than one process writes, and every record this design adds on the desktop (the process runs,
    moves, words in transfer, cursors, the said-set), each family's tables its owning subdomain's, the appointments the shell's. Every write is one
    transaction under the write lock taken at its start, which reads the store's oldest-writer stamp before it writes; a move and its words in
    transfer are written together and its open is claimed once. Migrations are forward only, a store stamped past a build's writing refused for
    its writes, a migration that raises the stamp run only once the start's confirmation is settled. Each JSON family moves in its own row,
    imported once: a family an older build only reads at the first start, left in place as the backup; one it would act on once the start's
    confirmation is settled (D139), set aside under its import's receipt; nothing written through but an authority's narrowing, so no older
    build ever reads back a revoked grant; a backup an older build changed offered back as an import at the return, a change of authority through
    the person's own press. For the configuration the store, not the file, is authoritative, and a hand edit of `driver.json` takes effect by an
    import (D50, amended).
18. **The driver's subdomains, one acyclic map** (§14; the owner's direction of 2026-10-25, the brief §10, the names and grouping the steward's
    by the owner's leave, *Roles* the owner's). Twenty-one subdomains, each a folder and at the last a namespace `Daoris.Driver.<Name>`, bottom
    to top, each naming only those below it: Platform, Store, Settings, Service, Install, Instance, Events, Knowledge, Agents, Workplace, Roles,
    Participants, Context, Course, Work orders, Review, Plugins, Workflows, Setup, Help and Loop, which holds both loops and no rules. Held by
    folders and a census in `StructureTests` that reads every type reference by Roslyn's semantic model, qualified, aliased and static ones
    included and a member's occurrence no edge, every exception an exact edge listed under its family, every file placed, not assemblies, since
    today's folders form one cycle; the code moved one subdomain per row, bottom up, folders first and every namespace unchanged until the last
    rows, as D166 point 4 orders, no behaviour changed and no Part B contract adopted, each upward edge cut by a port or a moved type in a row of
    its own. Loop keeps the look's executors built from its context, only the rules they hold leaving it, and nothing names a file in Loop's
    folder; the chat runner sits behind a port the course's subdomain declares, the types its executors share, the pending family among them,
    below it (design §14.2, §17, §18).

**Why.** The defects the survey counts come where two mechanisms answer one question; a question answered once, by a pure function
both loops call, cannot be answered twice. Every proposal reached a read over today's stores, because a new store would be a
second truth (D173's first guard), and one decision with many executors, because the disagreement is in the deciding, not the
doing. The work is read because the marks are what every record of this machine carries and what admission keys on, and `actor`
is provenance. The positions of D193's company are set up, not compiled, so what the code fixes is what each capability may do, and a
role composes capabilities and never extends them; an appointment's grant is the owner's, so no edit of configuration can widen it. The
move record is a store row its executor writes under its own process run's claim, which passes only from a dead run, with a mark before each
effect, since a guarded file write fences no spawn or prompt and no role on this machine has one holder to read; the destination never opened
twice is the service's, which moves the words in the same step and keeps its answer, and an old box beside the record would be a second recovery
authority. A word's settlement is the service's because a check in one store and a take in another can interleave. One desktop store gives the
move and its words in transfer one transaction, and gives the
Fabric's records, the role catalogue and the settings one place to be read, set and managed, while the service keeps its own. A trigger
journals for every writer, an older connector included, as the revisions already do, with the snapshot its coding needs, since a diff of
snapshots or a later lookup misses what happens between two of them. The driver's folders form one cycle, so a map with one direction, held
by a census and reached a subdomain at a time, is the one that can land in steps. The account rule is first because one function removes
the class of LIMITSWITCH1, LIMITRESUME1b, LIMITRESUME1c and LIMITGOON1 and builds what the owner decided.

**Rejected** (design §1.1, §9): a stored kind, the kind read from `actor`, and the participant's field named `kind` on a wire that already
has one; tables of issued identities before a subject needs one; a flat rule shape that drops typed requirements; one admission judge across
processes; tables keyed by a fixed list of kinds; hard-coded actors and roles, a role carrying doors, a flat role of both shapes, an added
role's id in `actor`, a role's authority read live, and a role holding an owner's act; the brainstorm's `off` honoured inside an extraction;
one loop for both kinds of line; start-from as a move; every pick waiting, or every pick moving; a manufactured reset; the service owning move
records' persistence; a home file per move with a compare-and-replace claim; old boxes written beside the move record; a move taken over by
its executor role's current holder; recovery of a conversation's move by the lease holder; a word's receipt in the desktop store read by the
client before a say; a narrowing applied only by intersecting the role's configuration at each act; news keyed by a record's `updated`; a
gate reading `using` lines as dependencies; namespaces renamed as files move; an older host's lost open searched for by `from` and moment; a move record for a same-conversation
resume; a receiver judged outside the say door's step; a moved word under the move's key alone; a transient try's words reclassified by a
later limit; minting by diffing snapshots, in the driver's client or in SQL; changes in memory only; a new event frame to the page; an
event-sourced database; one writer of the notices in the lease's loop; news told exactly once; one store for the service and the desktop, a
store per family, `driver.json` written through, credentials in the store, the Node CLI on SQLite; separate assemblies per subdomain now;
*Company*, *Roster*, *Org* and *Team* as the subdomain's or the set's name; Chinese for words never shown; 任命职责, and round 3's 职务 for the
role beside 角色 for the actor.

**This would amend**, on acceptance and each by a dated note in the row that makes it (design §7): D137 point 7 (LIMITSWITCH1's note reaching
a repository conversation and a closed quest's session) and point 4 with its design §2.2 (a closed quest's session whose own account cools
goes on, for that case alone, by the steward's reading of the owner's decision, already a dated note on D137 since LIMITSWITCH2 built it); D205 §3.5(d)
and points 1, 6, 7, 8 and 9 (the eight actors the catalogue's seeded roles read per id; a role's staffing D205's choices; the rules' keys kept
as their shapes in the store; `role_propose` extending `actor_propose`, point 9's *no `settings_*` grant* covering roles) and its rejection of
per-workspace choices (a working role's scope), D206 points 2 and 5 and its rejection of a same-account carry-on (the person's fresh-part press
its one exception), D189 points 2 and 3, D188 points 6 and 7, D167 point 1, D203 points 1, 6 and 10 (conformance; the terminal's field named
`work`), D208 point 1 and D209 points 1 to 4 (the appointed roles configured, an act within the appointment's grant, their bounds standing),
D210's AGENTERRORS1b note (a transient try's words stay uncertain), D173's FABRIC-C2a and FABRIC-CHAIN1 notes and ADR-B (built as a read; the
module map as its code's shape), D199 (the role filter), D155 and D176 (calls at the server), D196 (news told at most once, keyed by its
occurrence, one owner of the notices), D182 (the usage slice woken by every end; held answers by declared inputs), D101 (the kit's
`session/ended` frame gaining `reason` and `class`, its older fields kept), D116 (the glossary's *role*, 角色, in both shapes, *actor* pointing
at it, and the ten strings that say 职责 for an appointed role saying 角色), D172 (the home's
table gaining the desktop store and its wake file), D139 (the store's imports and its stamp-raising migrations timed by the start's
confirmation), D50 (for the configuration the store, not the file, is authoritative, a hand edit taking effect by an import) and D110 (the
configuration's export and import at every door), D198 (the GitDoor reading its families from the store, with the store's package), and D166
points 1 and 4 with D176 point 1 (the fifteen modules replaced by the twenty-one subdomains, D166's order and its rejection of renaming
namespaces as files move kept). Each amendment lands with the part its row is in. Until then each stands as written. D45, D191, D193, D197 and
D201 stand. *Round 5 changes the list* (design §19): D137 and D188 gain a note deciding the settlement's horizon (*never sent again by itself*
with no horizon for an attempt and seven days for a producer's retry, §19.2); D139's note defines *settled* by the install's swap journal
(§19.7); D50 is amended for `driver.json`'s `actors` alone, by row w-s (§19.15); and the amendments of D50 and D110 for the other settings
(z-ii), D198 (the GitDoor's families in the store), and D208 point 1 with D209 points 1 to 4 (the appointed roles configured, rows w-0, t, y-ii
and z-iv) go with the rows round 5 proposes for later designs, so this design makes them only if those rows stay in it.

**Rows:** FOUNDATION1a-i (one account rule over LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2, merged at `a85004bd0` and `db883b1cc`) to FOUNDATION1v (records
last), the role catalogue's 1w-0 to 1y-ii, the store's 1z-i to 1z-xiv and the driver map's 1mod-0 to 1mod-xxi, in the design's §10, §13.6 and
§14.6; Part A's rows are filed in `TASKS.md` when Part A is accepted and Part B's when Part B is, and until then FOUNDATION1's row points to them.

**What the checks do not cover.** A document. The code was read, not run, on main at `92ac2bd7e` and `c8eccf30e` (one `src/`), at

**Note, 2026-10-25 (Part B, round 5, under D191: §16.3's agenda settled; Part B proposed, both makers to read it).** Round 5 is written from main at
`eced2688e`, whose `src/` is round 4's unchanged, and settles each item of Part B's agenda against that code in the design's §19, the body carrying
it (§3, §4, §5.2, §11, §13, §10's Part B rows). What it settles, by point:
- **8:** two executors of one line are exclusive by the move's row, keyed by the record left, two shells on one home among them; a build from before
  the store beside a newer one is the downgrade table's older build at every moment, an empty destination held; a hung executor keeps its moves
  until its process ends and is said `move.quiet` past ten minutes; a shell says once that another runs on its home (§19.1). The keyed open's rows
  go with their opened record by trigger, no time bounding them, and an older host is told by the service's protocol marks before the open
  (§19.5). Where an older build moved the head past a destination, the move is held for the person: `Lines.Forward` is withdrawn (§19.3). A word
  is taken before the move writes `delivered`, so no word waits while its move says delivered (§19.4). A start's confirmation is settled once its
  install's swap journal is at no phase the launcher can still roll back from; the window confirms before its loop, and a headless start and a
  verb cannot run while a swap waits (§19.7).
- **9:** a word's settlement is written by a trigger on the take, so every writer settles, an older connector included, and keeps no disposition;
  *never sent again by itself* holds with no horizon for an attempt and for seven days for a producer's retry, past which a retry is a new word
  (§19.2). Row o's shape is `said` itself, against a host that moves words at its open; against one that does not, the shell holds as today
  (§19.11). The head's walk is indexed, row d-ii landing only with the say door's p95 within a tenth (§19.13).
- **13:** an occurrence's key is the durable id it was minted with, and one path tells it: a record event its journal position, a turn's answer its
  session and the `seq` of its `turn` event, a Confirm its press id; the transition number and its table are withdrawn with the reconciliation
  path's news, a teller below the floor telling none of the gap and saying so (§19.6).
- **5:** should the appointed half stay, a narrowing is computed at each act as the intersection of every revision of the role since the
  appointment's *Appoint*, never written into a live grant, so it reaches every instance and needs no order across a crash (§19.9).
- **17:** each authority-bearing file's class is chosen by its older reader's reading of absence, line by line: the standing confirmation and the
  plugin approvals set aside; `driver.json` (`drivable`, `access`, `standing`; `trees` an ordinary key), the permissions, the configured access,
  the git identities and the plugins' state left in place, their narrowings written through, the backup first and the store after, a crash between
  healed at the come-up with no press (§19.8). The GitDoor opens no store until its start passes row z-i's limit, and no family it reads moves in
  Part B (§19.10). A version matrix gives each service protocol against an older host, an older desktop and an older writer over the migrated
  database (§19.12).
- **15:** rows e, f-i and h land after mod-xiv and its port's cut (§19.14). Round 5 **proposes for later designs**, for both makers' reads, the
  appointed half of the catalogue (rows w-0, t, y-ii and z-iv), since no appointment or grant is offered in this build, and the store's families no
  Part B protocol reads (z-ii's general settings, with D50's amendment for them and the configuration's full export and import, and z-vi to
  z-xiv), which would leave no row of Part B rehoming a grant or a rule (§19.15).
- **16 and 17, the steward's change before both reads:** the actors' staffing stays in Part B, since the owner's direction names the Fabric, the
  actors and the roles as records better read, set and managed in the store. Row **1w-s** ⚖, cut out of z-ii and landing before 1w, imports
  `driver.json`'s `actors` once into `role_staffing` once the start's confirmation is settled, so every role's staffing, seeded or added, has that
  one home; the key is set aside, and an older build reads every seeded role *as before* from `adapter`, `intakeAdapter` and `helperAdapter`, which
  the newer build keeps written through as D205's writer does today, so Intake and Ask Daoris start or not as the store has them; an `actors` key
  found again is imported once, a widening held for the person's press; D50 is amended for `actors` alone; the staffing has its own export and
  import at the terminal and on the AI screen (§19.15).

Nothing of Part B is built. Both makers read round 5 before any of it is accepted, and the ⚖ rows wait for both reads.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (row 1b built, under D191's acceptance of Part A above).** FOUNDATION1b, "a word keeps its identity wherever it is held" (the design's §10, with §17's finding 8 and §18's 1.2), is built, changing nothing a person sees but the repair the Standing names under D137 and D188. The shell's held word (`SessionWords`' `Word`) carries its producer's key, its level, its files and its display choice, written to `held-words.json` additively by name (`key`, `level`, `choice`), so an older shell reads text, files and door as before; a file written before the row reads as a keyless word at the default level. The record path (the held word's try and the pump's) passes the key, and the headless fallback (`LoopWords`) passes the request's key, through one `SessionRequests.WordKey` the three doors now share; `PassAsync`'s forward says the word through the shell's own say with its level, key and display choice as the first say did. The record path passes no `choice` and no level, since the say door refuses a choice from any author but the supervisor and keeps nothing; the display choice is only the shell's, shown on the conversation. A producer's retry after a shell restart, and one said to a successor while its forwarded word waits there, keeps one word: delivers once holds while the word waits on `said`, and after a take it is row p's settlement. The census (`SayKeysCensusTests`) holds every say to the service's door outside the tests to a key or to a named legacy case: a channel message's words (the channel is their identity) and the terminal's verb where no loop drives the home. Proof: `SessionWordsKeptIdentityTests`, `DriverModuleConversationProcessTests`' restart case, `SessionsSayCommandTests`' headless case and the census, each seen failing first; `SessionWordsTerminalTests`, `SessionWordsWaitingTests`, `SessionLedgerTests` and `SessionStoreTests` whole. **Left as designed:** a keyless word (a producer that names none, a file held before the row) is said keyless, as today.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (row 1u built, Part A).** Row u is built as the design's §10 names it, and nothing of Part B. The ten Chinese strings that said 职责
for an appointed role say 角色 (`zh/projects.json` `projects.supervisor.ask.holder`, `.ask.share.legend`, `.ask.share.means`,
`.refused.window-only`, `.refused.unknown-role`, `.refused.choose-terminal`, `.refused.no-holder`, `.refused.revoked-holder`,
`.refused.holder-harness`; `zh/settings.daoris.json` `settings.daoris.steward.ask.share.legend`), their English strings and every key unchanged;
where a terminal is said to be started *for a role* (the supervisor's and the steward's share legends, and the supervisor's choose-terminal
refusal), which could as well be a working role's agent, it says **任命的角色**. The glossary gains *role*, 角色, with 职务, 岗位, 演员, 人设, 执行者 and 职责
avoided, its match `\b(actors?|roles?)\b` covering the *Actor*, *Actors* and *Roles* labels and, under `names:check --all`, the sentences that say *role*
(so a sentence that calls it 职责 is found); *actor* is `use: "role"`, a code and prose word for a working role's staffing, with no name of its own.
Proof: `glossary.test.ts`' *the role* (the entry, the match over the three labels, the pointer, no Chinese string saying 职责, the ten saying 角色) and
`names.test.ts`' hold-the-role case over the real glossary, each seen failing before the strings and the entry changed; `i18n:check` and
`names:check -- --strict` pass with the same nine budget reports as before and the sentence strays under `--all` unchanged. The 职责 that remains
in `docs/2026-10-24-supervisor-design.md` §9's table is that design's dated evidence, left as written. D212 point 14's text stands.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (row 1a-i built, under this decision's note of 2026-10-25 accepting it).** Row **1a-i** is built, changing no answer, no walk and no
counted start. `LineAccount.Decide(own, trigger, constraint, line kind, walk)` answers *Resume*, *Elsewhere*, *Wait(earliest, accounts, set)* or
*Hold(reason, repair)*, pure, in `Accounts/Rotation/LineAccount.cs` beside `ResumeAccount`; `DecideAsync` is the *runs twice* for a caller that cannot
hand its walk lazily, the roster's among them. The walk is asked at the three places the design names and nowhere else: an adapter with no toolchain,
the switch's walk (counted only for a driven open or taken record's carry-on), and the roster's own `ElsewhereAsync` (branches: own account not ready,
the asked model not offered on a ready one, and, by a second run, an own account the by-name ask found signed out); the roster's two own steps beside
it, the maker's reading of a limited record's account and the own account asked by name, stay the roster's, counted as before. The constraint axis
(none, a handed quest's account, a press's pick) is in the rule and read by no caller, no record keeping a pick yet. `LineAccount.Earliest(set, …)` is
the one reader of the earliest reset: the walk's states (`Unready` and the two sentences `RotationWords.Wait` and `WaitOf`), the switch's walk against
the own reset (the roster's switch, the sessions listing's wait sentence), the wake's coolings (`LimitWaits`, through `CoolingsOf`). `Hold`'s repair is
`Unready`'s refusal named (sign in, add an account, offer the model, clear the provider's refusal, change the set, install the agent).
`HarnessRoster.ResumeAsync` takes a `LineKind` where it took `walks`, `switches` and `walkCounts`; its callers (`ChatGoOn`'s switch and Ask Daoris's resume,
the driver's carry-on, limit resume and closed-quest hold, `LimitWaits`) read the rule; `SessionsRoutes` and `SessionsVerbs`, which only hand the
roster's reading of a cool-off to the listing and the verbs, are named *not yet moved* in the census test.
Proof: `LineAccountTests` runs 984 cases (own account × the person's new session and a limit's end × the others × the line's kind, with the no-toolchain
adapter) through the roster's resume and through the rule, seen to agree in the answer, the walks asked and the starts counted, before any caller moved;
`line-account-cases.txt` was written from the roster's resume then and holds each case's answer, its words (hashed), walks and starts through the moves,
which `ResumeAccountTests` (LIMITSWITCH1's and LIMITSWITCH2's cases, their calls only restated in the line's kind), `OwnSpaceTests` and the chat and
driver suites passed unchanged. **Where today's callers disagree, each keeps its answer:** (1) `ResumeChoice.Elsewhere` reads a near own account as
ready where `ResumeAsync` read *not ready* of it, but the roster's judgement never says *near*, so no case differs; the rule reads it as ready. (2) The
sessions listing's `Soonest` reads the cool-offs of the agent's accounts and says nothing where one of them is not cooling, where the roster's switch
walk, with the others signed out or refused, finds none ready and waits for the own account's reset, saying so: the listing shows no hold for that
case and the roster holds the words, as both did before. (3) The wake's set is every cooling account of the agent, one no longer on this machine
included, where the walk's is the accounts a start may take: so the three sets the design names differ, and the listing is a fourth. (4) No account
is outside the set in a roster (POOL1a2), so the rule's *outside* is its own and no roster case reaches it. `ReviewerChoice`'s earliest reset reads the
reviewer's tries and is not this rule's. No user sees a change: the changelog says nothing.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (Part B, round 6, under D191: both makers' reads of round 5 answered; Part B proposed, both makers to read it).** Both makers
read round 5 at `9f48860ba` (`docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md`): Codex, another round, 35 findings, 16 blocking for Part B; the independent
Opus reader, accept after named changes, 15 findings, 4 blocking; both yes to round 5's two cuts. Round 6 is written by Claude Opus 5.5 from main
at `6736d2db9`, whose `src/` is `86f32f40c`'s unchanged, and answers every finding in the design's §20, the stricter taken where the two overlap
and the Opus reader's smallest change where it meets Codex's counterexample; the body carries the contracts (§2.4, §2.7, §3, §4, §5, §7 to §11,
§13, §19). What it changes, by point:
- **8:** a move stands from `decided` until it ends `delivered`, `fell-back` or `closed`, a held move closing at the person's press, and one
  standing move per record left is a partial unique index, so a line moves again once its move ends. The keyed open refuses a record left that is
  active, not its line's head or gone, and the ledger's go-on step refuses a record a keyed open left, so a resume and a keyed open are one or
  the other. Against an older build's own move, which reads its words before its unkeyed open, a word can go twice, once by each build, as between
  two of today's shells: that is the bound, and *once* holds where the service's steps decide the order. A keyed open's row goes with its record
  left and stays `gone` where its opened record is deleted first, so a lost answer asked again after a delete is answered terminally, never opened
  again. The host's protocol marks are kept by the host's run, which every marked answer names, each mark a version (`settled` 1 from row 1p,
  `opens` 1 from 1d-i and 2 from 1d-ii, `head` 1 from 1d-ii, `journal` 1 from 1k). A swap journal that does not read is not settled. `move.quiet`
  is said on the line too.
- **9:** a word's settlement is one row per word id, with the record it last left and its key, read by key over the records the head walk visits,
  so a record with no stored entry and its successors answer one word; trimmed by time alone, so seven days hold exactly. It says only that a word
  left `said`: a take is told by the destination's own event for the word, and a word gone without one is *left*, never delivered again, the move
  held. The head walk's second probe, a driven part's quest, is measured; rows 1p, 1d-i and 1d-ii each land within their limits on `sessions`
  writes and the say door.
- **13:** a turn's answer is keyed by its session, the shell's process run and the moment its turn ended; a record event by the store's identity
  and its position; the journal's family is named only to a reader that asks for it.
- **16:** in Part B the catalogue holds working roles alone, refusing an appointed record before any other judgement; the supervisor and the
  steward stay in code, the shell's, until the design of the appointed half. Points 5 and 16's appointed clauses, point 17's appointments and
  every-setting clauses, and the design's §7 rows 16, 17 and 19 describe rows proposed for later designs (design §19.15), so read with this note.
  Row 1j resolves a start's role over today's actor choices; 1x adopts the catalogue.
- **15 and 17, row 1w-s:** its own judge (`ActorChoices.Judge`) and verbs (`daoris driver actor export` and `import`); each write intent, file
  and commit under `driver.json`'s lock, every crash boundary finished or said, its last projection kept in the store so the come-up tells its own
  unfinished write from another writer's edit; an entry that does not read left in `actors`; Intake's and Ask Daoris's keys absent wherever their
  work is off, removed or unreadable in any scope, so an older build is never wider in whether a work starts, the agent each of the eight runs on
  said as the price; at the return an older build's write offered as an import, never applied by itself but a turn-off, and a role *as before*
  reading its key live, so D50 is amended for the staffing alone. Point 15's ⚖ list gains **1w-s**, and drops 1t, 1w-0, 1y-ii, 1z-ii, 1z-iv, 1z-ix
  and 1z-xi to the later designs that would hold them.
- **Two deferrals, the steward's, for the owner to overrule** (design §8): the brief's §8 line seeding the two appointed roles into configuration,
  and its §9 line putting the appointments and every setting in the store with `driver.json` the export and import alone, each deferred to a later
  design by round 5's cuts; the brief carries dated notes of both.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (row 1c built: the participant's work, read once).** Part A's third row, built as its row names it (design §10; points 1

(`Sessions/ParticipantWork.cs`) and its twin in the driver (`Sessions/Domain/ParticipantWork.cs`, reading the marks as a `WorkMarks` value
declared beside it, so it names neither `PriorSession` nor `SessionView`), the one rule of §2.2 in two runtimes sharing no code and one table,
`fixtures/participant-work.json` among the service's tests, which the service's, the driver's and the page's suites read row for row: each
row's marks, its work, what each reader of the same marks answered before (`today`: the cap's slot, the page's drawing, the connector's partner,
Ask Daoris's repository) and what each reads by the work (`byWork`), every difference named and decided in the table. The service answers
`work` for every record of this machine on loopback, never on the wire and none for a mirror (`session-record-fields.md`'s rows 8, 10, 11 and
12: `SessionResponse` and `ToSession`; `PriorSession` and `ServiceClient.Prior`; the three stand-ins; the page's `Session` type), the sessions
slice's allow-list gains `work` beside `kind`, and `ActiveSessions.HoldsSlot` reads the work's table and answers every case as before. **Census
A** found two disagreements in its table and decides them: the partner drawn as a helper (D203 point 1, the one visible change) and a mirror's
slot (the work's table says none, `HoldsSlot` is told no mirror by its callers and keeps answering as it did, O4-9). **The one visible change** is a
partner drawn as a partner, in `work.identity.partner` (*Partner*, 搭档, D203 point 9), where a host sends its work; a host that sends none draws it
as a helper, as before (the two cases kept apart, X4-1.3), and `isHelper`, which keeps a helper's record to one turn with no box and offers it no
act that takes words, reads `serves` alone and refuses a partner exactly as it did, on either host (X4-1.3, X4-2.1; asserted in `acts.test.ts` and
`WorkFrame.landed.test.tsx`). **Census B** is three counts of every line that tests a mark's presence or the Ask Daoris repository's identity, one
per project's sources (the driver's, the modules', the service's), each file listed in `fixtures/participant-work-readers.json` as *tells* or
*other* with the Part B row that moves it (1e, 1h, 1j, 1q, 1r) or none where no row of the design does; a new line, or a new file, fails until it is
listed, seen failing on a planted reader. Two readings were taken where the design is not explicit: the census counts the lines by their shape and
names each *file* (a count per file, so a changed line in a listed file is not seen, a new one is), and the rail row's *helps …* line, which also
reads `serves` through `isHelper`, is left as it is, since the row draws the partner's *name* and nothing more. Nothing else changed; nothing of
Part B is built. The shared tables' reach is named in `tools/merge-branch.mjs` for the gates that read them.

**Note, 2026-10-25 (row 1mod-0 built: the structure gate, Part A).** Built as the row names it and nothing of Part B: the fourteen sites that read driver
sources by folder path find them by name (`DrivenInboxTests`, `DriverCommandTests`, `InstanceDoorTests`, `GitLaunchTests`, the modules'
`DriverLoopSendsBackTests` and the service's `McpRoleFilterTests` and `McpToolRegistrationTests`; `GitLaunchTests`' census of git starts, which walked
`Trees/`, now reads every driver source by name and lists each file that starts a process), then the gate. No file moved and no product code changed.
`fixtures/structure.json` holds the twenty-one subdomains in §14.2's order, each with its folder (`Work orders` is `WorkOrders/`) and its allowed `Domain/`
(sixteen hold one, where today's `Domain/` files are placed; Platform, Store, Instance, Help and Loop none), **all 784 files placed**, and the census.
`StructureAnalysis` compiles every driver source once over the runtime's base library (`Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` 5.0.0, pinned in the desktop's
`Directory.Packages.props` for the driver's tests alone; the driver, the GitDoor, the launcher and PieceStart take none) and binds each name that can be a
driver type to its symbol: plain, namespace-qualified, `global::`-qualified, through an alias (a namespace's, a type's or a nested type's) or a `using
static` target is an edge, a member named like a type is none, a `record struct` is a struct, a comment, a string and a `cref` name nothing. The compile
and the walk take about 27 s over the tree (the whole `StructureTests` class about 45 s with its one assertion over every diagnostic), the only error the
compiler reports being CS8795 for the 31 generated partial methods. **The census** is 993 edges in 235 files against the order, each a file and the type it
names, in 102 families with the subdomain after which each is cut: the design's named families (the chat runner's port, the types `ChatContext` holds,
`ChatStart` and `ConversationWalk`, the account rule's vocabulary, the service's records, the attention vocabulary, `HelpRoom.Repository`'s `daoris:help`, the
settings' parser's names) and, for every other edge, its pair of subdomains, cut after the lower one. **None is an edge into a file of `Loop/`'s folder.**
The `Domain/` crossings are eight in seven files and nine files with `async`; the rules Loop holds are the eighteen static `bool` methods of the files placed in
Loop (§14.5 rule 4's judgements), five moving with a named subdomain and the other thirteen helpers of an executor, the chat runner's core or the wiring view,
listed as staying until mod-xxi reads them. Today's fifteen test folders are listed as not yet moved. **What the census found that
the design's counts did not:** `DaorisHome`, `HomeIdentity` and `HomeLease` name `AtomicFile` and `Moments` names `Json`, Store's primitives, so §14.1's *names
none* of `DaorisHome` holds for the home's entries and not for its file primitive (the launcher compiles `AtomicFile` beside them); Platform's containment files
(`PseudoConsole`, `AgentStarts`, `PieceStarts`, `PieceJobs`) name types of Agents, Install, Roles, Work orders and Participants (ten edges), which §14.2 had
moved into Platform; the `Domain/` crossings are two beyond the design's six (`PartnerInstruction` names `DaorisHome`, `ReviewSetUps` names `ServiceClient`);
and `Agents/Acp/Acp.cs` is in Agents, below Course, so its names of the chat runner's core stay edges against the order after Course declares the port, until a
row puts the port lower. Each is a census entry with its cut named, not a change to the map. **The proof** was seen failing before the gate held it: the
planted qualified, `global::`, namespace-alias, type-alias and static-import references, the member named like a higher type beside a file that declares one
and names the type, and the `record struct` and `record class` all failed against today's identifier scan, which read none of them or counted the member; and
each rule's cases (place, module, pure, direction, loop, namespace, mirror and the map's own entries) failed with that rule switched off. The gate passes over
the tree with the census listed, and fails on a planted edge of a listed family that the census does not list, on a file `structure.json` does not place, on a
planted edge into `Loop/`, a `Domain/` file naming `ServiceClient`, a planted rule in Loop and a planted global using. D166 point 1's fifteen modules are

**Note, 2026-10-25 (Part B, round 7, under D191: both makers' reads of round 6 answered; Part B proposed, both makers to read it).** Both makers
read round 6 at `5be9557b9` (`docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md`): Codex, another round, 12 findings, 8 blocking for Part B, six of its round-5
findings still open; the independent Opus reader, accept after named changes, 15 findings, 3 blocking. Round 7 is written by Claude Opus 5.5 from
main at `abf03ea26`, which carries Part A's rows 1a-i, 1b, 1c, mod-0 and u, and answers every finding in the design's §21, the stricter taken where
the two overlap and the Opus reader's smallest change where it meets Codex's counterexample; the body carries the contracts (§2.7, §3, §4.5, §5.2,
§7, §10, §11, §13, §19), and the lines Part A's rows moved are re-cited (§21.3). What it changes, by point:
- **8:** the keyed open looks its key up before any other check; every move of a record out of an ended or parked state in the ledger's one step
  refuses a record a keyed open left, the move back to the person among them; a keyed step whose answer was lost is asked again only after the
  host's marks are read again, and a retried open answered without the key's echo is held for the person; a held move closes `resolved` by one
  rule, at the person's press or, read at each come-up and each look, once nothing it names still waits. **A move's delivery is told by its own
  `sent`**, written in the desktop store as its door's write returns and before the take, never by a session event, which the runner records
  before the write: a word gone without `sent` is *left* and the move held, never delivered again. The sites that open and hand words compose
  from the destination's moved words, record each word's own event under its id for the display, and copy its files into the destination's folder.
- **9:** a word's settlement is one row per record it left, word id and key, so equal ids on unrelated records never replace each other's; it is
  read by key over the line's records, back along `from` from row 1p and forward to the head from 1d-ii, each keeping door asking it before any
  refusal but the record's absence, so a retry forwarded to a successor or said again at the head after `Continued` keeps one word.
- **13:** a turn's answer whose moment equals an earlier one's in the same run, two turns ending at one reading of the clock or a clock stepped
  back, is not told; none is told twice.
- **15 and 17, row 1w-s:** every reader of the staffing moves onto the store in the same row, the update drain's Intake-off applied over the
  store's reading and the direct readers of the first-agent keys reading the staffing's first agent; a change of Build's first agent keeps Chat and
  Brainstorm as `Keep` does; a machine role *as before* becomes a row before a scope's off writes its key absent; a come-up finishing the first
  import writes its receipt first. A write is fenced against a newer writer by the store's pending, a claim of its live process run, and against
  an older one by one keeping write that compares the file on its own re-read and reads its lock again before its rename; a pause past
  `KeepLock`'s minute between that read and the rename is the bound every writer of `driver.json` has today, stated. An offer keeps the file it
  observed, the baseline its taking or leaving writes over. **The round-6 note's claim that an older build is never wider in whether a work starts
  is narrowed** to the works a person can turn off: an older build reads no added role, so where an added record does not read it starts a work
  that may not be off on the seeded staffing, as it would had the role never been added; Intake and Ask Daoris are off in it while such a record
  does not read.

  look of the lease holder's loop: a turn-off found there is applied at once, every other change found is kept whole for the person's *Take

  bounded only a newer write paused between its last look at its lock and its rename, but an older save paused after its re-read renames with
  no second look at its lock,
  which is taken as stale by its age, so it can restore a narrowed staffing after a newer commit. Two windows are decided as the downgrade's
  bound and stated: the file wider than the store from such a late older rename to the next check, an older build's start in between acting
  on it and the newer build never; and an older save lost inside a newer write's last instant before its rename. The round-7 note's
