**Note, 2026-10-27 (row 1d-i built: the keyed open and its record, Part B).** `SessionLedger.OpenAsync` and `OpenChatAsync` take `opens`. In the open's exclusive step, `session_opens` answers the key before any other check; a new key requires a local record left that stands, is the line's head and is not active. `Lines.HeadOf` follows local successors across kinds and a driven quest's later local runs, ending a planted cycle unplaced, over `sessions_from` and `sessions_quest`. The receipt keeps the destination and transfer independently of `said`; d-i's transfer is empty and no word moves. An older writer's delete runs the trigger: a destination deleted first leaves a `gone` receipt with empty transfer, and deleting the record left deletes the receipt. Every move out of an ended or parked state in `AdvanceAsync` refuses while a keyed destination stands, including the go-on and the helper's return to the person. The key and its transfer are request and answer fields alone, on no session record or sync wire. The host answers `opens: 1` beside `settled: 1` and its run, with `x-daoris-run` on answers.

`ServiceClient.Opens` is a door over the client's existing `IServiceDoors`, like `ConfirmationDoors`, keeping marks by host run and invalidating them on a changed or missing run. A first open to an unmarked host sends no key and is today's open. A lost or unreadable answer is held with the records `from` the predecessor as candidates, never adopted; its retry reads `GET /api/protocol` again first, sends no second open to an unmarked replacement, and holds an answer without the key's echo even if the replacement followed the protocol read. No move executor adopts the door in this row; their adoption belongs to the later move rows. No initializer seam or census entry was added; new files sit in Service and Sessions, and `map --check` confirms the unchanged 20 project modules and 20 dependencies.

**Proof:** `SessionOpensTests` (17 cases), `SessionOpenDoorsTests` (8) and `SessionOpensHostTests` (6), their new behaviour seen failing with the redirects, replayed transfer, advance guard, protocol invalidation, key negotiation, echo check or run header removed; the unreadable-answer case first failed on the inherited parser. Four admission cases also first failed on the inherited keyed route: a missing quest answered 500, a blank one 409, a blank adapter opened a record, and a missing chat repository answered 409. Required fields are now checked after replay, and a malformed new key answers 400 with its echo and no receipt. The older delete uses its own `DELETE FROM sessions` statement and proves the receipt's `gone` mark and transfer clearance. The helper-return case names a real taken quest, gives the predecessor a tree, ends its destination and then attempts the return, so another refusal cannot mask the guard. The tests hold replay after a planted word was taken and after its planted transfer left `said`, two independent keys, both orders of open versus go-on, active/successor/later-quest heads, deletion and rollback of the whole open if its receipt cannot commit. `SessionLedgerTests` whole and both structure gates pass. Across a crash, the case belongs to **f-i**; moved words belong to **d-ii**, neither claimed here.

**Landing measurement:** `SaidSettledMeasureTests` with `DAORIS_MEASURE_SAID=opens`, two scratch database copies under `_fixtures/`, 5,000 records and 60,000 settlements spread over a week, rollback journals and full syncs, 1,200 alternating pairs. Both copies keep p's settlement; the before copy drops only d-i's table, trigger and two indexes. Pooled `sessions` write p95 **17.51 ms before, 19.23 ms after, ratio 1.098**, within a tenth. The components: state 16.60 / 19.61 ms, keep 17.67 / 17.59 ms, take 18.98 / 19.82 ms; the pooled write is the row's limit. No live install or live home was measured.

**Checks:** service 2,797 and HTTP 345 pass after the admission repair; driver fast 14,927 pass, two fail, of 14,929; its `SessionsSayCommandTests` then passes all 71 in isolation. The two full-run failures were `A_running_session_on_a_next_step_door_reads_it_at_its_next_step` (exit 2, expected 0, line 443) and `Words_it_cannot_go_on_with_are_kept_and_said_with_why` (exit 0, expected 1, line 576); both were read, and no unrelated timing fix was made. The focused service run passes 139, the driver run 64. `SettledServiceProcessTests` with `process.runsettings` passes four cases, the service's real host and stand-in agents. The final `npm run verify` and the three requested CLI files pass (51). The merge tool's file runs all 182, MOD9 included: 181 pass, SESSDEL1c fails because the page reads `Events/Domain/HandOverAskWhy.cs` and the web gate's reach omits it, a mismatch already on this row's starting tree (`5dd81ffc1` added the page's read), untouched here. The complete failure was read; the quest's instruction to report a failure this change did not cause keeps its repair outside this row. The orientation, test-map (1,877 units), code-map, document-shape and duplicate checks pass. FOUNDATION1's backlog row stays unchanged.

**Readings taken:** the quest's “none until d-ii” and §10's row boundary leave executor adoption to the later move rows; `ConfirmationDoors` supplies the client-door pattern, with no new process-wide seam. §19.5's missing-mark retry is held before sending another open, the safe reading where an older replacement cannot replay the key. The landing comparison follows p's paired scratch method rather than reading the live install. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1z-i built: the desktop store, read by nothing, Part B).** Built as the row names it, and nothing more; no family moved into the store,
nothing reads it, and no person sees a change. **The store:** `data/desktop.db`, the home table's `desktop-db` entry (after `knowledge`, new since the layout so its old
place is its new one) in the three twins, equal cell for cell (`home.test.ts`, both `DaorisHomeTests`); `DesktopStore` in `Store/` with `store_version` (the schema's
version and the oldest-writer stamp), `store_revisions`, `store_runs` and `imports`, one connection per process behind a lock, write-ahead logging, `synchronous = FULL`,
a five-second busy wait then `DesktopStoreBusyException` in the process's own words, every write one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that **reads the stamp inside it**
(`DesktopStoreNewerException` where it is past the build's, X3-3.5), records the process's run in its first write, moves the family's revision and, once committed,
touches `data/desktop.db.wake` (nothing watches it yet, `HomeWatch` untouched). Migrations are numbered steps, each in its own exclusive transaction and read again inside it;
version 1 makes the four tables. A step that raises the stamp, and every import (`DesktopStore.Import`, the family's rows and the `imports` receipt in one transaction),
ask the start's confirmation first and write nothing while it is not settled; the store is below the install, so it is handed that reading (`StoreSettled`) and names none
of the install. **The confirmation's *settled*:** `Install/StartConfirmation` reads the install's swap journal, no install or no journal and `confirmed`, `installed`
(the silent swap among them), `rolled-back` and `refused` settled, `swapping`, `started` (a roll-back stopped part way included) and a phase it does not know not, and
**a journal that is there and does not read never settled and says so** (`StagedBuild.ReadJournal` answers null for it as for none, so the file's presence is read
apart, C5-1.7). **The package:** `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` 10.0.12 and `SQLitePCLRaw.bundle_e_sqlite3` 3.0.5 in the desktop tree's `Directory.Packages.props`, the service's
versions, taken by the driver alone (a twins row and `DesktopStoreTests` hold the pins equal and no reference to the service); the recorded *zero package dependencies*
of that file and of `daoris.lanes.json` is amended, the GitDoor and everything else that ships taking none, and the structure gate's compile gains the package's
assembly, its seam list now holding `DesktopStore`. **Proof**, each case seen failing first by breaking the guard it holds (the stamp check inside the write, the imports'
and the raise's confirmation, `started` read as settled, an unreadable journal read as settled, a reader opened read-write, a run never recorded, no wake): the shell, a
headless loop and a verb opening one store at once; a store stamped ahead refused for writes and said, and a writer that opened before another's migration refused when
it resumes; a migration interrupted at each of three steps leaving the store as it was; a start finding `started` or an unreadable journal importing nothing and raising no
stamp; the silent swap importing at its first look; a headless loop beside a swap waiting, then settled; a rollback after a first start meeting every file it acts on
byte for byte and a store it can still write.

**The GitDoor's start, measured (§19.10), and the limit is not met: the door opens no store, as the design already holds.** `GitDoorStartTests` builds the door's own
sources twice in scratch, as today's and with `DesktopStoreReader` and the package, the second opening the store read-only before `Main`, reading its version, revisions
and a table, and times one push the door refuses (no ssh), cold (five fresh layouts) and warm (100 starts, the two programs' starts alternating so a load falls on both),
beside no store, a quiet store, a writer holding the write lock, a writer committing flat out and one checkpointing the log in a loop (it runs only where
`DAORIS_MEASURE_GITDOOR` is set). On this developer machine, **loaded by other sessions' builds, not the install**, wall ms: no store, warm p50 111 → 119, p95 184 → 207
(+13 %); quiet store p50 130 → 177, p95 526 → 541; lock held p50 116 → 165, p95 298 → 396; committing p50 146 → 207, p95 398 → 552; checkpointing p50 109 → 155,
p95 178 → 255. The paired median cost of the open is 45 to 58 ms a start (5 ms with no file, the package and the native library still loaded), p50 +36 to +43 %, p95 +3 %
to +43 % against a noisy baseline; cold medians 906 to 1176 against 897 to 1280, both noise at five layouts. A busy writer or a checkpoint did not make the read wait (the
reader's cases hold that); the cost is the package's load and the open, about two fifths of a door's warm start, three to four times the limit of a tenth. So **no family the door reads moves into
the store**, which no Part B row needs (§19.15), and the door takes no package; a later design that wants one in the store re-runs this class and holds the limit then. **The write
latency `FULL` costs** (`DesktopStoreLatencyTests`, a scratch folder on a developer's disk, not the install): 400 writes alone p50 3.4 ms, p95 5.9 ms, max 61.5 ms; beside a
second writer committing flat out p50 3.4 ms, p95 6.8 ms, max 237.6 ms; the install's own figure waits for its next real run. **Readings taken:** the shell, a headless
loop and a verb are three stores of those kinds opened at once from three threads of one test process, each its own connection, which is what two processes are to SQLite's
locks, the cross-process case being the busy test's second connection; the settled reading lives in `Install/` and reaches the store as a value, since Store is below Install
(§14.3); the imports are a gate and a receipt only, with no family's rows, since none moves; `HomeWatch` is not taught the wake file, nothing reading the store.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1mod-iii built: Settings moved, its cuts landed, Part A).** Built as the row names it, and nothing of Part B: `driver.json` is read exactly as today, never the import §13.5 proposes, and nothing of row w-s's store is touched. **The move:** the five files the map places in Settings (`DriverConfig`, `DriverConfigUnreadableException`, `StandingChoices`, `DriverChoicesCommand` from `Platform/`, and `SessionLanguage` from `Sessions/Domain/`) are in `Settings/`, namespace unchanged, and `structure.json` marks Settings *moved*, with the eight test classes of those files (`ConfigReadTests`, `ConfigSeamTests`, `ConfigTests`, `DriverChoicesCommandTests`, `DriverChoicesTextTests`, `DriverConfigKeepsTests`, `SessionLanguageTests`, `StandingFormTests`) in the tests' `Settings/` folder and listed in the map's `tests`. The GitDoor, the launcher and PieceStart compile none of the five, so no linked include moved; each is built, and the GitDoor's wiring tests (`GitDoorsTests`) run. **The census, 980 edges in 96 families to 881 in 94 (the file-and-type count):** the one family listed after Settings, the settings' parser's and verbs' names of other subdomains' rule types, 86 edges in four files, was regrouped by the owner of the type named into thirteen (`Settings names Service` to `Settings names Help`), so that each cut takes one family off together, and every one of the 86 is off; so are thirteen edges of other families that the same moves took (the review's records off Agents, Context and Participants; a branch's name off Service; the landing rule off Knowledge; the route's two names off Install; `ActorChoice` off Install, which empties `Install names Roles`), and none was added.

**The parser's cuts, each a type moved down to the settings, its one owner, the rule's file answering through it so every other reader keeps its spelling and no answer, sentence or byte of `driver.json` changes** (each new type has a test class of its own in the tests' `Settings/`, holding the new spelling and the old over it): *Service*, `WorkspaceNames`; *Knowledge*, `StandingAnswer`; *Help*, `HelpModeNames`; *Agents*, `CoolOffDefault` and `AcrossDeclarations`; *Course*, `HandOverKey`; *Roles*, `ActorKey` (`WithIntake` and `WithHelper` extension methods of `ActorChoices`); *Participants*, `HelperKey` and `AccessKey` (`WithAccess` an extension method of `AccessRules`); *Workflows*, `WorkflowKey` and `CycleText` (the cycles' four edits extension methods of the workflows' new `CycleChoices`); *Work orders*, `BrainstormKey`, and a quest's *Try again* judgement `StandingChoices.Retry` as `QuestRetry.Decide`; *Review*, `ReviewKey`, `OpinionKey`, `HeavyKey` and `SessionChecksKey` (`WithHeavyGates` and `WithSessionChecks` extension methods of their owners), and a review put's procedure look as `ReviewLooks.Of`; *Workplace* and *Plugins*, `LandingKey` (the form, the rule, what a pattern may say and names, a ticket's key and the sentences that judge them, and the rule's reading and writing), `CommitKey`, `KeepKey` (the keep rules' bounds, which `KeepKinds` and `FloorPolicy` answer through, and the record `KeepChoices`; `WithKeep` an extension method of `KeepRules`, which holds `Settable`), `BranchName` and `PluginIds` (a plugin id's one spelling, which `PluginCatalog.IsId` answers through, `PluginCatalogTests`' one-spelling census naming it now), with `StandingChoices.Landing` as `LandingRules.Put`. After them `DriverConfig` and `StandingChoices` name no type of any subdomain above Settings. The cut carves the key's shape (its record, limits, sentences, reading and writing) down and leaves what a rule *means* (its resolution, edit and words) with its owner, as row mod-ii carved the home's reads out of `Store`, since §14.3 has the settings read keys by their shape; an operation that judged a higher subdomain's rule over the settings' write moves up to it as one method (`QuestRetry.Decide`, `ReviewLooks.Of`, `LandingRules.Put`).

**The verbs' cuts, a port declared and each verb in the subdomain whose acts it drives (§14.2: a verb file belongs there; §14.3: the lower declares the port, the higher implements it, Loop wires it).** The settings declare `ChoiceVerb` (a verb's word, the press it plans, what it says where its words only read, the run of a verb whose words run whole, and the lines it adds to `list`) and `IChoiceTerminal` (the home, the variables, the streams and the press through the instance's door), and keep the words every verb reads its arguments by (`ChoiceArgs`) and the grammar read before any verb is planned (`ChoiceGrammar`, which the lease a change holds still reads). `DriverChoicesCommand` asks `DriverChoicesWorld.Verbs` for any verb it has no case of its own for, and `Loop/DriverChoiceVerbs` wires the set: `HelpersVerb` (Participants), `HandOverVerb` (Course), `ReviewVerb` and `OpinionVerb` (Review), `AdapterVerb`, `HelperVerb`, `IntakeVerb` and `ActorVerb` (Roles), `RetryVerb` and `BrainstormsVerb` (Work orders), `CommitsVerb` and `KeepVerb` (Workplace), and `LandingVerb`, `WorkCycleVerb` and `WorkflowVerb` (Workflows). The instance's world implements `IChoiceTerminal` with the door, and the route's vocabulary, `RouteClass` and `InstanceRoute`, is the settings' (the door reads it from there), so every host and test that handed the command an `InstanceWorld` hands it the same. Each verb keeps its words, its press and its lines of `list`, moved whole; `ChoiceVerbTests` holds the port, the grammar and the terminal. **Readings taken:** (1) the landing verb sits in Workflows, not Workplace: it sets the workplace's rule with the plugin that lands by it and the graph that follows and says the strays the file keeps, so it knows plugins, agents and graphs, all below Workflows, and §14.2's rule that a verb belongs where its acts are driven is read with §14.3's that a subdomain names only those below it; the three families it alone tied together, Workflows, Plugins and Agents, therefore came off in its one commit. (2) What a verb needs that its subdomain sits below is handed to it by Loop rather than named: the second opinion's agents with the plugins beside the home (`OpinionVerb`'s constructor), the planner's verdicts, which the world held as a function over the work orders' own type (`DriverChoiceVerbs.With`, which the terminal's host, the tests and the bridge's tests call, `All` handing a retry no way to read them as the world did). (3) A world that is handed no verbs says of a verb the command has no case for what it said of an unknown one, `is not a standing choice this program changes`; every host and test hands the set. (4) `Changes` stays the settings' static reading of the words, since the lease reads it before any world exists, and the one list of words it shares with a verb, the brainstorms' minutes' flags, is held equal by a test. (5) One commit holds the port and its first verb, the participants', since a port with no verb proves nothing; each family after it is a commit of its own but for the three of (1). **The gate** (`StructureTests`) gained nothing it did not have: its census stands at 881 edges in 94 families, the settings' command and every settings file naming no type above Settings.

**Proof**, as the row states it: `StructureTests` passes with the census at 881 edges in 94 families (the row began at 980 in 96); the GitDoor, the launcher, PieceStart, the host and the modules build, and `GitDoorsTests` pass; the driver's fast half (`Category!=Process`) ran 14,769 tests and the modules' 2,672, the service's Mcp tests 181 and the one Process class this row edited, `DriverWorkflowsLandedProcessTests`, with `git.process.runsettings`, 3, all green but for one test in each of two whole runs of the driver's fast half, a different one each time (`ReadShareAccountTests.A_held_profile_Daoris_wrote_is_rewritten_not_refused`, and `ReviewGraphCensusTests`' `the graph never went quiet`, a wait on real git reads that took 39 s under the suite's load), each green alone with its whole class, 212 tests, and neither reads a file this row changed.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1z-iii built: the bindings, Part B).** Built as the row names it, and nothing more: no other family moved into the store, and no answer a person
sees changes but that an older build after the import carries on. **The family:** `bindings` is store migration 2, a new table with every column required (`session`,
`adapter`, the door the conversation was opened on, `conversation`, `kept`), which leaves the writer stamp at 1, so a build of z-i never opens it and still writes the store
(held by a case); a later row adds its own columns nullable and each writer names its own. **The code:** `BindingStore` in the new `Participants/` folder is the rows' side and
`HarnessConversations` (the map's Participants, `Conversations/GoOn/`) keeps the file's format and every caller, so no census entry is added, `BindingStore` naming nothing of
Course and the other files naming it through `HarnessConversations` alone. **The import:** each look of the loop (`Driver.LookAsync`, which the shell and the headless host both run)
imports every `data/sessions/<s>.harness.json` once the start's confirmation is settled, in the store's own import (the row, the receipt with the file's SHA-256 and class
`set-aside`, the family's revision, one transaction), then moves the file to `<s>.harness.json.imported`, only if it is still the bytes that were read; a file that does not read as a
conversation stays where it is, as evidence; a look with no such file asks nothing of the store, and a home with none makes none. **A home keeps its conversations in the store from
its first import receipt** (`InUse`, read through the read-only reader): until then a keep writes the file and a read reads it, as before, so a build rolled back before the confirmation
meets every file as it was, byte for byte, and no store; after it a keep writes the row (its adapter and conversation by name, its other columns left, never `INSERT OR REPLACE`) and never
the file, and every read reads the binding, so **an older build, which reads the file alone, finds none and carries on** (`ContinueWhy.Unkept`) rather than resuming an id the newer build
has replaced since, `HarnessConversations.Keep` replacing what was kept whenever the wire names the conversation. **A binding file an older build wrote during a downgrade** is the later
evidence: it is imported at the return, at the next look or at the read that meets it first, **its id winning for its own session** over the newer build's row, and set aside again; where the
store will not take it (written by a newer Daoris, busy) the resume reads the file as it stands and the look says why, once. **A deleted record takes its binding**: `SessionHomeFiles.Remove`,
which a delete and a clear both call, takes the row with the record's files, names `harness` among what went, and where the store will not take the delete (a newer stamp, busy) leaves the
row and names it with the store's path among what stayed; the set-aside file is a name of the same inventory entry (`.harness.json.imported`), so it goes too. **The shell's file reader
that no longer finds the file:** `ToolHomeLeftovers`' read of whether a record keeps a conversation to resume (it protects a folder) asks `HarnessConversations.Kept`, which counts a binding;
`tools/family-rehearsal.mjs` reads a session's conversation from the store when no file is there, since its `--once` runs import the files at their looks (the rehearsal is not run in this row; it
is the family run's to prove). **Proof**, each case seen failing first by breaking the guard it holds (a read ignoring the binding, the import leaving the file, a delete leaving the row or the
set-aside file, the older build's file not winning, the look's gate, a keep writing the file after the import or replacing the row, the receipt not read before an import, the look importing
nothing, a home in use without an import, a read not meeting the older build's file or not reading it where the store will not take it, a file that does not read imported, every open drawing
its own run, an optional column, a store that will not take the delete throwing, a file changed since it was read set aside): `BindingsTests` (nineteen cases) and `BindingsLookTests` (two), in
scratch homes of their own, holding the older build's side as the code before the row, and `HarnessConversationsTests`, `DesktopStoreTests`, `DesktopStoreReaderTests`,
`StartConfirmationTests`, `StructureTests` (no census entry added), `SessionDeletionTests`, `HistoryClearingTests` and the Process classes that resume a conversation
(`AnswerContinuesTickTests`, `ChatGoesOnProcessTests`, `LimitWaitsProcessTests`) green. **Readings taken:** (1) the store is opened for each use and closed, with one process-wide run
(`DesktopStoreOptions.Run`, so `store_runs` holds the process once), where §13.4 says one connection per process: `new HarnessConversations(home)` is built ad hoc over any home, in six places of
the driver and in many tests over scratch homes, and a held connection would lock a scratch home's files, which the design's one connection does not meet; the run's kind is `verb` until a composition root names the shell or the
host, which no row needs yet (row f-i's claims are the first to read it); (2) the import is the look's, not a separate come-up, since §13.5 reads the settled state "at each come-up and look until
settled" and the look is the one place both hosts share; (3) a left file whose SHA-256 an import's receipt already holds is only set aside, never imported again, since the same bytes with a receipt
are a crash between the import and its set-aside, whose newer binding the stale id must not replace (an older build writing the same bytes again is not read as a new write: it would have to resume
a conversation it can no longer find); (4) a later set-aside of the same session replaces the earlier `.imported` file, whose id the store's receipt names by SHA-256 and the row has since replaced,
rather than numbering them, which the inventory's one suffix could not take whole; (5) `DesktopStoreReader.Query` takes parameters, an addition the GitDoor's measurement links one file for and
does not mind.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1a-ii built: the account pick's provenance, Part B).** Built as the row names it: a start the person names an account for keeps, on its binding in
the desktop store and nowhere else, that they picked its account, and the account rule reads it as its constraint. **The doors** are the three the row lists, each passing
`picked` to `ChatRunner.StartAsync`: `START_CHAT`'s `profile` (with words through `StartWithAsync`, without through the route), the terminal's `daoris-driver chat --profile`, and the
person's hand-over press with an account (`ChatHandOver`: `AskedBy` the person and an account named, the terminal's `sessions hand-over --account` the same door). An agent's ask, the
curve's move, a limit's move and a hand-over naming no account keep today's reading and record none. The pick is the account the record opened on (`selection.Profile`), written
once the service has opened the record. **The rule's reading:** `HarnessRoster.ResumeAsync` takes `picked` and hands `LineAccount.Decide` `LineConstraint.Pick(account)` where
`HarnessConversations.PickedOn` says the record's account is the one the person picked; the conversation's go-on and the look's wake (`ChatGoOn.SwitchableAsync`, `LimitWaits`) read it
there, and a pick's wait (`Wait` over the walk's set, its own account first) is said in the words the own account's wait has always had, `ResumeChoice.Waits` true for it whatever
the trigger. A record from before the pick was kept, an agent's pick and a mirror hold no pick and walk their agent's accounts as they did. **The store:** `binding_picks` is store
migration 3, a new table with every column required (`session`, `account`, `at`) that leaves the writer stamp at 1, so an older build never opens it, still writes the store and finds
none of the pick: `BindingStore.Pick` and `Picked`, reached through `HarnessConversations`, and a deleted record takes its pick with its binding (`Forget`, `Holding`). Nothing is a field
on the service's record or on any wire. **Proof**, each case seen failing first by breaking the guard it holds (the roster ignoring the pick, the start not recording it, a hand-over
recording an agent's or the curve's account, the page's route not passing it, the wake and each limit move reading none, the delete leaving the row, a read that ignores the account or
takes a mirror's id; the terminal's `chat --profile` passes the flag as the route does and is the one door no test runs, `ChatConsole` having no in-process run): `BindingPicksTests` (eleven cases: a binding of a record from before and of a mirror holds no pick, the wire's keep leaves the pick, a deleted record takes it, a
build from before the row, which knows the store's first two steps, reading and writing its bindings over a picked record and finding no conversation where only a pick is kept),
`ChatHandOverTests` (a named account on a press, an agent's ask, the curve and no account; the runner's start with the flag, with words and without, and one with a named
account that is not the person's), `LineAccountTests`' constraint axis whole (every case of the
grid through the roster's resume with the pick and through the rule with its constraint, the answer the pick's alone and no walk asked, beside an agent's pick and a record from before
walking as they did), `DriverModuleStartWithProcessTests` (the route, with words and without), and `LimitGoesOnProcessTests` and `LimitWaitsProcessTests` (a picked conversation waits at a
limit, at a go-on and at the wake, and goes on in its own conversation at its account's reset). **Readings taken:** (1) the pick sits in its own table of the Bindings family rather than
a nullable column of `bindings`, since the pick is known when a start opens and the conversation's id only when the wire names it, and `bindings`' required columns would then
need an empty conversation, which a build of z-iii, reading every row there as a conversation to resume, would resume; `compatible-builds.md` has such rows in a table those builds cannot
see; (2) a conversation the person picked waits at a limit as well as at a go-on, since §7 row 2 sends *a recorded account pick* to wait and the live limit move walks its agent's
accounts beside the rule (`LimitMove.MoveAsync` and `MoveEndedAsync` read the pick first, the ended move's lost path, which has no conversation to keep, walking as before), and its wait
is said with `chat.limit-waits` over the pick's own reset, no new note code; (3) only a conversation's start is a pick: the quest hand-over press (`QuestHandOver`, a handed quest's named
account first and then its agent's walk, §3.2 step 4) keeps its reading, and a new part another path opens (the briefed move, a limit's move) has no pick, row a-iii's walk reading its own
account; (4) a pick whose account's reading does not offer the asked model resumes there as the rule's constraint says, never walking (the rule's built reading), and an older build's
delete of a binding leaves the pick, which names a record no later session reuses and which the newer build's delete takes; (5) a pick the store will not take (stamped by a newer
Daoris, busy) is lost with a `account.unpicked` warning in the machine log and the conversation walks as a record from before does, which costs the wait and never the start.

The sixteen test classes that mirror the files (`Tool*Tests` and `Tools*Tests`, `HeldFilesTests`, `HeldFilesProcessTests`, `InstallUpdateTests`, `ReleaseChannelTests`, `StagedBuildTests`, `UpdateCommandTests`, `UpdateDrainTests`) moved into the tests' `Install/` beside `StartConfirmationTests` and are listed in `structure.json`, which places every file at its new path with the census's exact edges under them, and marks Install *moved*. **The census, 642 edges in 79 families to 630 in 75 (the file-and-type count):** the four families listed after Install (12 edges) are off whole, and none was added. **The family the row's request names, `Install names Roles`, was already gone:** mod-iii's cut of the actors' key (`ActorKey`, commit `823a8e43b`) took `InstallUpdate`'s `ActorChoice` off the census with the settings' part, so the drain names no type of Roles, as §19.15's seam for row w-s reads it, and the drain's one remaining edge was *Install names Work orders*. and `ToolsCommand` held the instance's `InstanceWorld` and pressed through `InstanceDoor`, so `ToolsWorld.Instance` is an `IToolTerminal`, a port the install declares over the settings' terminal and the folder the program runs from (`BaseDirectory`), which `InstanceWorld` implements, and the verb presses through `PressAsync`, which is the door's call as it was. *Workplace*, in three parts: (1) a start's git, `Tools.Hand` asked `GitWiring` for the prompt variables and the doors: it asks `IChildGit` (`ChildGit.Current`), which the workplace's `WorkingTreeGit` answers with `GitWiring.Hand`, installed when the driver's assembly loads (`Trees/InstallWiring.cs`); (2) `LiveSessions`, the running sessions the delete's reader answers, names only the service's `LiveSession`, so the record is `Service/Domain/LiveSessions.cs`, and its read, which names `SessionProcesses`, is the agents' extension member (`Sessions/LiveSessionReads.cs`) so every caller keeps `LiveSessions.ReadAsync`; (3) the delete of a downloaded version judged and removed through the workplace's `DownloadJudge` and `DownloadClearing`: `ToolInstall.DeleteAsync` keeps its refusals and their words and asks `IToolDownloads` (`ToolDownloads.Current`) to clear the one version, handing the use files' paths and the sessions' reader, and is answered with `ToolVersionCleared` (the outcome, why a kept version is kept, by what), which the workplace's `DownloadClearings` builds from the clearing's own row; the verb's alone path asks the same port for the sessions its markers say run (`RunningAsync`), where it read `LiveSessions.ReadAsync` over the markers itself.

**Readings taken:** (1) the two ports are installed by a module initializer in `Trees/InstallWiring.cs` when the driver's assembly loads, as mod-iv's two were, not handed in by Loop: `Tools.Hand` is called at two dozen sites (every start the source scan holds) and `DeleteAsync` from the host's verb, the bridge's route and the tests, so a site that handed nothing would hand a child no git wiring or clear nothing where it does today; a test that wants another sets it and puts it back (`InstallPortsTests`), and an unset port throws rather than letting a child start without its git variables; the cost is two process-wide seams, which §14.3's wiring by Loop would have made explicit. **Note, 2026-10-26 (PICKCARRY1: a pick belongs to the conversation, so a new part keeps it; the steward's call, amending rows 1a-ii's and 1a-iii's reading that a new part keeps no pick).** Row 1a-ii's reading (3) and row 1a-iii's reading (2) said the new part a
briefed move, a limit's move, a hand-over press or a lost conversation's part opens keeps no pick, so a conversation the person picked an account for walked its agent's accounts again after one move, which
row 1a-iii's reading (2) left to the steward as a later row. The steward's call is that the person's pick is of the conversation, not of one part of it, so it carries to every part a move opens: **the new part's binding
keeps the same pick, and the rule reads it there as on the part it came from.** Built at `ChatRunner`'s start, the one place every move opens its part through (the briefed move and its not-offered path,
both limit moves, the hand-over press and the lost conversation's part all pass the part they continue as `from`): once the record is open, a start that is not itself the person's pick (`picked`) and continues a part whose
binding holds a pick for the account the new record opened on, on the same agent, keeps that pick on the new binding (`HarnessConversations.PickedOn` of the old part, the old record's agent against the new one's). An agent's pick, the
curve's account and a record from before hold no pick, so none is carried; the move itself still opens on the account the rule judged, which for a picked conversation is its picked account or a wait, and a part
that opens elsewhere keeps nothing. Nothing is added to the store, the record or any wire, and `PickedOn` still answers false for a mirror, a record from before and a store that cannot say. **Proof**, each case seen failing first by taking
the carry out: `BriefedMoveTests` (a picked conversation's press and its lost conversation's part each keep the pick, and the new part's next go-on, read as `ChatGoOn.SwitchableAsync` reads it, waits for that account while it
cools with another ready; an unpicked conversation's part keeps none and walks), `LimitWaitsProcessTests` (the ended move's part for a picked, lost conversation keeps the pick and waits; the start the live limit move makes, from its old part on the selection it judged,
keeps the pick on the account the person picked and on no other, and an unpicked conversation's part none) and `ChatHandOverTests` (a hand-over of a picked conversation to another agent keeps no pick though the walk lands on an account of the same name, which fails with the agent check taken out); row 1a-ii's and row 1a-iii's cases
(`BriefedMoveTests`, `ChatHandOverTests`, `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `ChatPendingProcessTests`, `LineAccountTests`) pass as they did. **Readings taken:** (1) the pick is carried only where the part opens on the picked account and its agent, since a pick names an account of an agent and
a part elsewhere is not on it (the rule would read nothing of it): a hand-over to another agent, and a lost conversation whose picked account cools and whose part the walk opens on another, keep none, which is the move's account judged as before; (2) the live limit move
leaves a picked conversation waiting on its pick (row 1a-ii's guard in `LimitMove.MoveAsync`), as does the ended move unless its conversation is proven lost, so the live move opens no part for a picked conversation today and its proof is the start it makes, not the move itself; the carry holds for it if a later row lets
it open one; (3) a pick the new part's store write loses is lost with the `account.unpicked` warning, as in row 1a-ii, costing the wait and never the move.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (the steward: the module-initializer seams of rows mod-iv and mod-v, read against §14.3's wiring by Loop).** Rows mod-iv and mod-v each took a port that a lower subdomain declares and the workplace answers, and installed the answer by a module initializer when the driver's assembly loads (`Trees/ServiceWiring.cs`, `Trees/InstallWiring.cs`), where §14.3 says a port is *wired by Loop*; each named the cost in its reading (1). The steward's call: **the readings stand, and a module initializer is the wiring §14.3 means for a port whose callers are built at sites Loop does not reach** (a client built in a test, a verb, a route or the launcher), since Loop's composition root would have to hand the answer to every such site and a site that handed nothing would silently read or release less than today; Loop's wiring stays the rule for a port its executors alone call. Three bounds keep it honest: (1) a seam is installed only in the folder of the subdomain that answers it, never in the declaring one, so the census sees the edge as downward; (2) an unset port throws rather than answering nothing, and a test that sets another puts it back (`ServicePortsTests`, `InstallPortsTests`); (3) row mod-xxi lists every such initializer in the wiring view beside what Loop composes, so the composition is read in one place though installed in several. A later row that adds a seam of this kind says so in its reading and keeps the three bounds; one that wants Loop's wiring instead takes it with no further call.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1mod-vi built: Instance moved, its cuts landed, Part A).** Built as the row names it, and nothing of Part B: the instance pipe's client, its door, its routes and the verbs' leases only, never a change to how an instance answers, and no table, no import and no store family. **The move:** the nine files `structure.json` places in Instance, all of `Platform/` (`InstanceClient`, `InstanceDoor`, `InstanceRoutes`, `PipeName`, `SentPresses`, `VerbLeases` and `ShellWord`, the design's seven, with `RegisterCommand` and `SyncPass`, the register verb and the sync pass, which press through the door and which mod-0 placed there), are in `Instance/`, each file's namespace unchanged and the folder flat as Settings', Service's and Install's are. **The linked consumers:** the GitDoor, the launcher and PieceStart compile none of the Instance files, and the driver project's embedded resources name none, so no include moved; each of the three is built, and the host with them. The five test classes that mirror the files (`InstanceDoorTests`, `SentPressesTests`, `ShellWordTests`, `VerbLeasesTests`, `RegisterCommandTests`) moved into the tests' `Instance/` with the stand-in and the test home they use (`InstanceStandIn`, `InstanceTestHome`), and are listed in `structure.json`, which places every file at its new path with the census's exact edges under them and marks Instance *moved*; the `*ThroughInstanceTests` stay with the verbs they hold, each its own subdomain's. **The census, 630 edges in 75 families to 617 in 67 (the file-and-type count):** the eight families listed after Instance (12 edges) are off whole, and so is the one edge of `Events names Workplace` that the same cut took (`NoticesRead` naming `TreesSaid`); none was added. No answer, sentence, note code or log line changed, and no byte the pipe carries.

**The cuts, one family per commit**, each a port the instance declares or a type moved down to its one owner: *Agents, Participants, Plugins, Review and Workflows* are one port seen from five owners. `VerbLeases` named the commands that read a verb's own words (`AgentsCommand.Changes`, `.Isolated`, `.Verbs`; `AccessCommand.Read`; `PluginCommand.Changes`, `.Verbs`; `HeavyMappingCommand.Read` and `SessionChecksCommand.Read`; `HeldResolutions` and `NodeWordActs`), which the lease cannot name from below; it now holds a registry of `VerbLeaseReading` (what changes a home, the verb's own second words, what acts on a named file's home alone), asked before its table for any verb a lower file could not spell, and each owner answers its own verbs through `VerbLeases.Answer` from a file beside its command: `AgentsLease` (`agent`), `AccessLease` (`access`), `PluginLease` (`plugin`), `HeavyLease` (`heavy`, `checks`) and `NodeLease` (`workflow`, `work`). Each reading is the arm it replaces, word for word, taking the words after the verb. *Workplace* was two edges: `GitIdentityCommand.Changes` is `GitLease`'s answer for `git`, the same port's sixth owner, and `TreesSaid`, which the door prints a press's answer through, moved down to Instance whole with `FollowedSaid`, which its `Followed` names, while the workplace's `TreesWords` keeps building it. *Roles*: the door read two things of them, whether the process started in a role's terminal and the refusal it says there, so `TerminalRole` (the mark, the code, the sentence and the line) is the instance's; `HolderKey.Take` writes the mark and `HolderKey.Forget` clears it, and `HolderKey.RoleTerminal`, `RoleTerminal.Code`, `.Sentence` and `.Refusal` are spelled as before for every reader, forwarding to it, while the roles keep their list of the verbs such a terminal runs. *Course*: `TreesRoutes` named `PressesTold`'s nine route constants, so the marks (`TreesMarks`, the window's route names each press is the twin of) are the instance's, `TreesRoutes` sends them and `PressesTold`'s constants are them, the same text on the wire; `RecoveryDrop`, which no `trees` press sends, stays `PressesTold`'s.

**Readings taken:** (1) the lease's port is a registry each owner answers from its own folder by a module initializer when the driver's assembly loads, as mod-iv's and mod-v's ports are, not one wiring file handed in by Loop: `VerbLeases.Holds` is read at the host's top (`Program.cs:400`), before any loop is composed, and by the tests, so Loop's wiring would not have been reached; one file naming five subdomains' commands could sit only in Workflows or above, and the families are cut one to a commit, each in its owner. The cost is one more process-wide seam. A verb nobody answers is *unclassed*, which the leases already say holds the lease, never silently a read: `InstancePortsTests` holds the answered set (eight verbs) and each reading, so a missing answer fails there. (2) The `work` verb's words (`recovery`, `held` with a resolution) read only `HeldResolutions`, the workflows', though the command is the work orders', so Workflows answers it, the lowest subdomain that can name what it reads; no case of `VerbLeasesTests` held that arm, which `InstancePortsTests` now does. (3) `TreesSaid` moved rather than being ported because the door is its one reader and printer and the workplace its builder: a port would have handed back a record the door already shapes; the four files that build it keep their spelling. (4) The role-terminal mark stays a process-wide static, as it was the roles' field, and its test never sets it, which every door running beside would read. (5) `RegisterCommand` and `SyncPass` are placed in Instance by mod-0's map, not by the design's list of seven; they are moved as the map has them. (6) The four lease files sit in folders of subdomains not yet moved (`Sessions/`, `Sessions/Heavy/`, `Trees/Nodes/`, `Trees/`), so `structure.json` places each by entry, as `Trees/InstallWiring.cs` is placed; the move of each subdomain's row carries them.

**Proof**, as the row states it: `StructureTests` passes with the census at 617 edges in 67 families (the row began at 630 in 75), every file placed (873), none after Instance; the GitDoor, the launcher, PieceStart, the host, the modules and their tests build, and `GitDoorsTests` (72, which compile no Instance file) and `GitDoorsProcessTests` (10, with `process.runsettings`) pass; the driver's fast half (`Category!=Process`) ran 14,883 tests, the modules' 2,672 and the service's `Mcp` classes with `ParticipantWorkReadersTests` 183, all green on the second whole run of the driver's; no class of the Instance folder is a Process one. `InstancePortsTests` holds the eight answered verbs and each reading, `TerminalRole`'s text and its forwarding, and `TreesMarks`' nine texts against `PressesTold` and the routes the verbs send. `npm run verify` passes, and in `src/Daoris.Cli` `node --test test/doc-duplicates.test.ts test/dogfood.test.ts test/platform/home-census.test.ts` (51) and `node --test tool-test/merge-branch.test.ts` (182, its MOD9 census among them) pass. The driver's fast half failed one line in its first whole run, `ReviewGraphCensusTests.Every_combination_of_the_range_starts_each_node_at_its_depth_and_answers_as_today` for `line: "main", record: "displaced", bases: "same"` (`G1 started in round 4, its depth 2`), a graph's rounds counted inside the whole suite's load, in a class naming no file this row moved or changed; the class passes alone, twice (189 each), and the second whole run passed all 14,883.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (PICKLOST1: a lost conversation whose account the person picked waits for it; amending PICKCARRY1's reading (1) for a lost conversation, and row 1a-ii's reading that the ended move's lost path walks).** Row 1a-ii's guard in the ended limit move (`LimitMove.MoveEndedAsync`) read *a conversation proven lost has no resume to keep*, so a lost conversation whose picked account cooled walked its agent's accounts, and PICKCARRY1's reading (1) took that as given (*a lost conversation whose picked account cools and whose part the walk opens on another keeps none*). That conflated the resume with the account: being lost means no resume is kept, not that the person's pick is gone, and row 1a-ii's rule is that a recorded pick waits. **Built:** the ended move reads the pick first for a lost conversation too; where its account is the person's pick the move asks the roster for that account alone (`SelectAsync` with it as the choice, the account rule's pick constraint) rather than walking, so the new part waits while the account cools (the move answers `LimitMove.OnPick`, never `ElsewhereAsync`, since a pick is never moved to another agent either) and, once it is ready, opens on it afresh, no resume kept, and keeps the pick (PICKCARRY1's carry, unchanged). A lost conversation with no pick, with an agent's pick or on the curve's account walks as before. The lost go-on (`ChatGoOn.LostAsync`) reads `OnPick` and waits, its words kept and unmarked, instead of falling through to the brief's move, which would refuse the same cooling account and mark the words as unable to go on; the limits wake already waits for a picked account's reset and goes on there. **Proof**, each case seen failing first by taking its guard out: `LimitWaitsProcessTests.A_lost_conversation_whose_account_cools_waits_for_it_when_picked_and_walks_when_not` (picked: it waits with another account ready, nothing opened, counted or marked, then at its account's reset opens on it afresh with the pick kept; not picked: it walks to the ready account and keeps none); the cases of PICKCARRY1, rows 1a-ii and 1a-iii (`BriefedMoveTests`, `ChatHandOverTests`, `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `ChatPendingProcessTests`, `LineAccountTests`) pass as they did. **Reading taken:** the ended move's lost path for a conversation the person picked is held to the picked account whether or not it is ready, so a lost, picked conversation whose account is ready and not the walk's first opens on the picked one, where it walked before.

**Note, 2026-10-26 (row 1p built: attempts acknowledged, dispositions and settlement, Part B).** Built as the row names it, and nothing of rows d-i or d-ii: no keyed open, no head, no word moved, so the head walk's forward records and the moved-word cases are d-ii's. **The service:** `said_settled`, one row per record left, word id and producer key, no disposition, written by the trigger `said_settled_left` (`AFTER UPDATE OF said`, guarded `OLD.said IS NOT NEW.said AND NEW.origin IS NULL`, for a list that is an array of objects on both sides) in the statement that takes the word, so every writer settles it, a connector built before the table included (the take, a state move's `clearSaid`, a take-back, the park's clear), a keep and an attempt settling none; a word with no key settles as `id:<word id>`; the trigger trims by time alone in its own statement, seven days, over `said_settled_at`, no count cap. `SaidKeys.Settled` reads it by key over the line's records, **the record the say names and its predecessors back along `from`**, one probe by id each, a record already walked or a predecessor gone ending the walk, inside the say door's, the helper desk's and the partner desk's own exclusive step, each asking it **before any refusal but the record's absence** (the say door before `Never`, `running`; the helper desk before its bound's refusal and `NotHanded`, over the record its hand reaches), and answers **`Settled`**, its own terminal kind, a success `{ message, settled: <record the word left> }` with no `said`, no `session` and nothing kept, **only to a say or hand that sends `settled: true`**; a request without it, an older client's, is answered by today's door, its refusals first and its key read on the record named alone, so it keeps a second word as today. `Again` stays for a word waiting on the record named, and a key whose word waits on a record the line has left (the shell's pump forwarding after a lost answer, the helper's hand after its asker went on) is kept at the record named as today and never `Settled` (*O8-F1*). **The supervisor's door is left as today**: its say sends no `settled` and reads no settlement. `GET /api/protocol` answers `{ run, marks: { settled: 1 } }`, the run an id the host draws at its start, classed Open in the door table. **The client:** `ServiceClient.SayAsync` sends `settled` on every keyed say but the supervisor's release, the helper's and partner's doors on every hand, and reads `settled` before `said`, as `SayAnswer.Settled` (kept, no word), `HelperHanded.Settled` and the partner hand's fifth member. **Every reader of a keyed answer handles `Settled` before a word, the census naming each** (`SettledReadersCensusTests`, beside `SayKeysCensusTests`): `ChatTurns.KeepAsync` and `DrivenInbox.KeepAsync` answer it as a retry of a word they hold already, holding, queueing, steering, attempting and taking nothing; the partner's hand marks the piece handed with no note and no event; every other say caller (Daoris's limit and restart words, the reopen and go-on words, the landing's and the transient go-on) reads it as kept, never dereferencing a word. **Attempts acknowledged** (§3.4 rule 5): the attempt's answer names each word it marked (`AttemptAnswer`: the words asked less its `unmarked`, none where the record refused, had no door, did not answer or lost its answer), `ISaidDoor.AttemptAsync` and `ChatMessage.Attempting` carry the ids marked, and **a word is written only once the record names it**: the conversation's turn (`ChatTurns`' pump), the word steered or sent mid-turn or held for the turn's end (`AcpSession.Attempted`, thrown `AcpWordsUnmarked` before the wire and said, never the session's work), the resumed run's first prompt and the native go-on's batch, which go with **only the words named**, so a word the person took back in the moment the go-on started no longer goes (`ResumeAsk.Narrow`, in place, so the door that sends it and the dispatcher that takes its words off read the same words); an unnamed word is left waiting on its record, untaken, and said once by `words.unreached` with the new reason `unmarked` (en and zh in `note.json`, held to the driver's declaration), never sent by anything but the next go-on. **Proof**, each case seen failing first by breaking the guard it holds (the trigger, the order before `Never` and `running`, the walk back, the horizon in the reader and in the trim, each desk, a waiting word's precedence, each reader's handling, the client's request and its read, each acknowledgement): `SaidSettledTests` (20: a take settles by record, word and key, a keep and an attempt none, the clear and the take-back, the legacy `answer` column, a list that is no array, an older connector's own take statement over the migrated database; the older client's retry a second word under a new id, the successor's untouched; a helper's and a partner's hand after the take, nothing kept; the pump's schedule, K waiting on A forwarded to B, kept as a new word and delivered once, A's copy still on A**) and `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests`' settled retry through a real conversation; `compatible-builds.md`'s own-statements case is the older connector's take. **Landing limits, measured on a scratch service** (`SaidSettledMeasureTests`, held to `DAORIS_MEASURE_SAID`, a database file under `_fixtures/`, the service's own default of a rollback journal and a sync at each commit, 5,000 records and **60,000 settlements spread over a week**, never the install and never its home). **Unpaired**, before then after, three runs each on a developer's disk loaded by other sessions: pooled say door p95 8.33, 8.25, 14.61 ms before and 5.38, 5.87, 5.38 after; pooled `sessions` write p95 8.60, 21.84, 15.63 before and 6.16, 6.07, 6.75 after, the machine's own load moving a figure by half between runs, which a tenth cannot stand beside. **Paired**, the measure the limit is judged by: the same records and words on two copies of one seeded file, the first with the trigger dropped and the door asked as an older client asks, alternated call by call, 1,200 pairs a run, three runs (the first before the table was made `WITHOUT ROWID`, which cut the take's cost from +10.8 % to +7.1 % and +4.3 %): a new word under a new key p95 +2.5 %, +2.5 % and +0.1 %; the same key while its word waits −1.0 %, −1.5 %, −0.8 %; the retry after the take p95 0.35 ms against 6.4 (−94 %, answered from the settlement without a keep); **say door pooled p95 0.95 of before** in each run; a state move +0.8 %, +0.7 %, −0.2 %; a word kept +0.1 %, +0.2 %, +2.9 %; a word taken, the write the trigger fires on, **+7.1 % and +4.3 %** (p50 +4.6 %); **the `sessions` write pooled p95 1.020 and 1.023 of before**, so both are within a tenth and the row lands. **The take rate and the table's size at a week:** the install's own rate is the install's to read and was not read (never the live install); on the scratch the scale the row names, 60,000 takes in a week, about six a minute, makes the table **6,156 KiB, 105 bytes a row, with its moment's index** (the database 7,496 KiB against 1,340 KiB without it), and a week's takes is all it ever holds, since the trigger trims by time alone; a week far past that rate makes the table larger and never wrong. **Readings taken:** (1) the settlement is asked first except where a word under the key waits on the record named, which is answered `Again` with that word as today, since the design reads `Settled` only for a key `said_settled` holds over the walk's records and `Again` for a word waiting on the record named; (2) the moment is whole seconds (`strftime('%s')`, which every build of SQLite reads, so an older connector's statement never fails on a function it lacks), the horizon read from the say's own `now`; (3) the table is `WITHOUT ROWID`, its primary key `(key, session, word)`, so the table's own order is the design's `said_settled_key` (the key and the record), the unique identity is the same three columns, and a take writes two trees, not four; (4) a say to the supervisor's door and a record's `answer` kept before `said` are as the design says (an answer in the old column settles nothing when taken); (5) **the all-or-none cases of the acknowledgement**: a single word goes or waits, and a go-on carries the words named; the one write that carries a native run's batch is filtered per word before the run starts; where the record names no word of a driven session's resumed first prompt the run fails before the wire with `AcpWordsUnmarked`'s sentence and its words wait on their record for the next look, since a resume nothing can be prompted in has no turn to say it by; (6) an unacknowledged word is said with `words.unreached`'s new reason, the page's catalogue sentence for it saying *while it worked never reached it* for every reason, a wording that fits a word left behind a failed mark too and that a new code would only repeat; (7) `GET /api/protocol` is read by nothing in the client: the marks' keeping by the host's run and the header every marked answer names are row d-i's, `settled` needing neither since an older host ignores the request's field; (8) **the pump's schedule of *O8-F1* is proven at the service door and at the dispatcher** (a word waiting on a record the line left forwarded to its successor, kept there and delivered once), the briefed move's and the live limit move's own reads of A's waiting words being today's code, unchanged by this row; (9) the crash before and after the wire, a limit's refusal carrying its words once, and a transient try's words staying uncertain are the cases of COWORK1c's `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests`, LIMITWORD1's `LimitWaitsProcessTests` and AGENTERRORS1's `AcpTransientTests`, run green beside this row's. **What the checks do not cover:** the install's own take rate and latency, and a real agent receiving a settled retry; the moved-word cases, d-ii's.

**GRAPHQUIET1 parent source-review follow-up, 2026-10-27.** This finding is source review, not a reported new queue result. The committed `6959cb117` helper was re-read: it already captured outstanding ReviewNode.Answered tasks before readiness observation and included them with arrivals/read completion in its wait set. Its completion-only regression is strengthened to register an independent next-round child after the waiter has begun; a positive second waiting handshake proves that arrival woke the helper while the acknowledged terminal leaf was still unfinished. The leaf's answer must then wake the ready frontier with no further arrival or whole-read completion. Production graph and scheduler remain unchanged.

The one-held-git-per-node assumption is now checked during every actual census run, not inferred from total starts alone. Before holding a git, the stand-in requires exactly one live, not-yet-called owner matching the command classifier and current budget, normalizing only K′/K and the N arrow/count labels. It never chooses among duplicates by creation/start order. On completion, every graph node must have exactly one recorded call with its own budget. The census Worlds exercise Review and Untracked; they create no landing and their scripted counts are zero, so Landed and Offer operations are not claimed covered. Repeated N reads lie in different frontiers and U uses w=1; this guard deliberately fails rather than guessing if that assumption changes. Negative cases reject missing and second calls.

The source walk agrees with that bound: under this stand-in TreeContext.GuessesApart selects separate G1–G3 nodes, not the possibly compound G wrapper; W/D each use one RangeAsync stream, S one status stream, and these Worlds' U paths do not take the oversized/unreadable-file shortcut. The remaining exercised nodes each issue one whole git read. The separate w=4 UntrackedWindow stand-in case does not claim the graph census's w=1 ownership accounting. These are current fixture conditions, not a new constraint on production.

The confirmed additional gap was failure cleanup: CensusAsync previously restored WorkingTree.Runs without releasing/settling outstanding held work when an assertion failed. Its finally now releases current and future registrations, awaits the actual read and graph settlement with shared patience, then restores the stand-in. Any cleanup fault is retained alongside the primary failure. Teardown freely answers the existing script and stops ownership measurement; it does not turn the failed assertion into a pass. A deterministic unexpected-topology failure proves its held parent and a child registered during teardown both settle. The first four-class focused follow-up passed **257/257, zero skipped**, including all original 189 census cases plus 18 new cases (207 census, 33 GitLaunch, 9 base choices, 8 review command), retained as `local/scratch/graphquiet1/frontier-accounting-teardown.trx`. No Process/whole suite, parent record, TASKS, production source or quest state changed. Parent retains full integration and GRAPHQUIET1 backlog ownership.

- **Lower homes, the higher names answering through them** (`HistoryClearing`, `HistoryHome`, `SessionHomeFiles`, five tuples): where a run's files lie and how its id reads (the runs and looks folders, the opinion gates' folder, the chat key and its record, `Bound`) are Workplace's `WorkplaceRunFiles`, since the history, its clearing and a session's inventory are the lowest readers of them (row 10); `WorkflowRunBindings` (`Folder`, `FolderOf`, `IsRunId`, `PathOf`) and `ChatRuns` (`Prefix`, `IsChatRun`, `RecordOf`, `GatesFolder`, `LooksFolder`) alias them, so every other reader keeps its spelling and no path or sentence moves.
- **A facet the owner implements** (`AutoLander`, `EndJudgement`, `EntryLands`, `SessionTrees`, `TreeLands`, and the files' use of `WorkflowProcess`): `IWorkplaceProcess` carries what Workplace reads of a process (`Named`, `Name`, `Landing`, `Problem`, `Unread`, `LandingCannot`, `Record`, `BoundRun`, `Decided`, `LandingSays`, `Opinion`, `OpinionCannot`), over types Knowledge already holds; `WorkflowProcess` implements it, the three new members being `Binding?.Run`, `WorkflowRunBinding.Why` and `WorkflowGate.LandingSays`. Every signature that took the record takes the facet, so no caller changes.
- **A port and a required holder** (`IWorkplaceWorkflows`, installed from `Workflows/WorkplaceWorkflowsWiring.cs`, resolved only where reached, failures propagating unchanged): `AcceptedGates` and `DeclarationTextAsync` (`GeneratedWriters`, two tuples: the landed route's accepted blob and its text; the declaration's file name is `GeneratedWriters.DeclarationFile`, which `LandedGates.File` aliases), `ReadProcessAsync` (`AutoLander`, a quest's process read from the quests a delegate answers, so the port names no Review type; `WorkflowProcesses.ReadAsync` gained the delegate overload its world overload calls) and `BindChatFirst` and `ChatPressOf` (`ChatLandCommand`, two tuples: the catalogue is loaded, then the clock read, as before, and the plugins are read from the catalogue inside the operation).
- **The gate's assembly is the workflows'** (`TreeGates`, eleven tuples, and `SessionTrees`'s two): the landing gate reads the run's process, judges its workflow part, hands that one process to the review and the second opinion, then judges the graph's part, and the review's reader and `LandingGate` take `WorkflowProcess` and `WorkflowGateState`, so the composition can sit only where both are named. `IWorkplaceGateWorkflows` (declared in `TreeGates.cs`, where the Review types it carries are already named, so no tuple of the Review family moves) takes the gate and the process reads; `Workflows/TreeGateWorkflows.cs` holds the moved bodies (`GateAsync`, `GraphAsync`, `ProcessAsync`, `WorkflowAsync`) in their original order, and `TreeGates` keeps the second opinion, the paths a chain changed, the fork point and a one-line forward each. `trees.WorkflowAsync` is Workflows' extension of `SessionTrees` (its only callers are the run's view), and the wiring converts the facet back to `WorkflowProcess` at the one place it is installed, since `WorkflowProcess` is its only implementation. The Review cut follows in the same file: what it finds there is the gate port's `LandingGate`, `IOpinionWorld` and `IReviewWorld`, no workflow type.
- **The trap, which would have been a tuple the base had and the cut removed:** `AutoLander.ProcessAsync` named `IReviewWorld`, a Review tuple of `AutoLander.cs`; moving it whole to Workflows made that tuple vanish, so it stays Workplace's owner resolution (`RunOwner`) over the port, and the loop's one caller that hands the result on to `OpinionLook` reads the concrete record through `WorkflowProcesses.ConcludedAsync`. `TreeLands` reads `Decided` and `LandingSays` of the process in place of `WorkflowRunBinding.Why` and `WorkflowGate.LandingSays`.

**Note, 2026-10-28 (row 1mod-xi: Roles' cuts built, on the move commit `414c64c5c`).** The thirteen "Roles names ..." tuples are gone: **260 to 247 tuples, 30 to 27 families**, Workplace untouched. The exact comparison against the move commit's fixture removes only the three families (Roles names Context 2, Review 2, Work orders 9) and adds no tuple; every other family, the `pure` rows, Loop rules, seams and subdomains are equal, and the fixture gains one owner entry (`Sessions/Work/RolesWorkOrdersWiring.cs`) and one test mirror (`RolesBoundaryTests.cs`). No behaviour, namespace, stored or wire shape, proof, key, decision memory or persistence body changes; `SupervisorProof`, `SupervisorDecision`, `StewardCommand`, `RoleActForm`, `RolesBrief`, `ActorChoices`, `ActorVerbs` and `BuildWalk` are byte-identical to the move commit. Each cut, with the line of the design's §14.2 table (row 11 puts the thirteen in Roles and row 15 `Quests/`, `Questions/QuestionFiles` and `Sessions/SessionsCommand` in Work orders) that decided it:

- `ActorRun` and `OneTurnReask` name `SessionEvents` (two tuples): `ActorRun` takes Events' `ISessionEvents` (it only subscribes to `Evented`), and `OneTurnReask.Say` passes it to `SayActor`, which already took it. The record's engine stays Context's.
- `OneTurnReask` names `ReviewerChoice` and `ReviewerPassed` (two tuples): the re-ask is Roles' rule (row 11), the choice is Review's. It is `OneTurnReask<TChoice, TPassed>` over Roles' `IActorChoice<TChoice>` (chosen, effective entry, selection, what was tried, and `WithSelection` in place of the `with`), `IActorTried` and `IActorPassed<TPassed>` (`Roles/ActorChoice.cs`), which `ReviewerChoice`, `ReviewerTried` and `ReviewerPassed` implement; Review's `Review/ReviewerReask.cs` declares `OneTurnReask : OneTurnReask<ReviewerChoice, ReviewerPassed>` so the three owners' calls and the tests keep their name. Moving `ReviewerPassed` into Roles was the alternative (it only holds an `ActorEffective`) and was rejected: it would also have emptied three Participants-names-Review tuples (`HelperPass`, `HelperChoice`, `PartnerChoice`), and this cut removes exactly Roles' thirteen.
- `SupervisorQuarantine` names `Snapshot` (one): `DecideAsync` and `LookAsync` are generic over Roles' `ISupervisorLook<TLook>` (the two record dictionaries it reads, and `WithSupervisor` / `WithRecords` where it wrote with `with`), which the planner's `Snapshot` implements by those same `with` expressions. Callers and tests pass a `Snapshot` and get one back; the bodies are otherwise unchanged.
- `QuestionSupervisorDecisions` and `SupervisorDecision` name `ParkQuestion`, and the first names `QuestionFiles` (three): `ParkQuestion` and `ParkQuestionChoice` move byte-identical to Roles (`Roles/ParkQuestion.cs`), the lowest subdomain that reads them, with the exact body of `QuestionFiles.Parsed` as `ParkQuestions.Parsed` and the writer's six bounds beside it; `QuestionFiles` keeps the file, aliases the bounds as constants and delegates `Parsed`. The decisions' memory calls `ParkQuestions.Parsed` where it called `QuestionFiles.Parsed`: the same parse, no change to what it keeps or checks.
- `SessionsSuperviseCommand` names `SessionsAsk`, `SessionsWorld`, `QuestionFiles`, `AskWords` and `AskWordsText` (five): the verb reads its world and ask through `ISupervisorWorld` and `ISupervisorAsk`, which `SessionsWorld` and `SessionsAsk` implement without change to their members, and `Read` takes the factory `SessionsCommand` hands it to build its ask. The question file and the ask's words come from the required `IRolesWorkOrders` holder, installed by Work orders from `Sessions/Work/RolesWorkOrdersWiring.cs`: `QuestionOf` is `new QuestionFiles(home).Read(session)`, and `AskWordsAsync` reads the ask where the print read it, with the go-aheads' lines (`unread` for none) and the words beneath the quest moved into it unchanged, in the same order (read, then the question and its parts printed).

Where the orientation's map and the code differed, the code and §14.2 decided; the cuts, with the table's line for each (row 10 keeps a session's tree, a landing and its hold in Workplace; row 16 gives Review `Review/`, `Sessions/Heavy/`, an opinion's gate and a check's verdict):

- **`LandingGate` and `LandingGateWords` stay Review's, read through a facet.** `LandingGate` holds the workflows' part and the graph's as well as the opinion's and the review's, so it cannot move down; `IWorkplaceGate` (`Workplace/WorkplaceReview.cs`) carries what the landing reads of it (the tip it was judged at and `WithTip`, the first part that holds as a landing's refusal, the process, the `LandingReview` and `LandingOpinion` kept on the record, whether the graph's part asks a pass, and its plan and log lines, which `LandingGateWords` answers), and `LandingGate` implements it. `TreeLands`, `EntryLands`, `TreesPlanWords` and `AutoLander` take it where they took the record. `IWorkplaceGateWorkflows.GateAsync` now returns it over `IReviewWorld`; Workflows' wiring converts the world back to `IOpinionWorld`, which every world a door or the look hands the gate is, and the gate to the facet.
- **Review's parts of the trees' gate are extensions of `SessionTrees` in Review** (`Review/SessionTreeGates.cs`: `GateAsync`, `ReviewAsync`, `OpinionAsync`), so every caller keeps its spelling and gets the `LandingGate` record; the second opinion's part (`OpinionAsync`, its chat-run variant) moves byte-identical to `Review/TreeGateOpinions.cs` over `TreeGates`' context, and `SessionTrees.GateOfAsync` is the workplace's own read of the facet. An extension is no census edge, so these sit in the subdomain that may name what they call: Review reaches the gate only through the Workplace port.
- **A world facet and a type moved down:** `IReviewWorld` (the service's quests and asks, over `QuestView` and `AskView` alone) is Workplace's, the lowest reader; `IOpinionWorld` (the opinions) moves to Review beside `ServiceReviewWorld`. The look reads `IWorkplaceLandingWorld`; Review's `IAutoLandingWorld : IWorkplaceLandingWorld, IOpinionWorld` and `ServiceAutoLandingWorld` carry the opinions, so the seven test stand-ins that implement `IAutoLandingWorld` compile unchanged. Its `Again()` is declared again on the derived interface and the base's is overridden explicitly, so a stand-in's own `Again` is the one the look calls.
- **A port:** `IWorkplaceReview.OweLanding` (`AutoLander.Owe`, four tuples), installed from `Review/WorkplaceReviewWiring.cs`, resolved only where reached; its body is the original's with the clock read only where a due is made, and its failure handling unchanged.
- **Files that move whole to Review** (`ChatPresses`, `ChatLandCommand`, `EntryAccepts`, sixteen tuples): each is the person's press over the gate and the opinion, names nothing above Review, and is named below Review only by `SessionTrees`' use of the entry's lead, which is now `EntryLandings.LeadOf` (the body of `EntryAccepts.EntryLead`, which answers through it). `ReviewServed.ReviewingAsync` (the folders a review step shows from) leaves `BranchTidying`, whose tidy already takes it as a delegate; its two callers and the three test calls changed their spelling.
- **Words both spell, the lower home owning them** (`SessionHomeFiles.ChecksSuffix`, `PassLeftovers.OpinionCopyPrefix`, `CommitRules.RepositorySource` and `WorkspaceSource`, `LandingDoors`, `GeneratedWriters.SystemShell`): `SessionChecks.Suffix`, `OpinionTree.Prefix`, `OpinionSource`, `ReviewDoors` and `HeavyStep.SystemShell` alias or answer through them, so every other reader of the review's names, `HelperRules` and `HandOverRules` among them, is unchanged and no other tuple moved.

- **Help (9).** `HelpRoom.Repository` is `KnowledgeRoomIdentity.Repository`, which it already aliased, and `HelpRoom.GoesOnIn` is Knowledge's `KnowledgeRoomPaths.GoesOnIn` (body and its `Same` unchanged; `HelpRoom.GoesOnIn` delegates). `HelpConversations` (two tuples) moves whole to `Context/`, not to Knowledge where §14.2 row 20 puts it: it names `SessionHomeFiles` (Workplace), `SessionEvents` (Context) and `HarnessConversations` (Participants), so Knowledge and Participants each failed the new test on exactly those edges; Context is the lowest subdomain that holds what it names, and it is below Course. - **Review (1).** `HandOverRules` read `OpinionSource.Repository` and `Workspace`, which alias Workplace's `CommitRules.RepositorySource` and `WorkspaceSource`; it reads those.
- **Work orders (8).** `ChannelAcks` moves to `Course/Domain/ChannelAcks.cs` (the driven inbox is its lowest reader; the loops and the work orders' channel words name it downward). The claim's words, with the `Who`, `When` and `Quoted` they share with the section beneath a quest, are `ChannelClaims`, which `ChannelText.Claim` and `Beneath` call, so the page's twin and the claim's wording stay one string. The quest's take judged after a session ended (`TakenAway`, `TakenAt`, `BroughtForward`) is Course's pure `QuestTakes`, and the transient end is `LimitResume.EndedTransient`; `Planner`'s names delegate, so the planner, the loop and the tests keep their spelling. `LimitResume.Line` takes the quest and the record a start would resume (`start.Quest`, `start.Resumes`) in place of the `Consideration`, its one caller passing both; `HandOverAskWord.RunAsync` takes the home, the ids and the verb its one caller held in the world and the ask; `StopAnswer` moves to `Course/Domain/` beside `SessionMoves`, its lowest reader.

The static lines the executors read move down to their owners and the runner's names alias them: `ChatTurns.LostLine` to Events' `SessionNotes.LostLine` (beside `WithdrawnLine`, read by `Acp`, the continuations' words and the sessions' terminal), the lines a turn's message and an ending put in the record to `ChatRecorded` and `ChatEnded` (`ChatRunner.Recorded`, `Told`, `EndedNote`, `NotKept` and `EndedByPersonNote` keep their spellings for the tests). `StartFrom` (the start-from codes the page's twin reads by the file's name, which it keeps) moves to `Course/Chat/` with `StartFromTests`; it names nothing of the runner.
