No behaviour, namespace, stored or wire shape, sentence, log line, evaluation order or refusal changes; each moved body is byte-identical to the one it left. §14.2's table gives the cuts no row of their own (row 15 names what Work orders owns, and the design's other rows name its readers), so each was decided from the code by the principle §14.2 states: the lowest subdomain that reads a type owns it, and what only an owner can make is reached through a port it installs. Each cut:
- **Help (2).** `SessionsCommand` and `SessionGroups` told Ask Daoris's record by `HelpRoom.Repository`, which is `KnowledgeRoomIdentity.Repository` already; they read that.
- **Plugins (6).** The terminal's door word is `WorkplaceDoors.Terminal`, which `PluginEvents.Terminal` aliases (`SessionsCommand`'s and `WorkCommand`'s); the two loads of the home's plugins for a roster or a cool-off go through Agents' required `AgentPluginReads.Current.Load`, which Plugins installs over the same `PluginCatalog.Load(home, built.Names)`. `WorkObligations`' two Plugins types left with the records they judge (below).
- **Review (6).** The planner's sit and the set-up's environment are `WorkOrders/Domain/SetUpSits.cs`, the planner being their lowest reader: bodies byte for byte but the rule standing for a repository, which Knowledge's required `KnowledgeReviews.Current.Review` answers (Review installs it over `ReviewRules.Resolve`, as the room's reads already do); `ReviewSetUps.Sits` and `Environment` delegate, so the loop, the desk and the tests keep their spelling. The environments told as "`a`, `b` or `c`" and the person's verdict as the machine log's `review.verdict` line are `WorkOrders/Domain/ReviewDoorWords.cs`, the quest's and the ask's doors being their lowest readers (`ReviewRules.Either` and `ReviewLines.Verdict` delegate); the verdict's door is Workplace's `LandingDoors.Terminal`, which `ReviewDoors.Terminal` aliases, and a choice's problem is the settings' `ReviewKey.NameProblem`, which `ReviewRules.NameProblem` already delegated to. `TickReport` carried `IReadOnlyList<OpinionJudged>`, whose gate is the review's `OpinionGateState`, and `OpinionJudged` is named by the look's executor, the host's loop and the page's route, so it stays where it is: the report holds the new facet `IJudgedOpinion` (session, quest, repository, workspace, since, auto), which `OpinionJudged` implements, and the host's loop (`Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs`, the one reader of the report's list) reads them back as the review's records with one `Cast`.
- **Setup (2).** The intake's room reads what a repository that declared nothing says about itself through the required `IWorkOrdersSetup` (`Describe(root)`), which Setup installs from `Setup/WorkOrdersSetupWiring.cs` over `SelfDescriptionFiles.Read`, resolved only for such a repository; `RepositoryDescription` implements the room's `IIntakeDescription` (summary, stack, source), which `IntakeRoom` tells a repository by.
- **Workflows (14).** The workflow choice's words that two doors judged a shape by are the settings': `WorkflowKey.Current`, `WorkflowProblem` and `UndeclaredInWorkspace` (new there, byte for byte; `WorkflowSelection`'s names alias them) with `WorkflowKey.IsKindId`, `KindIdProblem` and `IsId` already there (`WorkflowSelection.IsWorkflow` and `WorkflowNamed.IsId` have the same body). The ask's check that a saved workflow can be chosen, the intake's mapping of each kind to its workflow with what it lowers of the person's part, and what a unit still owes in the nodes' and resources' records go through the required `IWorkOrdersWorkflows` (`Unusable`, `KindsOf`, `Obligations`, `Owing`), which Workflows installs from `Workflows/WorkOrdersWorkflowsWiring.cs`, resolved only where one is asked. The records' reading moves whole to `Workflows/WorkflowObligations.cs` (`Read`, `Of`, `Owing` and the two entries, byte for byte; eleven tuples, as the records and their plugins' vocabulary are Workflows' and below it), leaving `WorkObligations` in Work orders with `Attach`, which joins them to the units, and `Read` and `Owing` answering through the port; `Of`, which takes the records, is `WorkflowObligations.Of` (two tests follow it).

Each cut:
- **The node declaration (nine tuples: `NodeDeclaration` in `PluginManifest`, `PluginPageRead`, `PluginSteps` and `PluginCycles`, `NodeReach` in `PluginSteps`, `PluginCycles` and `PluginTrials`, `NodeRetry` and `NodePerform` in `PluginSteps`).** `NodeDeclaration`, `NodeRetry`, `NodePerform` and `NodeReach` leave `Trees/Nodes/NodeContract.cs` for `Plugins/Domain/NodeDeclaration.cs`, whole and unchanged: a manifest's step is read into the declaration, the plugin page carries it, and the performed steps' cycles and trials select by the reach, so Plugins is the lowest reader of each (Workflows, the runtime, the node table and Ask Daoris's workflow proposals name them from above; no subdomain below Plugins reads one). Every namespace stays `Daoris.Driver`, so no reader changed a line. `NodeKinds`, `NodeSource`, `NodeAuthority` and `NodeAuthorities` stay in Workflows, which no Plugins file names.
- **The node table's judgement (one: `NodeDeclarations` in `PluginSteps`).** `NodeDeclarations.Problem` judges a declaration by the authority by kind, which is Workflows' (`NodeAuthorities`, `NodeKinds`, `NodeSource`, `NodeRefusal`, `WireEnd`), so it stays; `PluginSteps` asks the required `IPluginStepRules.Problem` (`Plugins/PluginStepRules.cs`), which Workflows installs from a `[ModuleInitializer]` in `Workflows/PluginStepRulesWiring.cs` over `NodeDeclarations.Problem`, resolved at the one place a step is judged, with the same order and the same code.
- **The work runtime's ports (two: `IWorkRuntimePort` in `Hooks`, and `IWorkPerformPort`, which it extends and which would have become a twelfth tuple the moment the first moved).** Both interfaces were declared in Workflows (`Trees/Nodes/NodeRuntime.cs`, `WorkPerformer.cs`) over types that are Plugins' (`WorkPerformCall`, `WatchedUnit`, `WorkStepHeard`, `WorkWatchFrame` and the work wire's answers) and implemented only by `HookSet` and the tests' stand-ins, so they are `Plugins/WorkRuntimePorts.cs`, byte for byte. The performer, the observer, the cleaner, the runtime and the held attempts take them from above as before.

**Note, 2026-10-29 (row 1mod-xvi: Review's cuts built, on the move commit `17e4a597f`).** The twenty-one "Review names Workflows" tuples are gone, so Review names nothing above it: **34 to 13 tuples, 3 to 2 families**. A type that another family's tuple names stayed where it is (`WorkflowProcess`, `WorkflowGateState`, `GraphGateState` and the rest of the workflows' types are named by no tuple left but stay Workflows', their readers above Review). Each cut:
- **ChatRuns (6 files).** The chat run's spelling and look folder are Workplace's already (`WorkplaceRunFiles.IsChatRun`, `LooksFolder`, which `ChatRuns` answers through), so the opinion due, the gate, the review gate, the gate files and the chat looks read those. The press's chat line (`LineOf`) is `WorkplaceWorkflows.Current.ChatPressOf(...).Line`, which parses the records and answers the record's line, or its own id where it holds none, as the capture did; the chat run a record is one of is the participants' `ParticipantsWorkflows.Current.ChatOf`, called over the same parse and the same case-insensitive match (`OpinionPresses.ChatRunOf`).
- **WorkflowProcess (6 files), WorkflowKinds, WorkflowProcesses.** `OpinionLook` reads only what `IWorkplaceProcess` holds (the opinion rule and why it cannot start, the problem and the unread), so it takes that. The review gate takes the new `IReviewedProcess` (the review rule and the version's look) and the session checks and the long-call bound the new `ICheckedProcess` (a graph version's claimed options and, new on the process, `WorkChecks`, the steps version's work step's `checks` field, which `SessionChecks.Of` read through `WorkflowKinds.Work` and `Steps`); `WorkflowProcess` implements both. `WorkflowLook` (the record a version's look is) moves whole to `Review/Domain/WorkflowLook.cs`, `ReviewGate.Decide` being its lowest reader. The second opinion's ask reads the run's process through the participants' `ReadProcess`, whose `run` is now nullable as `WorkflowProcesses.Read`'s is, and the run a chain's work is, `ReviewGate.RunOf` (body unchanged; `WorkflowRunBindings.RunOf` answers through it).
- **The landing gate's workflow and graph parts (WorkflowGateState, WorkflowGateStates, GraphGateState, WorkflowGate, WorkflowLines, WorkflowKinds, WorkflowProcess in `OpinionGate.cs`).** `LandingGate` holds the run's own part and the graph's as the facets `IWorkflowGatePart` (state, whether it speaks, whether it lets the work go, what it says, its process, and `Held`, the machine log's `workflow.held` line, the landing step's where the part lets go) and `IGraphGatePart` (state, lets go, says, asks a pass, and `Held`, the graph's line for the process it is handed), declared in `Review/Domain/RunParts.cs`; `WorkflowGateState` and `GraphGateState` implement them, building the same lines by the same `WorkflowLines`, and the gate's `Process` is `IWorkplaceProcess`, whose `LandingSays` is `WorkflowGate.LandingSays` already. The readers that need the concrete records (the keep command, the graph press, the bridge's workflow waits and the tests) cast back through `LandingGateParts.WorkflowPart` and `GraphPart` in the workflows.

**Note, 2026-10-29 (row 1d-ii part 1 of 5 built: the desktop's half of the `continued` refusal, Part B).** **The wire**, fixed by the steward for parts 1 and 2: the say door's refusal word is `continued`, the driver's existing `WordsNever.Continued`, now one constant with `SayAnswer.Continued`; the refusal's response gains `head`, the head record's id as a string, at the status the say route gives its other refusals (409, beside `error` and `refusal`); `/api/protocol`'s marks gain `head: 1` in part 2, and `opens` is raised to 2 in part 3; in the service's exclusive step `Continued` comes right after row p's settled check and before `Never`, `running` and the key-already-waiting answer, so a retry naming a record the line has left is answered `continued`, the caller forwards it to the head, and a key already waiting there answers as kept (O8-F1's older build's unkeyed successor). Part 1 sends and answers no `continued`: against today's hosts Daoris works as before, and parts 1 and 2 land in one batch. **The client and the mark:** `ServiceClient.SayAsync` reads `SayAnswer.Head` from a `continued` refusal naming a non-empty `head`, and no head from any other refusal or from a `continued` that names none; `SessionOpenDoors.HeadAsync` keeps the `head` mark beside `opens` by the host's run, read again once an answer names another run or none, none for a host without the protocol or whose answer does not read, and never a throw. **The walk:** `HeadWalk` (`Course/GoOn/WordsHead.cs`) holds the records one word was said to, ending at a head it asked already or past eight; `WordsHead.FollowAsync` says the caller's own say again at each head, so the key, level, author, channel and files' names go as they went first, and `ChatFiles.Carry` keeps the files in each head's folder before the say there. **The dispatchers** (the steward's pre-mortem, point 1): `ChatTurns.KeepAsync` and `DrivenInbox.KeepAsync` answered `continued` return the head (`TurnKept.Head`; `InboxKeep.Continued` with `InboxKept.Head`) before the fallback that holds a refused word in this process, so nothing is queued, steered, attempted or written on the old door and `KeepOrHoldAsync` never holds it; a reword so answered changes nothing (`QueueEdit.Taken`); `ChatRunner.SayKeptAsync` answers `ChatSaid.Head`. **The census**, `ContinuedReadersCensusTests`, names the 18 says to the door in 16 files, each made as named, the dispatchers' keeps in seven files and the box's route. *Own:* `SaidDoor` hands the head to its dispatcher; the headless `LoopWords` says the request again at the head through the head's inbox or record, key, level and files whole, the files carried by its new `Home`, and names the head as `WordsHeld.Head`, written `head` beside the request's answer; the shell's `SessionWords` says it again through its whole judgement from its record path, its pump (once, under the word's key, off the held file once the head took it), a stale inbox and the turns, the head as `WordsAnswer.At`, the shell's own. *Follows:* the terminal's verb where no loop runs, naming the head (with a loop it names the head the loop's answer names and follows the words there); a failed run's words (`WordsLeft`, files carried, shown at the record that kept them); the ended limit move's composed message; the reopen and go-on words, a running session's stale inbox said at the head through the same doors; the landing's word; the terminal's rebase word; a refused start's moved words. *Not on:* Daoris's restart word at a shell's close; the chat's limit word before its own go-on (`ChatGoOn.WakeAsync`) and the cool-off's (`DriverContinuations.KeepLimitWordAsync`), which exist to wake their own record and at the head would wait with nothing to take them off; the live wake's limit word and the transient go-on, `running` words of the run this process holds; the supervisor's release, its door today's; a channel message, whose queue is the channel; and `LongCalls`' word about this run's call. `ConversationRoutes.cs:142` reaches the door only through `SessionWords.SayAsync`. **An older host:** one without the `head` mark never answers `continued`, and the say paths that judge by the driver's own `WordsNever.ContinuedIn` (the shell's say and pump, the terminal's verb) keep it, conversations alone, as today; with the mark it stands aside for the service's judgement (§19.12's row). **An older desktop** meeting part 2's answer reads `continued` as a refusal word it does not act on: the terminal and the headless door say the service's sentence as it comes, an older shell's pump says the held word lost with it, and an older dispatcher holds the word in its process as for any refusal. `A_refusal_word_the_reader_does_not_act_on_says_the_services_sentence_as_it_comes` ran on the base build, `3fb3559c3`, with part 2's exact answer (`continued` with `head`) and passed; The cases: a terminal's word with a file landing once on a closed quest's session's successor conversation, the head the driver's own walk cannot see; the headless door through a chain of two, under the request's key, its file carried, shown and nudged at the head; a stale inbox holding nothing; a planted cycle ending the walk at the door and in the pump, the word said lost; the box's word with its key, file and display choice; a stale driven inbox in the shell; the pump once under its key and through a chain of two; and a host without the mark refused before anything is asked. **Readings taken:** (1) the forward is the caller's, never the client's: a client that walked by itself would leave a word meant to wake its own record on the head with nothing to take it off, and say a `running` dispatcher's word at a head this process does not run; (2) the bound of eight is past any line two says can see move, and ends a host that names a new head at every say; (3) the shell's and the headless door's forwards go through their whole judgement, so a head this loop runs takes the word at its inbox or turns; (4) `Superseded`, a driven session whose quest ran later here, is still judged before the say as today, though part 2's head would name the same session; (5) the pump's forward keeps today's hold of its record's turn while it says the word at the head, as the forward to `ContinuedIn`'s successor does, so a planted cycle with words held on both records at once could hold both turns, a case no move writes; (6) `WordsHeld.Head` and `WordsAnswer.At` are additive, and the page's answer names `to` alone, as before. **Part 2 must match:** `continued` with a non-empty `head` at the say route's refusal status, its `error` naming the head, which an older desktop says; `head: 1` among `/api/protocol`'s marks beside the run; `x-daoris-run` on the say's answer, so the mark is not read again at every say. **Not run, the parent's:** the Process classes over the changed say paths, `SettledServiceProcessTests`, `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests`, `ChatGoesOnProcessTests`, `ChatTransientProcessTests`, `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `LimitGoesOnProcessTests`, `HandOverProcessTests` and the modules' `DriverModuleConversationProcessTests`; none can meet `continued` before part 2, and the say paths that judge by the driver's own walk read `/api/protocol` once more where it finds a successor.

**Note, 2026-10-29 (row 1d-ii part 2 of 5 built: the head at the say door, in the service, Part B).** Built in the service beside part 1's readers in the driver and the modules, one batch; no word moves yet and `opens` stays 1, which part 3 raises. **The walk:** `Lines.WalkAsync` follows a record's line forward inside its caller's step, one probe a hop on `sessions_from`, and for a driven part with no successor one on `sessions_quest` that reads the quest's newest local run's id alone, reading the record whole only where it is another one; it answers the head, the records visited and how it ended (`Head`, `Cycle`, `Bound`). `Lines.HeadOf` is its head, null on a cycle or at the bound, so d-i's keyed open reads it as before. A teammate's record is on no local line: it is its own head with no probe, so the say door refuses it `not-ours` as before, never `continued` to a local run of its quest. **One bound, `Lines.Bound`, 256 hops,** for the walk forward and for the settlement's walk back along `from`, which row p stopped at 256 without saying so: either walk reaching it refuses with a sentence, since a settlement read over a line cut short could keep a taken word a second time. A line of 256 parts is far past any the install makes, and the walk costs a line only its own hops. **The say door's step, in order:** the record's absence; the walk; with `settled` and a key whose word does not wait on the record named, a word under the key waiting at the head, a record other than the one named, answered settled naming the head (*O8-F1*), then the settlement over the record named, its predecessors back and the walk's records forward (*C6-1.3*), a line not read whole refused `unplaced`; then a line that loops or runs past the bound refused `unplaced`, and a record that is not its line's head refused `continued` with `head`, nothing kept; then `Never`, `running`, the key's waiting word answered as kept, and the keep, as before. So a retry named to a record the line has left is settled where its word left the line or waits at the head, refused `continued` where it waits on a part the line has left, and kept once at the head; an older client, which asks no settlement, is refused `continued` too. The supervisor's door is unchanged. **The desks:** the helper's and the partner's hand walk with `Lines.WalkAsync` from the record their helper serves or their partner leads, across kinds and to a driven quest's later run here, and read the settlement over that record's line back and the walk's records forward; the helper keeps its reach of sixteen links (HELPERWAIT1b, the driver's look's) inside the walk's bound; a line that loops or runs past the bound is refused with its sentence at both, where the partner desk handed a piece where its sixteen hops stopped (`docs/FIX-LOG.md`). **The wire:** `SessionSayRefusal.Continued` and `Unplaced`, `continued` and `unplaced` at 409, the say door's status for a record as it stands; `head` on `SessionSayRefusalResponse` beside `continued` alone; `continued`'s sentence names the record named and the head, which an older desktop prints as it comes; `Program.SayRefusal`, now a member of the host's `Program`, names every value (`none` for the value never sent), so its `running` default is reached by no declared value; `GET /api/protocol` answers `head: 1` beside `settled: 1` and `opens: 1`, and every answer, a say's among them, carries `x-daoris-run`.

**Proof**, each case seen failing: first as a compile failure, then, with the two values and `Head` declared and nothing built, 18 cases of `SessionLedgerTests` (the head across kinds: a chat's successor, a driven part's successor conversation, a driven part with no successor whose quest ran again here and one whose quest did not; a chain of three; a planted cycle; a line past the bound and one at it; `continued` before `Never`, `running` and a waiting key, at the head a waiting key answered as kept) and `SaidSettledTests` (open before say and say before open, each across two connections; *O8-F1* both ways; *C5-1.2*, *C5-3.2*, *C6-1.3* with and without a stored `entry`; a keyless word by its id and a re-say at the head after `continued`; the settlement's walk past the bound; both desks at the head). **The two-connection order is held by the test, not by timing:** a function and a `TEMP` trigger only one connection sees (`SaidSettledTests.HoldAsync`) stop that connection's own statement inside its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` step, so the other host's step waits on the write lock until the test releases it; the open, part 3's mover, can be held the same way. The cases a stub passes were seen failing by breaking their guard: the head judged before the settlement failed six (row p's retry to a record that runs again among them), the walk back one hop short the case at the bound, the head's waiting key off *O8-F1*'s second way. The wire's four (`SaidSettledHostTests`' marks, word table and `continued`, `SessionOpensHostTests`' say after a keyed open) failed with the mark, `head` and the two words taken out; four more, written for part 1's readers (409 with `error`, `refusal` and a `head` that is a record here; the sentence naming the head word for word; `x-daoris-run` on a `continued`, a kept and a settled say; a planted cycle answered `unplaced` with no `head`), each failed with its guard off. `LocalHostTests`' MSG1a case failed until a teammate's record was its own head. Row p's case reading a later part's settlement from an earlier one now finds it, the forward walk being this row's, and row p's cycle case keeps its word before planting the cycle, since a line that loops keeps none. **Ran:** `SessionLedgerTests` whole (119), `SaidSettledTests` (31), and the service's classes reaching the door, the desks and the walk (370), the HTTP host's (153). `HeadServiceProcessTests` (the driver's tests, beside `SettledServiceProcessTests`, over `RealServiceHost`: Codex's case at the head and a late word to an ended driven part reaching the conversation that went on from it) is built and not run, the parent's with `process.runsettings`.

**The latency, prepared on a scratch service** (`SaidSettledMeasureTests` with `DAORIS_MEASURE_SAID=head`; two copies under `_fixtures/` of one file seeded with 1,667 chats, 1,667 driven parts, 556 chains of three and 60,000 settlements over a week; the copy before is the same door with `WalksHead` off, the walk being code and not schema; 400 rounds alternated; never the install or its home). Pooled say door p95, before and after: a chat with no successor 18.42 / 19.05 ms (1.035), a driven part with no successor 17.88 / 18.95 ms (1.060), both 18.10 / 19.05 ms (1.052), every say 19.80 / 19.14 ms (0.967, the chain's first part refused without a write). The reads alone cost the probes: the same key while its word waits p50 0.46 / 0.65 ms on a chat and 0.46 / 0.65 ms on a driven part, the retry after the take 0.56 / 0.73 and 0.56 / 0.77; a write's p50 moves by 2 % at most. A first run, before the quest's probe read the id alone, had the driven branch at 1.092; the id-only probe is what the second run measures. The landing limit is the install's say door p95 within a tenth, the parent's to measure.

**Readings taken:** (1) the bound is 256 for both walks, refused with a sentence; (2) `unplaced`, a word beside the steward's fixed `continued`, is the refusal of a line that cannot be walked, which a reader before this row, and part 1's, reads as a refusal it does not know and says its sentence; (3) a teammate's record is its own head; (4) the helper's hand keeps sixteen links, the driver's look reading as many; (5) `SessionLedger.WalksHead` is internal and turned off by the measurement alone. **For part 3:** the open moving the old part's words under their keys with transfer ids, `opens` raised to 2, its moved-word cases and the delivery sites, on this walk and this door, the two-connection hold above proving its race.

**Note, 2026-10-29 (FOUNDATION1e part 2: `LineCourse.Decide` and its table, read by nothing).** Row e's second part writes the one decision, `Course/Domain/LineCourse.Decide.cs`, a partial of `LineCourse`: pure, synchronous, naming no seam type, it answers the nevers and a held line first and then each trigger's path, and every answer carries the preconditions it was read on. Its account step reads the account rule's answer once, as the executor hands it, and never walks: the executor's one reading of where the line's next account is (the rule over the cooling store, a new part's walk, a role's order). Part 1's facts were widened where a path's answer needs one: a word's key and whether it was said after the record ended; the head's session, quest status, Ask Daoris, a refusal at the open, the protocol door, an agent no longer here, the resume judge's code (read by its kinds alone) and the revision; the account reading's recorded pick, a limited conversation's wake reset, a later choice on another agent, the walk's agent and the curve's step; *Try again*; the hand-over's why and asker on the press; the limit word's keys (`CourseLimit`); and the carry-on beside the trigger. The answers gained a named account that is the one account and whether it is kept as the pick, the restart word, a key on Daoris's word, a wait's doors, a hold's code, two hold reasons (`Stays`, where nothing moves the line and today writes nothing, and `Refused`, a press that does not apply, by its code) and three doors (*Go on with Daoris's brief*, a hand-over, the person's yes to an agent's ask). The proof is `Driver.Tests/Course/course-cases.txt`, mirroring `line-account-cases.txt`: 253 rows by path in the design's order (the account rule's answers, then B, E, J, D; A, F, K, N; G, H, I with a consent case per door; C; the answers-alone hold; a standing move), each ported from the case of today's suite that shows the path's answer, with every case of the 26 counted suites either a row's or left out by reason (an executor's effect, a reading handed in as a fact, the planner's own judgement, inside the run, a press's argument, a precondition, or which executor takes a record). Nine rows read a source line that no suite pins today (named `code:` in the table: three word readings of point 1, the refusal at the open and the unjudged model of point 7, point 3's driven reading, point 10's live limit move given back its own account, a pipe door's limit and Ask Daoris passed by the curve), and the standing move's three are the design's, since no path writes a move record yet. Path L stays a non-decision (a send-back answers as B's words kept, checked case by case) and M reserved (no case answers a carry). Two door codes differ from the say door's and are kept by the hand-over: a stand-down and a superseded session are `Never` to the course, where the quest's hand-over refuses them `quest-elsewhere` and `not-last-run`. Where two paths answer the same facts two ways, the decision tells them apart by the trigger or a fact and keeps both; each is a decision for the row that adopts it, h for conversations and i for driven lines, with its rows in the table:

1. **Which words go to a new part** (rows B-30, B-29, E-16, A-26; h, i). One rule written at five sites, read three ways: the person's words alone where a part opens for a lost conversation, a press or a fall-back (`BriefedMove.cs:99`, `:183`; `LimitMove.cs:337`, `:449`, `DriverContinuations.cs:1057`, `:1102`, `Driver.cs:2501` without a switch); the person's and Daoris's words that state a fact where a cooling own account's switch opens it (`DaorisWords.NewPartCarries`, `DaorisWords.cs:56`, at the same five with `switching`); and every word of Daoris's where a resume opens a new session (`DriverContinuations.cs:966`, `:1102`), D79's resume, which is none of A to N. The course reads `CourseWord.NewPartCarries` where the answer is the switch and the person's otherwise. Rows h and i each write it once, through the course's words.
2. **Daoris's limit word** (E-02, F-03; h, i). The same sentence twice (`LimitResetWord.Text`, `LimitMove.cs:691`; `LimitResume.Words`, `LimitResume.cs:19`). A conversation's wake keys it by the wake's clock (`LimitWaits.cs:183`, `:251`; `ChatGoOn.cs:437`), a driven line by the cool-off's `until` as its note says it (`DriverContinuations.cs:78`, `LimitResume.cs:57-62`). The course keys by `CourseLimit.Woke` for a conversation and `Until` for a driven line; one key and one sentence is h's and i's to settle.
3. **The maker's usage before an own-account resume** (E-02, F-07; h). A driven limited line's ready own account is asked its maker's reading first, a reading still out held as cooling (`HarnessRoster.cs:1038-1043`); a conversation's wake reads the cooling store alone (`LimitWaits.cs:163`, `:177-180`). The course takes the answer as read; whether the wake asks the maker is h's.
4. **A move to another agent at a limit** (D-06, K-03; h, i). A conversation walks its own agent's accounts first (`LimitMove.cs:104-105`, `:294-296`), then its actor's choices on another agent with the out agent's removed (`:589`), never for an agent's pick (`:576`), a closed quest's session or a go-on outside a limit (`:308-314`). A driven line at a limit walks Build's order choice by choice as a new start does (WALKAGENT1, `Driver.cs:2047-2058`, `:2128-2136`), so another agent comes in the order's own sequence and no pick holds it, a handed quest keeping its agent (`:2048`). The course reads `CourseAccount.Later` for a conversation and the walk's answer with its agent for a driven line; one order, §3.2 step 4's, is h's and i's.
5. **When a running hand-over happens** (H-01, I-01; h, i). A conversation's at its turn's end (`ChatHandOver.cs:67-69`, `:142`); a quest's session stopped at once for the person's press (`DriverHandOvers.cs:102-131`) and taken at its turn's end for an agent's ask (`:227-235`, `:287-303`). The design states it as a difference of door (§3.1's paths table; D163 point 1, D188 point 1): each row keeps its door's.
6. **Cannot go on's fall-back** (E-14, LA-13; h). A conversation whose own account is signed out, refused or gone holds as *cannot go on* with *Start a conversation with these words* (`ChatGoOn.cs:642-648`; the conversation walks nothing, `LineAccount.cs:329-332`); a driven record walks at once to a new part, `retry` (`LineAccount.cs:329-331`, `Driver.cs:2541-2568`). Today's answers stay until the owner answers the design's open question 2 (§8); row h adopts that answer.
7. **The asked model not offered** (B-28, B-27, LA-19; h). The driven go-on judges its own account's reading for the record's asked model (`Driver.cs:2100`, `HarnessRoster.cs:1013-1015`) and walks the same choice's other accounts with its own passed (`LineAccount.cs:302-303`), a new part `retry`; a conversation's go-on asks no model (`ChatGoOn.cs:490-494`) and resumes, and only a refusal at the open starts its actor afresh, `not-offered` (`ChatGoOn.cs:561-566`, `BriefedMove.cs:179-236`). Whether a conversation's go-on asks the offer is h's.
8. **A named account: the pick** (H-09, H-11, I-01; h, i). The person's hand-over press keeps its account as the conversation's pick; an agent's ask and the curve open theirs as a pick without keeping it (`ChatHandOver.cs:156-160`, `CurveMove.cs:114-115`); a handed quest's named account comes first, then its agent's walk (`Driver.cs:2145-2147`, `HarnessRoster.cs:929-940`). The course says it as `Pick` kept by the person's press alone, and `Named` for a quest's session; §3.2 step 4 keeps today's readings, which h and i adopt so.
9. **The answers-alone hold's moment** (AA-18, AA-17; h, i). A conversation's go-on judges it before its resume (`ChatGoOn.cs:548`), a live conversation's helper's answer at its turn (`ChatHelperWords.cs:96`), the driven look before the planner reads the marks (`Driver.cs:1002-1005`), and the supervisor's route at its keeping and its release, refused `supervisor-bound` so nothing is kept to be held (`SupervisorDoor.cs:305-306`, `Driver.cs:989-992`). The course answers the supervisor's word at its keeping with a hold that offers no door and every other answer at the bound with *Go on with this answer*, *Not now* and a word of the person's; one moment for every door is h's and i's.
10. **Its own account for an automatic part** (D-08, K-08; h, i). A conversation's automatic part never takes its own account: the live limit move and the ended one read the walk's own account as no room and wait (`LimitMove.cs:107`, `:298-299`), the ended one allowing it only where the conversation is proven lost. A driven line's takes the walk's account, its own included: a carry-on after a cut-off (`Driver.cs:2137-2148`), a limited line on a door not known to fail closed, whose step 1 is the protocol door's alone (`DriverContinuations.cs:54-63`), named `carry-on` (`Driver.cs:4782-4788`), and a fall-back keeping its resume's account (`HarnessRoster.cs:1126-1128`). §3.2 step 6 states the rule the course would take; h and i adopt it with their path.

Nothing reads the course: `CourseVocabularyTests` still finds no production source naming one of its types, and the census lists every site `not yet moved`.

**Note, 2026-10-29 (FOUNDATION1f-i part 1: the move record's tables, its one-standing index and its claim by process run, read by nothing).** Row f-i is built in three parts, and this is the first; part 2 is the take-up of dead runs' moves at its place in the come-up's order, and `move.quiet`; part 3 the moved words' `sent` and a hold closed `resolved`. Nothing reads the record, so Daoris works as before. **The schema** is store migration 4, two tables an older store-aware build never opens, so the writer stamp stays at 1 and the step runs at the first start whatever the confirmation says. `moves` holds one row per move: required are the columns every decision writes (the record left, the executor `conversation` or `driven`, `how`, `from_why`, `from_by`, the phase, `claim_run` naming a `store_runs` row, `claim_generation`, the decision's moment); nullable are each later phase's moment, the `opens` key, the destination, the effect mark (what, begun, outcome, ended), the fall-back's code, the hold's and the close's reasons, the settled account and the brief's recipe (its why and its event bound). `move_words` holds each word in transfer by its move and transfer id, with its word id, producer key and order, and a disposition and its moment that later parts write. Neither holds a word's text, a path or a credential. `moves_standing` is a unique index on the record left over the rows whose phase is not `delivered`, `fell-back` or `closed`. No CHECK holds a phase or a reason, so a phase a newer build adds stands and is kept as written. The store's connection now enforces foreign keys, so a claim names a run the store holds and a word its move, and a move's id is never handed out again.

**The one-standing rule.** A decision is a single insert inside the write's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. Where the index refuses it, the standing row is read under the same lock and answered, and the transaction is rolled back, so nothing is written and no revision moves; no upsert and no earlier read decides it. A move that ends frees its record, its row kept as evidence, and the record's next move is a new row; every phase before the end, a hold among them, keeps the record. **The claim.** Every phase, effect mark and outcome is one write that checks inside its transaction that the move is there, that its run and generation are the claim's, and that the claim's run is the writer's; the update names the claim in its own condition too. Phases go forward only: a hold only closes, an ended move takes nothing, and a phase written again is the first write's. A second effect mark over one with no outcome is refused, since an effect begun and never recorded is a taker's hold. A take is one update that raises the generation, guarded by the run and generation the taker read, and is refused, with nothing written, for a move gone or ended, for the taker's own run, for a generation that moved since it was read, and for a run alive by the lease's test or one that cannot be told dead (a start the run could not read when it began, a process whose start no caller may read, a run the store holds no row of). **Liveness** is `Platform/ProcessLiveness`, the lease's own test taken out of `HomeLease` (a second's tolerance, a start that cannot be read counting as alive); the lease calls it, and the launcher compiles it beside the lease. **The run and kind** moved from the bindings' store to `Store/StoreProcess`, below both families, so the bindings and the move store write under one run per process; the window's root names its process `shell`, the headless loop names its `host` once its verb is read, and every other process stays `verb`. **A deleted record takes its moves** through a Workplace port beside the bindings' (`WorkplaceMoveRecords`, which the Course installs): `SessionHomeFiles.Remove` deletes a record left's moves and their words and names them `hand-over` among what went, the name the boxes they replace go by; a store that will not take the delete leaves them, named among what stayed, its path once among what failed, and a home with no store gets none.

**Settled before the build.** The box importers (the limit-move and the hand-over boxes) are part 2's, built and tested there and called from no production site until rows f-v and f-vi, since importing a box now would set it aside from today's readers, still its only owners. How the course reads a destination's standing move is row h's and part 3's, so part 1 answers only the record left's standing move and nothing calls it. **Readings taken:** (1) §3.3 names no `resolving` phase: a hold being resolved stands until its close commits, which the proof shows; (2) `move_words` lands whole now, its disposition unwritten until part 3, since the table is new and no older build holds it; (3) a limit's refused turns' bound and the channel messages held, which §3.3 lists with the delivery, are left to the rows whose moves deliver, each a nullable column then; (4) only the record left's moves go with a delete, and a move whose destination is deleted first closes `gone` at a later take-up or read, not here; (5) the shell's kind is named at the window's root alone, since the modules' loop runs in that process.

**Proof**, each seen failing first: every new type first failed to compile; then, against the code with step 4 emptied and the delete unwired, 26 cases failed at run time; then rounds of mutants were each caught at the line that holds them (the own-run check, the take's generation, the in-transaction generation and run checks, a start never read counted dead, the index without its predicate, a refused decision committed, liveness ignored, a second mark over an open effect; in the delete, the wrong name, the store's path twice, the refusals' kinds narrowed, the held read's fallback emptied). `WorkplaceMoveRecordsPortsTests` (8 cases: the installed factory the Course's, the delete opening it once after the files and the bindings, each refusal's kind a failure at the store with the held moves staying, a missing factory required only where the delete reaches the moves), `MoveStoreTests` (24 cases: two runs on two connections deciding one record at once 25 times, one row each time; each end freeing the record and every standing phase keeping it; a take once with the generation raised, refused from a live, an own, an unreadable or a moved claim; the delete; the downgrade, an older build of three steps writing as it did and finding no box, the newer build back reading its move as it left it), `MoveStoreCensusTests` (no production source names the move store's types but the delete's wiring), `StoreProcessTests` (the roots' kinds read off their sources), `ProcessLivenessTests` and five `DesktopStoreTests` cases (step 4 with the stamp at 1, its columns, its foreign keys, a step-3 store migrated whatever the confirmation, a step-3 build still writing). `StructureTests` whole with no census entry added, `CourseCensusTests`, `CourseVocabularyTests`, `BindingsTests`, `BindingPicksTests`, `HomeLeaseTests` and the deletion suites pass.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (row 1d-ii part 3 of 5 built: the open moves the words, in the service, Part B).** Built in the service and the driver's open door; the delivery sites are parts 4 and 5, so no executor sends a move yet and every one works as today. **The wire**, the steward's call: `OpenSessionRequest` and `OpenChatRequest` gain one optional member, `moves`, `{ from, words, read }`: the record the words move from, the ids the course named (`Carries`, judged in .NET) and the ids it saw waiting there when it read. `moves` goes beside `opens` alone: without a key it is refused 400 with nothing opened, since no receipt would answer a retry and a lost answer could move twice. Moves that do not read (a blank source, a source other than the open's `from`, a missing list, a blank id) are refused 400 `malformed` with the key's echo and no receipt, after the key's replay, as d-i's required fields are. The answer's `transfer` lists each moved word in order: `word`, its id on the record left; `id`, its id on the record opened; `key`, its producer key, no member for a word with none; `transfer`, the open's key and the word's id (`<opens>/<word>`), so a keyless word has one; `order`. `GET /api/protocol` answers `opens: 2`. **The read mark is the ids the course saw**, never a position or a moment: an id is the one thing of a word the course holds that no later write changes, `said` keeps no sequence, a list's positions shift at every take, and a word's moment is its caller's clock. A word on the source whose id is neither read nor named was kept since the read.

**The selection** is .NET's and is not judged again: every named word still waiting moves, whoever said it; of the words kept since the read, the person's (no `by`, `helper` or `from`) with no attempt move too. A late word a dispatcher attempted may have reached its agent, so it stays for the come-up to settle and is never sent again by itself; Daoris's own words, a helper's answer and another agent's claim kept since stay (§3.4 rule 6); a named word withdrawn since is gone and in no transfer. **The step**, `KeyedOpenAsync`'s one exclusive step: the key's receipt first, answering the stored destination and transfer and never reading a record's words again; the moves judged; the record the words leave, the open's `from` or, where the open names none, `moves.from` (start-from's `Start`, whose new record has no `from`), which must stand, be its line's head and not be active, as d-i's record left; the open; `SessionStore.MoveSaidAsync`, which reads both records' lists as stored and writes the source's remaining elements and the destination's moved ones ahead of any it holds, so the settlement's trigger writes a row on the source for each moved word; and the receipt with the transfer. A receipt that cannot be written throws and the step rolls back the open and the move together. **Each moved word keeps its id**: the destination opens in the same step and holds no word, so no id can collide there, and a d-i host replaying this build's receipt reads `Word`, `Key`, `Transfer` and `Order` and drops `Id`, so a reader falling back to `word` is right only because the id is kept. It keeps its element as stored, a later build's fields among them, but `attempt`, the source's dispatcher's mark: on the destination no dispatcher has written it, and a word carrying another record's attempt would be taken back as uncertain at the next come-up (`UncertainWords`) and never go; the course carries an attempted word only where a limit's conclusive refusal says it never reached its agent. The receipt keeps d-i's element shape with `Id` added; an element without it reads as its word's own. **Start-from's source stays its line's head:** the go-on guard reads only a keyed destination whose `from` is the record left, which every d-i receipt has, so a start's source can still be said to and go on, while a continued record cannot.

**The driver's door:** `SessionOpenDoors.OpenAsync(…, moves: MoveWords)` sends `moves` only beside the key to a host whose `opens` mark is 2 or more, and never a `moves` member the caller's body carries; `DestinationOpen.Moves` is true where the host was asked and echoed the key, and `DestinationOpen.Moved` lists each `MovedWord(Word, Id, Key, Transfer, Order)`, an element without `id` read as its `word` and one without a word, a transfer id or a whole order an answer that does not read, held as d-i holds one. **Parts 4 and 5 must send** `MoveWords` (the record left or the start's source, the `Carries` ids, the ids read) through this door, and **read** `Moved` whatever `Moves` says (a d-i host replaying a newer receipt answers what moved), delivering and taking each by `Id` on the destination and never by the old record's ids; where `Moves` is false and `Moved` empty, an `opens` 1 host or none, they read, deliver and take the old record's words themselves as today.

**Proof**, each case seen failing first: the service's as compile reds (`OpenMoves` and the transfer's `Id` missing), the host's at run time on the base code (`opens` answered 1, an empty transfer, `moves` without a key opening a record), the door's as a compile red. Six of the cases a partial build passes were then seen failing with a guard broken in turn: the attempt check off the late words and the attempt kept on the moved copy failed the selection case, the go-on guard reading any receipt the start-from case, the unkeyed refusal off the no-moves case, the receipt written `[]` the replay cases, the source check off the malformed case. `SessionOpensTests` (23, 6 new): the selection with a withdrawn named word, a late word, a helper's answer, an agent's claim, a late attempted word and a keyless word, metadata and order kept and the destination's later word behind; the replay after every moved word was taken, an older door's word kept on the source meanwhile and left there; the receipt's failure rolling the move back with no settlement; start-from into a record with no `from`, its source then said to and gone on, a running source refused; an older desktop's keyed open and every unkeyed open moving nothing; moves that do not read refused after the replay. `SaidSettledTests` (39, 8 new), each over two connections where it races: say before open moving the person's word, its retry named to A settled naming B and at B kept once; open before say leaving the say refused `continued` with nothing on A, kept once at B behind the moved word; a word moved through two successors one word, its retries named to any part settled; a keyless word moved under its transfer id and settled by its id on each record it left; a word moved from a record with no stored `entry` to one holding one, taken there, its retry to the old record answered as kept; Codex's case with the keyed open, the taken word moved by nothing and the pump's forward to the new head settled, and a waiting word moved and its forward answered as kept with that word; an older build's unkeyed open and an older desktop's keyed open moving nothing, K on A refused `continued` and kept once at the head, never settled. `SessionOpensHostTests` (2 new): the wire's transfer and the record answered, the replay's transfer byte for byte; moves without a key or that do not read refused, nothing opened. `SaidSettledHostTests`' marks case reads `opens` 2. `SessionOpenDoorsTests` (28, 5 new: a theory of four and a fact): moves sent only at a mark of 2 or more, a body's own `moves` never sent; the transfer read whole and an element with no `id` read as its word's. **Ran:** `SessionLedgerTests` whole with `SessionOpensTests` and `SaidSettledTests` (181), the host's `SessionOpensHostTests` and `SaidSettledHostTests` (18), the driver's `SessionOpenDoorsTests` (28), `GitSeedTests` and `ShortWaitsCensusTests` (13) and `StructureTests` (55). **Not run, the parent's:** `OpenMovesServiceProcessTests` (the driver's tests, beside `HeadServiceProcessTests`, over `RealServiceHost`: a keyed open through the driver's door moving a named and a late word, a late word's retry named to the source settled naming the destination, and the open asked again after both were taken answering the same destination and transfer), with `process.runsettings`. The say door's latency was not measured again: its path is unchanged, the open being its own step, and the say door reads nothing this part adds.

**Readings taken:** (1) the read mark is the ids seen; (2) a late attempted word stays, the conservative side of *never sent twice*; (3) the moved copy's attempt is cleared, the course having judged the word carried; (4) the word keeps its id, `id` and `word` both answered so a reader names the one it acts on; (5) `moves.from` must equal `from` where both are given, one source per open; (6) start-from's source is the receipt's record left, and the go-on guard reads only a destination that continues its record left. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1j part i of 3: one start plan and one walk, a quest's start and an intake on it, Part B).** Part i builds the plan in `Roles/StartPlan.cs`, beside `BuildWalk.cs`'s pass memory and pass record, as the design's §14.2 places it, and moves the two Loop walks that were near copies of each other onto it; nothing a person sees, says or waits on changes. **The plan:** `StartPlan` takes a work, resolves its role by `StartPlan.RoleOf`, the seeded role of the design's §2.2 table (Build's for `design` while no role takes it), and that role's order by `ActorChoices.Effective`, read once for the start; no catalogue is read until row x adopts `RoleCatalog.RoleFor` (*C5-3.6*). `ResolveAsync` is the one walk, choice by choice: an agent this machine lacks is passed `unavailable` and one the caller's gate refuses by the gate's code, each before any account is asked; then D197's account walk, judged for the entry's model; the first allowed is taken; with none, the start holds on the wait's sentence in the role's own words (*Build waits…*, *Intake waits…*), the soonest cool-off beside it. An order of one entry is today's walk exactly: its one account walk asked with the caller's flags, nothing passed or said. Its answer, `SettledSelection`, replaces `BuildWalk`: the role, the entry taken, the account walk's selection, the passes, the agent as the walk resolved it and the reservation. **The reservation:** the plan takes no count of its own. An account walk counts the start only where it chooses an account (`HarnessRoster.Chose`, under the count's lock, inside the walk's gate) and only where the start counts, so a start holds exactly one reservation, the taken entry's, and a look's starts spread as before, each count taken before the next start's walk; a probe, the re-ask among them, asks with `counts: false`, takes none, says no pass and remembers none. The open, and its one `Release` where the agent refuses the model, are unchanged and read the same number. **One vocabulary:** `StartPasses` names the seven codes once (`unavailable`, `no-posture`, `no-connector`, `not-offered`, `cooling`, `signed-out`, `held`), and `StartPasses.Of` reads a held account walk by its facts. **One comparison:** `ReaskMove.Same`, in `OneTurnReask.cs`, is the test the re-asks repeated (another entry of the refused one's agent, on the account it refused on); the plan's `ReaskAsync` and `OneTurnReask.SameAccountAsync` read it, and no sixth re-ask was written. **The moves:** `Driver.WalkBuildPassesAsync` and the start's re-ask build the quest's plan (`BuildPlan`: its gate `no-posture` and `no-connector`, its passes remembered under the quest), the re-ask still answering null where its walk throws; `RunIntakeAsync` builds the ask's plan once and reads its order, its walk and its re-ask from it, an order of one still resolving its agent before any account is asked and holding on what that walk throws, a walked order's walk throwing as before. The machine log's `actor.chose`, `actor.passed` and `actor.reasked` lines keep their names, and their keys in their order.

**Settled before the build**, the steward's calls: the plan's type lives in `Roles/`; four walks into one means one choice-by-choice loop with one pass vocabulary, the reviewer keeping its order by maker and its held-agents rule as its own rule over that loop, in part ii; the re-ask extends `OneTurnReask.cs`; the Loop walks' sentences stay their words, so the codes made one change no sentence. **Readings taken:** (1) the account walk is asked by the entry's agent name, as the quest's walk asked it; the intake's asked by the resolved adapter's name, which the roster resolves alike, so the call is the same; (2) the intake's gate is none, an agent this machine lacks being the plan's own pass; (3) `RoleOf` spells the works as the participant's work codes spell them, since Roles sits below Participants, and `StartPlanTests` holds the two alike; (4) `StartPasses.Codes` is the Loop walks' seven, and the reviewer's own codes (`not-installed`, `pin-not-installed`, `refused`) are part ii's to read onto them; (5) the pass memory and the machine log are the caller's, and what a line names beside the role may be none, so the chat's own memory and its lines, which name no subject, fit as they are.

**Proof**, each seen failing first: `StartPlanTests` (9 cases) and the comparison's case in `OneTurnReaskTests` as compile reds; then, with the plan built, its account walks forced to count failed four (a probe's count, an order of one's, the spread, the re-ask's), and a second counted walk at the taken entry failed four (one reservation per start, the re-ask's, the spread, the gate's walk count); `BuildWalkTests`' new case, a quest's start answering Build's settled selection with one reservation and its re-ask taking none, as a compile red against `BuildWalk`. **Ran:** `StartPlanTests` 9, `BuildWalkTests` 11, `OneTurnReaskTests` 7, `ActorChoicesTests` 31, `IntakeRoutingTests` 3, `IntakeKindTests` 7, `HelperLookTests` 79, `MachineLogTests` 29, `AccountRotationGoalTests` 35, `RolesBoundaryTests` 5, `GitSeedTests` 10, `ShortWaitsCensusTests` 3 and `StructureTests` 55, and the two Process classes over the moved paths, `IntakeTests` 41 and `ModelRefusalTickTests` 3, all green. **For parts ii and iii:** part ii puts the reviewer's walk, and the helper's and the partner's through it, on the plan's loop, its order by maker and its held-agents rule its own over the loop and its codes read onto `StartPasses`, `OneTurnReask` unchanged; part iii puts the chat's walk and its re-ask on the plan once FOUNDATION1d-ii part 4 lands, its gate `unavailable` for an agent that holds no conversation and Ask Daoris's `no-posture`, its kind `Conversation`; the plan asks no chosen account and takes an order of one by today's quest walk, so part iii adds the conversation's picked account and its walk of a one-entry order as the plan's options. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (row 1d-ii part 4 of 5 built: the conversation sites open with the moving open, Part B).** Built in the driver; part 5 adopts the driven carry-on and the refused open. **The open point:** `ChatRunner.StartAsync` takes `moves` (`IChatRunning` with it) and, where it is given and the start is no replacement for a refused one, opens through `ServiceClient.OpenChatMovingAsync`, which sends the chat's one body (`ChatOpenBody`, shared with `OpenChatAsync`) through `SessionOpenDoors.OpenAsync` under a key of its own (`chat-move/<guid>`), the record left `moves.from`, and tells `Opened`'s watchers as the unkeyed door does; what moved comes back on `ChatStart.Moves` and `ChatStart.Moved`. Only the sites below pass `moves`; every other caller of `StartAsync`, the hand-over and the curve among them (rows f-v and h), opens as before, and Ask Daoris's room opens through its own door, unkeyed. **The sites:** the briefed move and its not-offered path name the person's words and read every waiting id; start-from names its record as the moves' source, its new record having no `from`; the live limit move names the words its turns held and reads those alone, so every word of the person's kept since, one said after the old part's end among them, moves; the ended limit move names the box's words and reads every waiting id. Where the open moved them (`MovedWords.Moving`: `Moves`, or a transfer answered), each site composes its delivery from the destination's moved words (`MovedWords.OnAsync`, by `MovedWord.Id` in the transfer's order, never the old record's ids): each word's files copied first from the record left's folder into the destination's by their kept names (`ChatFiles.Moved`: a name the destination keeps already passed over, every read through `AtomicFile`), then marked attempted on the destination where this runner holds the part (`AttemptedAsync`, one the mark does not name left waiting there for the destination's own go-on), handed, and taken off the destination by their ids once handed, judged by what it holds after, never the take's answer; the old record says where they went by the ids it held. Where the first hand fails, nothing is attempted and the words wait on the destination, which the sentences now say (*so they wait on `c2`*) where they said the words stay on the old record. Against a host whose mark is below 2, or that keeps none, each site reads, hands and takes the old record's words as before, and the ended move keeps LIMITWORD1's second read. **The display** (O6-F2, rule 5): each moved word is one `User` event under its id with its author and files, as `ResumeAsk.Opening` records a go-on's (`MovedWords.Of`), recorded by the door before it writes: `ChatContext.Shown` holds the events for the message whose words they are, as `Browsers` holds a browser's line, and the protocol and native doors (`ProtocolChat.Shown`, `NativeChat.Shown`) and a text door's hand (`ChatHandOver.SayHandedOver`, which also tells the message's `Delivered` once it sent) record them in place of the one joined event (`ChatRecorded.Recorded`'s `shown`). The refused turns' words, which wait on no record and carry no id, stay one event. `ChatMessage` is Course/Domain's, held by another row in flight, so the display is held beside the message rather than on it; a field on it would replace `Shown`. **The ended limit move** (O6-F13): its box gains `moved`, written only when true, so an older build reads the box as before; with it, `Said` names the moved words on the box's `To`. The body writes the box with every moved id before it reads the destination, then narrows it to the words marked attempted for the hand; `DeliveredAsync` takes those off the destination and ends the box only once the destination holds none of them, never asking the record left, whose take of words it does not hold would answer success. **The interim recovery of a moved box**, until row f-vi's durable `sent`, taken up on what the destination holds and never on its events, since each moved word's event is recorded before the write: none of the box's words waiting there, the box goes; any waiting marked attempted, the hand began and may have reached the agent, so nothing is delivered or said again, each such word is taken back off the destination with one `lost` line naming them there whatever its conversation shows (O7-F4), and the box goes once none waits; all waiting never attempted, the hand never began, so the move is said once from the box at the destination's head under `limit-move/<session>`, the moved words leaving the destination into it, the word taken back where they cannot leave, and the box goes; a box that moved no words is read as before. So a shell cut off after the display and before the write, come up twice, neither declares the delivery nor says a joined copy again. **The census:** each `hands` row of `course-sites.json` says `moving` (`moves` for the five sites above, `part 5`, `unkeyed` for the hand-over and the curve, `no move` for a conversation's own go-on and start-with), and `box` names the ended move's `MoveEndedAsync`, `DeliveredAsync` and `TakeUpBoxesAsync`; `CourseCensusTests` holds a `moves` site to asking an open to move its words and reading the destination's. **Proof**, each seen failing first: the start-from and files cases as compile reds; the briefed move's failed-hand, delivered and mark-1 cases at run time on the old move; the six limit cases, the two box come-ups and the four delivered ones, at run time against the previous `LimitMove`; the native door's case with its display unwired; the census case against the unmarked rows. `StartFromTests` (24, 5 new), `MovedWordsTests` (4, new), `BriefedMoveTests` (13, 4 new, Process), `LimitWaitsTests` (4, new, Process), `CourseCensusTests` (4, 1 new); `ChatGoOnTests` (25), whose `ChatLedger` answers a protocol mark only where set (`Opens`), keyed opens with their receipts and the move, and takes that leave the words (`Keeps`), `ContinuedReadersCensusTests`, `SessionOpenDoorsTests`, `StructureTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests`, `ReadShareConversationTests`, `WorkplaceCoursePortsTests`, `SettledReadersCensusTests`, `GoOnBriefedTests` and `DriverCommandTests` green. **Not run, the parent's:** `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests`, `ChatGoesOnProcessTests` and `LimitGoesOnProcessTests`; their `ChatLedger` keeps no mark, so each runs the older host's path and no assertion of theirs moves. **Readings taken:** (1) the moved words are marked attempted at the site, as the hand is made, rather than at the door's write, so a site whose part is not held marks nothing, and a shell cut off between the mark and the write leaves them for the come-up's uncertain take-back, the conservative side of *never sent twice*; (2) a moved box's never-attempted words are folded into the come-up's one keyed copy and taken off the destination, so they go once and after the brief; (3) the box ends on what the destination holds after the take. **Part 5 builds on:** `ChatStart.Moved`, `MovedWords`, `ChatContext.Shown`, the `moved` box and the census's `part 5` rows; a refused start's replacement still opens unkeyed, so a moved part whose agent refuses it at the open hands its message to the replacement, which shows the words as one event, until part 5. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-i part 2: the take-up of dead runs' moves, the box importers and `move.quiet`, read by nothing).** Row f-i's second part of three; nothing production calls what it adds, so Daoris works as before, and part 3 is the moved words' `sent` and a hold closed `resolved`. **The take-up** is one unit, `Course/MoveTakeUp`, that an executor calls at its come-up and at each look, in the design's order. First the imports of any box an older build wrote, each its importer's: held until the start's confirmation is settled and asked again at the next look, a record whose box is still to be imported being `Never` to the take-up and to the caller's sweep meanwhile. Then every standing move of the unit's scope whose claim's run is dead by the store's liveness, the lease's own test: a shell's scope is every conversation move, the lease holder's look's every driven one, a `--share` loop's the driven moves of the lines it names. Each is taken by part 1's guarded update under the generation the look read, so of two takers the second is refused `moved` and nothing is taken twice; a live run's move, the taker's own, one whose run the store holds no row of and one at a phase a newer build wrote are kept. A taken move whose record left or destination the caller's ledger says no longer exists closes `gone`; one whose effect was marked and has no outcome is held `unrecorded` (`MoveHoldWhy`) and never marked again; any other is the caller's to go on from its phase. Last the caller's own sweep, handed what the look found (`MoveLook`). **A hung executor** keeps its moves: a live run's move at `opening`, `opened`, `copied`, `verified` or `delivering` whose phase moment is ten minutes old is said once, the note `move.quiet` with its minutes on the record left's line and a `move.quiet` line in the machine log naming the move, its phase and the run's kind and pid. The once is the store's: `MoveStore.SayQuiet` is one guarded update of the move as read that writes the phase moment it was said for, so of every process looking exactly one says it and the others write nothing, a look that reads it said asking no write at all, and a later phase that stands as long is a stretch of its own. `quiescing` waits on the old part's own turn and a hold on the person, and `old-ended` has begun nothing, so none of them is said. **Two shells:** a shell's come-up records its run (`DesktopStore.Enter`, a transaction that writes the run's row and nothing else) and says once for its run, through the channel its caller hands, that another live shell runs on its home, with its process and since when, and logs `home.another-shell`; it refuses nothing.

**The importers**, `Course/MoveBoxImports`: `LimitBoxImport` and `HandOverBoxImport`, called by no production site until rows f-v and f-vi, since an import sets a box aside from today's readers, which still own it. Each reads a box's bytes once and its move from those bytes (`LimitBoxes.Parse` and `HandOverFiles.ParseBox`, each reader's one parse taken out of its read), writes it as the store's own row claimed by the importing run at generation 1 with the import's receipt in one transaction (`MoveStore.Import`), and only then moves the box to `<box>.imported`, and only while its bytes are still those read; a box rewritten meanwhile is the next pass's. A box whose very bytes a receipt holds is only set aside. A box whose record has a standing move waits behind it, left in place, and is imported once that move ends; there are never two records of one move. A box that does not read is left as evidence and holds no record. A limit box is an ended record's brief at `old-ended`, or at `opened` with its destination and the words it said in order under `limit-move/<s>/<word>` once it names its new part, its recipe's bound its `before` and its refused turns' bound its `through`; its why is the one its message is composed by, `limit` where a limit held the record within `through` (the caller's reading), else `retry` for the box's `account`, else `resume`. A hand-over box is a hand-over decided at the moment it was asked, to its agent and account, by its why and its asker. **Store step 5** adds three nullable columns to `moves`: `agent`, a hand-over's; `refused_bound`, which part 1's reading 3 left to a delivering row and the limit box's import needs now; and `quiet_at`, the telling's. A step-4 build names its own columns, so the stamp stays at 1 and the step runs at the first start, and a read of a store a step-4 build left reads the three as empty.

**Readings taken:** (1) only a box held for the confirmation, or by a store that would not take it, holds its record from the take-up; a box behind a standing move does not, so a dead run's move and an older build's box of one record never wait on each other, the move taken up first and the box imported once it ends; (2) an import claims its move under the importing run, a live executor, which rows f-v and f-vi then carry on with; (3) an imported move has no `opens` key, its open, where one was made, an older build's unkeyed one; (4) `move.quiet` is said on the record left's line, for the saying process's own run's moves too, since a hung executor's own look still runs; (5) the other shell is said once per run, so a second come-up of one process says nothing. **Left to the rows that call them:** a set-aside box goes with its record's delete only once `SessionHomeFiles`' inventory names `<box>.imported` (outside this part's lane), and the limit importer's reading of a limit is `LimitHeld` over the record's events, both f-v's and f-vi's to hand when they first import.

**Proof**, each seen failing first: the store's cases as a compile red (`DesktopStore.Enter` missing) and the columns' case at run time; the importers' and the take-up's against the code with the importer's pass emptied, the take-up's loop emptied and the other shell's saying cut (24 of 28 failed, the four left asserting that nothing is taken, said or imported); then three mutants each caught by its own case (the quiet's once-guard dropped, the set-aside's byte check dropped, a waiting box's record taken up); the note code's with the English catalogue's entry changed; the web's case with the page's map entry missing. `MoveBoxImportsTests` (15), `MoveTakeUpTests` (14), `DesktopStoreTests` (4 new), `NoteCodesTests` (1 new), `MoveStoreCensusTests` (1 new, and part 1's census over the two new files), the web's `note.test.ts` (1 new). **The process proof**, `MoveClaimProcessTests` (`Category.Process`; it builds a child program once into `_fixtures/`, referencing the driver library under test and the store's packages at the desktop's pins): two shells on one home deciding one line at once, one row and one claim and the second said once; an executor paused after its mark commits and before its effect, another process's take refused and its look taking nothing, `move.quiet` said once past ten minutes and never taken over, then the executor killed and its move taken up and held at the next look, the effect never made. It was written after the code and so seen red only by its own first run, which hung reading a running child's error stream; run once since with `process.runsettings`, both cases pass in under a minute with the child's build, and it is the parent's in the full set.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-i part 3: the moved words' `sent` and a hold closed `resolved`, read by nothing).** Row f-i's last part; nothing production calls what it adds, so Daoris works as before, and with it row f-i is built. **`sent`** is each moved word's delivery evidence, a disposition in part 1's `move_words` column, so no store step was needed. `MoveStore.Sent` writes it in one transaction under the claim's check, at `delivering` alone, for each word the write carried, with the outcome `sent` of the effect last marked where that has none, since the write returning is that effect's outcome; a word already sent is unchanged, and a transfer the move did not make or a word disposed otherwise is refused with nothing written. `delivered` is refused inside its own transaction while any word the move moved lacks `sent`. `Course/MoveDelivery` is the order an executor keeps as its door's write returns: `Wrote` (that transaction, false where it failed), then the take of the written words off the destination, made whether or not `sent` was written since the turn took them, then the reading. **The one reading** (`ReadAsync`, under the claim) is the same at the take's return and at a come-up's take-up, at `opened`, `copied`, `verified` and `delivering`, and reads `sent` and the destination's `said` alone, through a port (`IMoveSaid`) that carries no settlement and no session event: a word with `sent` is taken, taken off where it still waits and the destination read again; one gone without it is *left*, whichever door took it, a `sent` whose transaction failed among them; one waiting, attempted and without it, is uncertain; one waiting never attempted is still to deliver. The move closes `delivered` at `delivering` once every word has `sent` and none waits, a move of no word once its effect's outcome is recorded. It is held where a word is *left* or uncertain (the new reason `undelivered`) or an effect it marked has no outcome (`unrecorded`), the hold naming them; its uncertain words are then settled with the `lost` note and the one rule read. Otherwise the caller goes on, and a destination that cannot be read is read again later, nothing written. **The dispositions** are `sent`, `left`, `uncertain`, and `held`, a word a hold waits on for the person (§19.3's hold, reason `superseded`, f-ii's to write); `MoveStore.Hold` takes the words it names with their dispositions in its own transaction, never a word sent.

**`resolved`** is `Course/MoveResolve`, §3.3's one rule. A hold names its move's words disposed *left*, uncertain or held, and its begun effect; it closes `resolved` once none of those words waits on its destination's `said`. A begun effect alone keeps no hold, so a hold naming no word, or with no destination, closes at its first read without asking the destination. The executor reads it under its claim (`ReadAsync`, which first settles a named uncertain word still waiting) at each come-up and look, where the take-up reads every hold of its scope, its own and a dead run's once taken, a live run's being its own executor's; and wherever the course reads a record's standing move as a precondition (`StandingAsync`: the move whose open made the record, else the one it left, a hold under this run's claim read first and another run's answered as standing). **The press's close**, settled: the person's press through one of a hold's doors (a take-back at its destination, a word said at the head) reads the same rule and closes the hold without its claim (`PressedAsync`, through `MoveStore.PressResolved`, one update guarded by the phase `held` that leaves the claim as it was). §3.3 names the press beside the executor's reads and puts the claim on the executor's alone; the press is made in the window, while a hold's claim may be a host's live run or a dead one; and a held move takes no effect, no mark and no phase but its close, so a close by another process interleaves with nothing a claim fences, and the executor's own close after it is a phase written again. Part 1's `Close` still requires the claim. **The destination's standing move:** `MoveStore.StandingTo` finds the move whose open made a record, and `Decide` and `Import` answer that move for a decision of the record, nothing written, under the write lock as the index answers the record left's: the store's half of the precondition that a destination takes no move of its own while its move stands. `MoveTakeUp` takes the port as `said` (none reads no delivery and closes only holds naming no word), and `MoveLook` gains `Delivered` and `Resolved`. `Course/MoveSaid`'s `MoveSaidDoor` is the service's door: a record's waiting words and their attempt marks, the taken door, and the take-back door with the `lost` note written whatever the conversation shows (round 8, *O7-F4*).

**Readings taken:** (1) a hold names its words by disposition, so the rule needs no column, and a word sent is taken and never named; (2) `sent` records the effect's outcome, so a crash between `sent` and the take reads the write as returned, and a move of no word has its evidence; (3) a word the attempt did not name waits never attempted and no hold names it, so once the hold closes it goes on by its destination's own go-on, which sends only words never attempted; (4) the take-up holds and resolves in one look where nothing named waits, `MoveLook.Held` keeping the move as held for the caller's note; (5) the head's read before delivery (§13.4 step 5) and the `superseded` hold's naming are f-ii's, the reason and the `held` disposition here for it; (6) the row's live `--share` case, which part 2's process proof left out, is added here with a really killed deliverer, so the three live cases §13.4 names are all proven with processes.

**Proof**, each seen failing first: the new types as compile reds; then, against stubs of the new methods, the 31 new cases at run time; then five mutants each caught by its own cases (the take before `sent`, `delivered` without the `sent` check, the destination's decision unrefused, a begun effect keeping its hold, the press closing without the rule). `MoveStoreTests` (29, 5 new), `MoveDeliveryTests` (12, new: `sent` before the take and `delivered` after it, `delivered` only once every word has `sent`, a failed `sent` read *left*, a crash at `delivering` read by `sent` alone with *left* and uncertain words named, §13.5's `delivering` and `opened` rows, a destination that cannot be read, a move of no word), `MoveResolveTests` (9, new: the press, the superseded-then-second-move and resolved-then-second-move cases, the course's read with no look, a begun effect alone, another run's hold, an uncertain word settled at the next read, §13.5's `held` row), `MoveSaidDoorTests` (3, new), `MoveTakeUpTests` (16, 2 new: a taken move at `delivering` closed delivered or held; holds read at the come-up and the look), `MoveStoreCensusTests` (3, over the three new files) and `MoveBoxImportsTests` (15). **The process proof**, `MoveClaimProcessTests` (4, 2 new, `Category.Process`): the instance and a `--share` loop as two live child processes, each look taking nothing of the other's, then the instance killed, the share loop taking nothing and the next lease holder's look taking its move; and a deliverer killed after its `sent` committed and before its take, the next look taking it up, taking its word off once and closing it delivered. Both new cases were seen failing against the child with each rule broken (the share loop's scope widened, `sent` never written), then passing, with `process.runsettings`. **Ran:** the fast classes with `StructureTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests`, `CourseCensusTests`, `CourseVocabularyTests`, `WorkplaceMoveRecordsPortsTests` and `DesktopStoreTests`, 212 green (the process class's two earlier cases among them, its name within the filter), and `MoveClaimProcessTests` (4) green.

**Rows f-ii to f-vi and h build on:** `MoveDelivery.Wrote` at each door's write return (the ACP prompt's `OnWire`, the native start's return, a driven protocol part's first prompt), then their own take, then `ReadAsync`, or `WroteAsync` where a door has no take of its own; `ReadAsync` as the one reading of their own crash; `MoveStore.Hold(…, MoveHoldWhy.Superseded, …)` naming the destination's waiting words `held` after the head's read; the hold's note said from `MoveLook.Held` or `MoveDelivered`; `MoveResolve.StandingAsync` wherever the course reads a record's standing move (h's limit-move branch, i's driven line) and `PressedAsync` after the person's press through a hold's door; and `MoveTakeUp` handed `new MoveSaidDoor(service, events)`. Each adoption moves `MoveStoreCensusTests`. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1j part ii of 3: the reviewer's walk on the one start plan, and a continuation on its record's role and asked choice, Part B).** Part ii moves the second opinion's, the helper's and the partner's walk onto the plan, and the driven continuations onto their record's choice; the second is what a person sees change. **One loop:** `StartPlan.LoopAsync` is the plan's one loop, which its own walk and the reviewer's both run: the entries in the order a walk gives, each tried once, the first that runs taken, each other passed in order in `StartPasses`' codes and said once; a walk's own rule over it says which entries an earlier pass holds.
