Each entry's accounts are the plan's one ask of D197's walk (`StartPlan.AccountsAsync`), which counts the start where it chooses an account and the start counts, so a start holds exactly the one reservation of its taken entry, a passed entry's walk counting nothing. The plan takes a fence (a rule's or an ask's agents, each expanded into the role's entries for it) or the entries its caller holds. **The reviewer:** `ReviewerChoice.ChooseAsync` builds the plan of its actor's work (`review`, `help`, `partner`) for the repository's workspace and walks it under its own rule, kept in `ReviewerChoice`: its order by maker (another maker's agent, then a maker not declared, then the work's own family) and its held agents (an agent held whole, by an install, its pin, a cool-off or a sign-in, holds its later entries, which are neither asked nor passed; a model refused is the entry's alone). Its own codes stay on each try, for its sentence and its `ReviewerUnavailable` code, so every sentence, label and gate code reads as before; its passes are said in the plan's vocabulary (`ReviewerHeld.Pass`: `not-installed` and `pin-not-installed` are `unavailable`, `refused` is `held`, the rest by their own names), which `OneTurnReask.Say` reads (`IActorChoice.Passes`) for the opening note's actor line and the machine log's `actor.chose`, the words of the line unchanged. `ReviewerChoice.Settled` answers the plan's settled selection. `StartPasses.Of` reads an agent absent, or absent at its pin, as `unavailable`, so the quest's and the intake's walks name the same facts as the reviewer's does. No caller of the reviewer's walk is a probe, and none walks again after it but the one-turn re-ask, unchanged. **The continuations** (the design §2.4's first row, §7 row 5): `StartPlan.ContinuedOn` is the agent a continuation goes on with, the one its record's choice was asked of, else the agent the record opened on, else its role's first entry, the role the record's `actor` or, where it names none this build knows, the work's seeded role; a role whose entry does not read, or that is off, holds the start in its sentence. A quest's start that goes on from a record (a carry-on, a go-on with the person's words, a resume on an answer) and that no hand-over named starts on it, never on the machine's `adapter` key, and its target keeps the record's choice, so a part carried on from Build's second choice runs on that choice's agent with its model and effort and its record says choice 2; the person's words to such a record go on in its own conversation (`ResumesOwn`, now against the record's agent). A handed quest keeps its named agent (D205's PRESSMODEL1 note), and a line an account's limit cut off keeps D206 point 6's walk of Build's order. Each hold names the agent its start asked (`StartRun.Agent`), which the look's report of a wait names (§2.4's second row) where the start began on the machine's agent. The same rule answers the listing's fresh plan (`SessionGroups.Waiting`, a hand-over's agent first, then the record's) and a reviewer's same-agent press on a record that names no agent it ran on (`OpinionGateLook.Reviewers`). **A brainstorm voice** (§2.4's table, D205's round-2 note): each pass is asked for the workspace of the repository its brainstorm asks about (`BrainstormTurn.Workspace`), so it runs the version that workspace pins; a voice's plan (`DriverBrainstorms.Voice`) is Brainstorm's with its own entry alone, so a voice that cannot run is dropped by the brainstorm as before and has nothing to be replaced by. **Left, re-cited by grep for `config.Adapter` and `Adapter ??`:** part iii's, the chat walk's, `Course/Chat/ChatGoOn.cs` (`record.Adapter ?? config.Adapter`, `:569`), `Course/Chat/BriefedMove.cs` (`:113`) and the Modules' `ConversationRoutes.cs` (Chat's order, then `config.Adapter`, `:51`, `:173`); the look's report before a first start's walk (`Driver.cs`'s `StartBegun`, which names the machine's agent until the start's hold names the walk's); and the helper's own policy and its *No second opinion* sentence (§2.4's helper row), which no part of this row's three builds.

**Readings taken:** (1) the reviewer's held agents are its own rule over the loop, a predicate over its own record of each entry, so its families and its labels never reach Roles; (2) a pin not installed is `unavailable`, as D205 point 4 names an agent that cannot run here at all, and the plan's own walk reads it so too; (3) the record's role is its `actor` until a binding keeps the role (§2.7, row x), and the agent its choice was asked of is read before the agent it opened on, as the PRESSMODEL1 note reads it; (4) a continuation keeps its record's `choice` beside its `asked`, so the head says the choice the part runs as; (5) the look's wait names the agent the start asked from the start's own hold, the report before the walk unchanged. **Proof**, each seen failing first: `StartPlanTests`' four new cases, `ReviewerPlanTests` (9), `ContinuedChoiceTests` (2), `BrainstormVoicePlanTests` (2), the new cases of `SessionWordsWaitingTests` and `BrainstormLookTests` as compile reds; then at run time, against the code before each change, the carry-on held on the first agent's cool-off (`ContinuedChoiceTests`), an absent pin passed `held` (`StartPlanTests`), the listing holding nothing for a record on another agent than the machine's (`SessionWordsWaitingTests`), and `ContinuedChoiceTickTests` (`Category.Process`, new: real ticks on the protocol stand-in) holding the carry-on on the stub's cool-off. **Ran**, all green: `StartPlanTests` 13, `ReviewerPlanTests` 9, `ReviewerChoiceTests` 52 unchanged, `ContinuedChoiceTests` 2, `SessionWordsWaitingTests` 13, `BrainstormLookTests` 14, `BrainstormVoicePlanTests` 2, `RolesBoundaryTests` 5, `OneTurnReaskTests` 7, `BuildWalkTests` 11, `HelperLookTests` 79, `PartnerLookTests` 11, `OpinionGateTests` 40, `OpinionDeliveryTests` 37, `OpinionRecheckTests` 7, `OpinionWritersTests` 6, `OpinionPacketTests` 30, `OwnSpaceTests` 28, `PartnerJudgeTests` 8, `PartnerMeasurementsTests` 3, `ActorChoicesTests` 31, `ActorOpeningTests` 8, `IntakeRoutingTests` 3, `IntakeKindTests` 7, `AccountLimitHoldTests` 24, `AccountRotationHoldTests` 2, `AccountRotationGoalTests` 35, `SignedOutWaitTests` 7, `SignInRefusedStartTests` 30, `HandOverLookTests` 13, `HandOverAskLookTests` 11, `SessionGroupsTests` 156, `BrainstormBoundsTests` 14, `MeetingTests` 16, `HelpPlaceWorkspaceTests` 13, `MachineLogTests` 29, `StructureTests` 55, `GitSeedTests` 10, `ShortWaitsCensusTests` 3, `LineCourseTests` 8 and `CourseCensusTests` 4, and `ContinuedChoiceTickTests` 1 with `process.runsettings`. **Not run, the parent's:** the Process classes that read these walks' words, `HelperProcessTests`, `BrainstormProcessTests`, `ChatPendingProcessTests`, `PartnerProcessTests` and `ReviewTreeTests`, and the carry-on's ticks `WalkAgentTickTests` and `PressModelTickTests`. **For part iii:** the chat's walk and its re-ask go on the plan's loop as the reviewer's did, through `LoopAsync` and `AccountsAsync`, its gate the plan's `Gate`; the chat's continuations (`ChatGoOn`, `BriefedMove`, the start route) read `StartPlan.ContinuedOn` with the work `chat`. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-ii part a of 2: the live limit move keeps its move record, and the shell takes up a dead shell's conversation moves).** The first row whose move writes f-i's record; part b adds the crash-point and downgrade process proofs. **The holder** is `Course/ConversationMoves`: the store under the process's run, the service's door to a destination's `said` (`MoveSaidDoor`), the delivery's one reading, a hold's one rule and a `MoveTakeUp` of the conversations' scope, its `move.quiet` said on the record left's console and kept in its events as a conversation's driver's line is. **The live move** (`LimitMove.MoveAsync`) decides its move once it holds the conversation's going-on and before it holds its turns: executor `conversation`, how `brief`, why `limit`, by `daoris`, the brief's recipe `limit` bound before the refused turns (`LimitHeld.Before`), the refused turns' bound the events the decision read, and the account the walk settled where the move stays on its agent. The record's standing move is read first as the course reads it: this run's hold that names nothing waiting closes `resolved`, and this run's move past its write (its prompt's outcome recorded) is read by its delivery's reading and closes `delivered`, since a new part its limit refuses at its first turn is judged as that turn ends, which can come before its move's own reading. A move still standing for the record, the one it left or the one whose open made it, means no move now, nothing written and the turns untouched; a store that will not take the decision moves nothing and says so in the limit's not-moved line. Then: `old-ended` once the old part ends; `opening` under a key drawn before the open and handed to it (`MoveWords.Opens`, which `OpenChatMovingAsync` sends in place of its own), so the stored key is the one sent; `opened` with every word the open moved by `MovedWord.Id`, read whatever `Moves` says; `delivering` and the prompt's mark before any moved word is marked attempted or handed; at the write's return (`ChatMessage.Delivered`, which `ChatTurns` fires after `Sent()` and before its take-off, and the text door after its send) `MoveDelivery.Wrote` for the handed words' transfers, then their take off the new part, then the reading, which closes the move `delivered` once every word it moved has `sent`. A move of no word, an older host's or one whose words were taken back meanwhile, records its prompt's outcome `sent` at the write and closes on it; the text door, which tells no message of no moved word that it wrote, records it as its hand returns. A word the new part holds that the attempt did not name is read as still to deliver, and the move is held `undelivered` naming none and closes, the word left for the new part's own go-on. **A write the store refuses** stops the move before what it fences, with today's words for that stop: before the open, nothing opens and the words stay on the old record; after it, no hand is made and the new part says Daoris's words did not reach it. A move that goes no further is ended by its phase in the move's own `finally`: before the open, `fell-back` with the code `not-opened`; with its open asked and no record answered, held `unrecorded` and closed at once, naming nothing; past the open with its hand refused, its prompt's outcome `unsent`, held `undelivered` naming no word, since its executor knows none was written, and closed, its attempted words left to the come-up's settlement as before. The turns not held, a word having started a turn first, closes the move `superseded`.

**The take-up's places.** The shell's come-up runs the conversations' take-up first in `ChatGoOn.TakeUpAsync`, before the limit boxes' take-up and the go-ons; the shell's look runs it in the limits stage before the limits' wake (`DriverLoop`'s `Limits`), the shell's one existing periodic place for its conversations, beside each look of the watch, so no new timer was made. A shell beside a headless lease holder builds no watch and so runs no look, as round 7's *O7-F11* already reads: its moves are read at its come-up and wherever the course reads a record's standing move. A dead run's move the take-up leaves to its caller is ended by its phase, nothing sent: `decided`, `quiescing` or `old-ended` falls back `not-opened`, its line going on from its old part by its own come-up; `opening` is held `unrecorded` and closes, naming nothing; from `opened` to `delivering` it is the delivery's reading, a move whose words wait never attempted held `undelivered` naming none and closed so its destination's own go-on carries them. A destination that cannot be read now is kept, in the process, for the next look's reading. **Readings taken:** (1) the decision comes before the turns' hold, so a refused decision never takes the words a limit's hold moves out of the queue; (2) `move_words.word` is the word's id on the destination, which d-ii part 3 keeps equal to its id on the record left; (3) Ask Daoris's room opens through its own unkeyed door, so its move's `opens` names a key that door never sends, as an older host's does; (4) the take-up does not ask a lost open again under its key: a move at `opening` closes held and its records go on by their own come-up, as an older build's would, which part b's crash at that phase proves; (5) the other shell is said in the machine log alone at the come-up, since the course holds no channel to the window and the chat runner's door to one was another branch's in flight; (6) the census names the moves that keep their record (`course-sites.json`'s `records`, `CourseCensusTests`) and the store's readers (`MoveStoreCensusTests`: the holder, the live move's decision and the delete's wiring, and the come-up's and the look's order).

**Proof**, each seen failing first: `LimitMoveStoreTests` (11 cases, new) as compile reds; then, with the holder built and the move unwired, the five live and come-up cases at run time; the case of this run's move past its write at run time before the decision read it; and a mutant writing `sent` after the take caught by the order case. The live move decided, `opening` under the key the open is sent, `opened`, marked, `sent`, taken and `delivered` in that order on one move; a move of no word closed on its prompt's outcome; a destination's own move answered as standing with nothing written; this run's resolvable hold closed first; this run's move past its write read first; the conversations' take-up taking a dead run's move once at each phase before delivery and ending it by its phase; `move.quiet` said once past ten minutes on a moved clock; the shell's come-up through the chat runner taking a dead shell's move under its own run. `CourseCensusTests` (5, 1 new) and `MoveStoreCensusTests` (4, 1 new, the first rewritten for its readers) were seen failing on the unnamed records and the unwired look. **Ran**, green: `LimitMoveStoreTests` 11, `MoveStoreTests` 29, `MoveTakeUpTests` 16, `MoveDeliveryTests` 12, `MoveResolveTests` 9, `MoveSaidDoorTests` 3, `MoveStoreCensusTests` 4, `CourseCensusTests` 5, `LineCourseTests` 8, `ChatGoOnTests` 25, `MovedWordsTests` 4, `SessionOpenDoorsTests` 28, `StartFromTests` 24, `StructureTests`, `GitSeedTests` and `ShortWaitsCensusTests` 68, and `LimitWaitsTests` 4 with `process.runsettings`. **Not run, the parent's:** `LimitGoesOnProcessTests`, `LimitGoesElsewhereProcessTests`, `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests` and `ChatGoesOnProcessTests`; their ledgers keep no `opens` mark, so each live move there is a move of no word closed `delivered` at its write, its store written in the case's home, and no assertion of theirs reads the record, the home's files or the open's key.

**For part b:** a shell killed at each phase of the live move (after the decision, after `old-ended`, after `opening` with and without the open made, after `opened`, after the mark and before the write, after the write and before `sent`, after `sent` and before the take, after the take and before `delivered`), its come-up ending the move as above and each moved word delivered once, uncertain and said, or carried by its destination's go-on, never sent twice; §13.5's table run with an older build's come-up at each phase, then this build's return; and §19.3's and §19.4's cases the row's cell names for ii. **Rows f-iii to f-v build on**, for a conversation's move: `ConversationMoves.BeginAsync` with the move's decision before anything is held or ended, a standing answer read as no move now; `ConversationMove`'s writes before what each fences, a refusal stopping the move there; `Wire` at the door's write return through the message's `Delivered`, then the move's own take, then `ReadAsync`; `EndAsync` in the move's `finally` where it went no further than its hand; and the come-up's and the look's take-up, which ends what it takes by phase and sends nothing. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1k part 1: the event journal and the `sessions` half of its triggers, read by nothing).** Row k is built in two parts, and this is the first; part 2 adds the `quest_log`, `asks` and channel triggers, the `journal` change family named only to a reader that asks for it, the `journal` mark, the older client's reader proof and the p95 measurement. Nothing reads the journal, so Daoris works as before. **The table** `event_journal` (`Sessions/EventJournal.cs`, created by the sessions schema beside `said_settled`) has §4.1's columns, `position` as an `AUTOINCREMENT` key, so a position is never reused after a trim or after the table is emptied, and one index on `(record, position)`. Only `position`, `at`, `record` and `change` are required, and the inserts always give those a value (the record's id is coalesced, since a `TEXT` primary key may hold a null). `at` is whole milliseconds from SQLite's own clock. It has one column the design's table does not list, `words_over` (the count past the 64th word, **positive for words added, negative for words taken**, since the design names the overflow row for adds only and a take of more than 64 words is as unbounded), and §4.1's `op`, `ask`, `message` and `line_code`, empty until part 2. **The triggers** on `sessions` journal the raw transition and nothing else: `opened` at an insert (the state, the work's marks as a closed list, the entry, the part it continues and why, its scope and quest); `state` when `OLD.state IS NOT NEW.state`, with the limit flag on either side and the codes of the note's parts as the statement wrote them (at most eight, never a value or text; a note that is not JSON codes none); `limited` when the flag moves while the state stays, so **a move that also limits is one row** carrying both, which departs from the pack's reading of one trigger per column writing a row each; `word-added` and `word-removed` for each word entering or leaving `said` by its id, in list order, with its author class (`person`, `daoris`, `agent`, `supervisor`, `helper`, `opinion`), level and whether it carries a key, and `words-overflow` past 64. An attempt mark rewrites `said` at its length and a same-state or note-only write changes none of the guarded columns, so they journal nothing; a mirror pass (`origin` set) journals nothing; and an answer still in the old `answer` column is not a word added when the list begins beside it. A list that is not a JSON array on either side, and a note that is not JSON, journal nothing for that part and never fail the statement. The inserting statement deletes the rows past the newest 50,000 by position, so an older build that never trims still bounds the table. **The store's identity** is written by the sessions schema (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, so a stamp an earlier build's observer wrote stands), and `ChangeStream.Identity` now only reads it and waits (its open fails and is tried again) where none is written yet. **Readings taken:** (1) the entry key is cut at 64 characters as an id, so a longer one is not whole in its row, the record holding it; (2) the `at` is milliseconds, not the seconds `said_settled` keeps; (3) a state row and its word rows written by one statement are in the engine's order among themselves, and in commit order against every other statement's. **Proof**, each seen failing first (14 `EventJournalTests` as missing-table reds, the change stream's identity case as a compile red): each move once, in order; same-state, note-only, attempt-mark and mirror writes journalling nothing; a limit with the state and alone; the words by author class with no text or key in any column; the old column's answer; an end, a reopen and an end, and a park, its answer and a park again, each coded from its own row after the record was overwritten; more than 64 words in one statement and in one take; a row bounded whatever the record holds; 50,000 rows kept by an older build's own statements, and positions past an emptied table; an older build's own insert, state move and whole-list saves minting rows without failing; malformed notes and lists never failing a statement; the identity written once and an existing stamp kept. **Left for part 2:** everything the row's cell names beyond these: the other three triggers, the `journal` family and its opt-in, the mark, the older client's reader over a journal-only append, and the install's p95 and typical-size measurements. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (row 1d-ii part 5 of 5 built: the driven carry-on and the refused open on the moving open, Part B; row 1d-ii is complete).** Built in the driver; with parts 1 to 4, every site the census names that opens a part and hands it words opens through the moving open. **The driven carry-on** (`Loop/Driver.cs` at its open, `Loop/DriverContinuations.cs`): a start that hands its new session words, a fallback's from the record whose resume fell back (as the fallback read it again) or a resume's from the session that asked (SAYHELD1, SESSWATCH1a), opens through `ServiceClient.OpenSessionMovingAsync`: the unkeyed door's one body (`SessionOpenBody`) under a key of its own (`drive-move/<guid>`), the record left the words' record, `moves` naming the words it hands (`DriverContinuations.Hands`: the person's; a resume's, Daoris's too; the roster's switch, `NewPartCarries`) and reading every id it saw waiting there; the open's watchers are told what the record names (`DestinationOpen.Recorded`). A first start, and a carry-on that hands no words, open unkeyed as before. Where the open moved them (`MovedWords.Moving(DestinationOpen)`), `HandedOnAsync` delivers from the new session (`HandedMovedAsync`): read there in the transfer's order, each word's files copied first from the record left (`MovedWords.OnAsync`); each shown there by its own `User` event under its id before its run is written, but a word the instruction hands by itself under its id (a resume's, the switch's Daoris words), which the run's opening shows already (`HarnessCapture.Opening`); the record left says where they went by the ids it held; then taken off the new session by their ids. That take is the delivery: the take door keeps the person's words that reopened the work on the ask, said to the new session, where `WithAskWordsAsync` reads them for its instruction, as today's take off the old record naming the new session kept them, so a word kept between the read and the open reaches the new agent exactly once, in its instruction, and no dispatcher of the new session's hands it again. The instruction's `Handed` is read from the moved words. Words the open did not name stay with the person on the record left, marked, as before (`StaysWithPerson`).

**The refused open's identity rule** (`Loop/Chat/ChatRunner.ReplaceAsync`, `Course/Chat/ChatPending`, `Loop/Chat/ChatTurns.TakeMoved`, the port `IChatTurns`): the replacement's open names the moving words the refused record keeps (`ChatPending.KeptIds`, named and read alike, so a word of the person's kept there since moves too) through `OpenChatMovingAsync`. Each word the open moved goes to the replacement's turns as that record's own word: its id the destination's, kept there, its files the destination's folder's, copied first, and none of the refused record's marks or take-off; `TakeMoved` hands it through the destination's own door, so it is marked attempted there before its turn is written and taken off there once on the wire, never off the refused record, and its id is known to the turns, so a producer's retry the destination answers with that word (`ChatTurns.KeepAsync`) is acknowledged with no second turn. It is shown by its own `User` event under its id, held by that id for the door that writes its turn (`ChatContext.ShownMoved`), since each moved word is a turn of its own and a person's turn is otherwise recorded without its id. `MovedTo`'s stripping (`Id`, `Kept`, `Attempting` and `Went` cleared, the take off the refused record naming the replacement once on the wire) is kept only for a word the open did not move: one held in this process alone, and every word against an older host. A word of the person's the open moved that no moving word held is handed after them. A producer's retry that joined the moving words under the key of a kept one is that word said again and goes nowhere (`ChatPending.Moved`), on any host. **Part 4's display settled:** a replacement owed takes the refused part's `ChatContext.Shown` entry at once and holds it for the replacement's turn of the same message, so a moved part refused at the open shows each moved word under its id on its replacement, and nothing stays held for a record that never wrote its message. **An older host:** at `opens` 1 the key goes with no words to move, with no marks no key goes; `Moves` is false and `Moved` empty, and both sites read, hand and take the old record's words as before. **The census:** no `part 5` row is left in `course-sites.json`: Driver.cs's carry-on open and ChatRunner.cs's replacement are `moves`; the driven go-on's resume (Driver.cs's `resumes`, DriverContinuations.cs's `go-on`), which goes on in its own record, is `no move`; `CourseCensusTests`' closed values drop `part 5`.

**The end-to-end case** (§10's proof: a late word to a closed quest's driven part reaches the conversation that went on from it): `LateWordServiceProcessTests` runs the service's own host (`RealServiceHost`) and the shell's chat runner on the protocol stand-in. A quest's driven part ends and its quest is declined, the person's word kept on it; the closed quest's part goes on by itself as a conversation where its own is lost (`ChatRunner.GoOnLostAsync`, part 4's briefed move), and a late word with a file is kept on the driven part as the conversation's keyed open is on its way. The open moves both; the agent is handed each once, the late word's file linked from the conversation's own folder; each is shown there under its id before the answer; the driven part says where they went; neither waits anywhere after; the late word's retry named to the driven part is answered settled.

**Proof**, each seen failing first: `ChatTurns.TakeMoved` and `ChatPending.KeptIds` as compile reds; then, with `KeptIds` empty and `TakeMoved` the plain take, at run time: `ChatTurnsTests`' new case (the retry under the moved word's key a second turn), `ChatPendingTests` (2, new: the kept ids none; the joined retry handed twice), `CourseCensusTests` against the adopted rows (Driver.cs asking no open to move its words), and the new Process cases on the base paths: `ChatPendingProcessTests`' moved word (never marked attempted on the replacement), its mark-1 case (no keyed open) and the moved part's replacement (one joined word with no id), and `SessionMessagesTickTests`' late word and mark-1 cases (no keyed open). Built, the moved word's turn was then seen shown with no id, which `ShownMoved` settled, and the driven case's display assertion over-read the opening's own events, narrowed to the moved words. The refused open's mark-0 case passes both ways: it is today's path, pinned. `LateWordServiceProcessTests`, which part 4's briefed move already passes, was seen failing with the driver's door sending no `moves` (`SessionOpenDoors.OpenAsync`), the late word left on the driven part, as was the driven late word case.

**Readings taken:** (1) only a carry-on that hands words opens keyed, so the keyed open's refusal of a record left that is not its line's head or is active (row d-i) reaches no carry-on that opens today without words; (2) the driven delivery is the take into the ask, not a turn of the new session: its moved words leave it before its instruction is composed, keeping today's reopened words and the instruction that reads them; (3) a replacement's open reads what it names, the pending start holding no other read, so every word of the person's kept on the refused record moves; (4) a moved word's display on a replacement is held by its id, a moved word being a turn of its own; (5) a retry joined under a kept word's key is dropped on any host, which an older host's path also handed twice; (6) the gated stand-in of `ChatPendingProcessTests` is its own helper (`GatedConfig`), since a fourth string beside the accounts was read as the gate by overload; and that class and `SessionMessagesTickTests` keep their scratch under `_fixtures/`, removed through `GitSeed.Discard`, where they used the system's temporary folder.

**Ran**, all green: `ChatTurnsTests` 51, `ChatPendingTests` 2, `CourseCensusTests` 4, `LineCourseTests` 8, `MovedWordsTests` 4, `ContinuationTests` 56, `DrivenInboxTests` 38, `ContinuedChoiceTests` 2, `StructureTests` 55, `GitSeedTests` 10, `ShortWaitsCensusTests` 3, `SessionOpenDoorsTests` 28, `ContinuedReadersCensusTests` 2, `SettledReadersCensusTests` 2, `StartFromTests` 24, `CoursePortsTests` 5, `WorkplaceCoursePortsTests` 7, `ChatGoOnTests` 25, `ReadShareConversationTests` 23 and `GoOnBriefedTests` 9; with `process.runsettings`, `ChatPendingProcessTests` 12, `LateWordServiceProcessTests` 1 and `SessionMessagesTickTests` (21, the class whole once; its carry-on cases again after the last change). **Not run, the parent's:** the driven tick suites whose stand-ins keep no marks, where a carry-on that hands words now opens through the keyed door, which reads `/api/protocol` first and, finding no mark there, sends the unkeyed body as before, so no assertion of theirs should move: `AnswerContinuesTickTests`, `AskAndWaitTickTests`, `LimitResumeTickTests`, `TransientRetryTickTests`, `OpinionResumeTickTests`, `ContinuedChoiceTickTests`, `WalkAgentTickTests` and `PressModelTickTests`; and `BriefedMoveTests` and `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, whose replacements open keyed only where their `ChatLedger` sets a mark. The say door's path is unchanged by this part; its p95 on the install stays the parent's to measure (part 2's note). **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1j part iii of 3: a conversation's go-on, a briefed move, a new chat and a terminal's start-from on the record's agent or Chat's order, Part B).** Part iii takes the last four `config.Adapter` fallbacks that part ii's note left, the design's §2.4 first and third rows; what a person sees change is that a Chat order they set is where their new chats, their start-froms and their old conversations run. **A conversation goes on on the agent whose conversation it is:** `ChatGoOn` and `BriefedMove` read the record's `adapter` first (its conversation is that agent's, so it goes on there or not at all, an agent no longer here holding it as before), and only a record naming none, one from before the adapter was kept, takes `StartPlan.ContinuedOn`'s answer for Chat (its asked choice, else Chat's first entry), never the machine's `adapter` key. `ContinuedOn` throws where Chat is off or its entry does not read: `ChatGoOn` catches it and holds the words with that sentence (`HeldWords.Program`, unmarked, so the next word tries again), `BriefedMove` refuses `agent-gone` with it, so neither faults, and the briefed move's refusal now names the agent it looked for where the record named none. A hand-over's named agent still wins, and its walk is the caller's. **A new chat takes `ActorChoices.NewChat`**, the one answer of the page's `START_CHAT`, its `SESSION_START_FROM` and the terminal's `chat` and `sessions start-from`: a named agent is the person's pick alone, its model Chat's first entry for it (D205 §3.5(d)); otherwise Chat's order, whose first agent the start asks and whose whole the runner walks. The terminal passed no order before, so its start-from, and its `chat` without `--adapter`, ran on the machine's key and walked nothing; both now hand the runner Chat's order, as the route does. Chat cannot be off (`ActorKey.MayBeOff` is Intake's and Ask Daoris's), so `Effective("chat")` is empty for no config the person can write; the old `?? config.Adapter` after it was unreachable and is replaced by the role's own answer, `ActorChoices.FirstAgent`, with a sentence where that too is null, so the key is read in one place only, where Chat is left unset (§4.0's *as before*). **Left, as the scope named:** the look's report before a first start's walk (`Driver.cs`'s `StartBegun`, held by LANDBACK1 part 2; the hold's `StartRun.Agent` already names the right agent once a start holds), and the helper's own policy with its *No second opinion* sentence (`HelperChoice.cs`, `ReviewerChoice.cs`, §2.4's helper row), a separate part. **Proof**, each seen failing first: `ChatChoiceTests` (7 cases, new): `ActorChoices.NewChat` as a compile red; then, with the helper built and the go-on and move unchanged, at run time the four cases of a record naming no agent (a go-on and a briefed move on Chat's choice, each with Chat unread holding with its sentence). The record-names-its-agent cases pass before and after, as guards. `DriverModuleStartFromTests`' new case holds the route on Chat's order with the machine's key naming no agent (green before: the route's order was already read first), and `DriverCommandTests`' source assertion now holds the terminal's `ActorChoices.NewChat(config)` and no `config.Adapter` in `ChatConsole`. **Ran**, green: the above and `ChatGoOnTests`, `StartFromTests`, `ContinuedChoiceTests`, `DriverModuleGoOnBriefTests`, `DriverModuleConversationTests`, `StructureTests`, `CourseCensusTests`, `LineCourseTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests`; Process: `ChatGoesOnProcessTests`, `ChatGoesOnInSyncProcessTests`, `BriefedMoveTests`, `HandOverProcessTests`, `DriverModuleStartWithProcessTests`.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1k part 2: the journal's other triggers and its change-stream family, Part B; row k is built, the install's measurement apart).** Part 2 completes row k, and nothing but the change stream reads the journal. **The triggers** (`Sessions/EventJournal.cs`, each in its own store's schema, after the columns it reads) take part 1's shape: raw transitions only, guarded `OLD.x IS NOT NEW.x`, never raising, ids cut at 64 and codes at 32, the trim in the inserting statement. Each store's schema now runs the table's creation first (`EventJournal.Table`, `IF NOT EXISTS`), since a trigger on a table whose store was opened alone on a new database would otherwise write to a table not there and fail its writer's statement. **`quest_log`** journals an `AFTER INSERT` only, `quest-op` with the quest and the operation's kind in `op`: an operation kept, made here or integrated from a remote (the quests family wakes for both); a remote's number placed on an operation, a rebase's rewrite in place and a delete of older operations are bookkeeping on an operation already kept and journal nothing, as the quests family does not wake for them. **`asks`** journals an `ask` row at an insert and one for each move of `state` (`WHEN OLD.state IS NOT NEW.state`), with the state on either side and the scope; the asks revision moves on every write, but a whole save with the state unchanged (the upsert's update path fires no insert trigger), a note, a word, a list appended to, an intake bound and a delete are not moves of the ask the design's `ask` code names, so they journal nothing. **`channel`** journals a `message` row for each insert, the person's, a session's and a teammate's mirrored alike (the design excludes a mirror for `sessions` alone, whose records are another machine's, where a mirrored message is a fact new to this machine, as the channel family's wake already treats it), with the message, its ask, entry and session as ids and `marks` as closed codes (`message`, `question` or `answer`, then `urgent` and `mirrored`); never text, repository or agent. A mirror seen again (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), a post the cap refused, a read mark and a forgotten channel are no insert, so journal nothing. **`line_code` stays empty:** the design's line codes are Daoris's lines, which are read from the quests per read and never stored (§4.2), so there is no stored line for it to code. **The family** (`Platform/ChangeStream.cs`): `journal` is a constant beside the others and is in neither `ChangeFamilies.All` nor any default answer; `Observed` (`All` then `journal`) is what the sampler follows. Its revision is the journal's newest position read from the key's own count (`sqlite_sequence`), not `MAX(position)`, so a trim or a table emptied never takes it back, and the journal's floor (the lowest position kept, one past the newest where none is) follows it. Both are appended after the five revisions in `Revisions`' one SQL, so no reader's column shifts: the families read by index 0 to 4 as before, and the array is two longer, which `IsAnother` compares whole and the sampler's moved test reads over `Observed` only. **How a reader asks (the reading taken):** the design says only that the stream names `journal` to a reader that asks, so a reader asks with a query parameter on the same route, `GET /api/changes?journal=1` (or `true`), over a cursor, since a cursor is the family-blind counter an older client already holds and a flag on it would have to survive an epoch change; `ChangeStream.WaitAsync` takes it as an optional fourth argument, defaulting to no. A reader that asks is woken by the journal, told its family after the five, told every family including it at a bootstrap, a stale or a malformed cursor, and answered `journal: { store, floor, newest }` with every read (the floor answered with every read, §4.1's Kept row); one that does not ask is answered what it always was, with no new member and woken by nothing a journal-only append does, though the sampler counts the move and re-judges every held wait (a wait that finds nothing named waits on). **The `journal` mark** is 1 in `GET /api/protocol`, so a newer client reads whether a host answers the parameter; a host before it ignores the parameter, and a client without the mark never sends it. **The older client's reader** is the driver's `LedgerChanges.RunAsync` (asks `wait` then `since` and nothing else, wakes its `ledger` topic for any family named); a service test cannot run that class, so the host and Process tests ask the route as it does and judge the rule it applies, an answer naming any family: a word taken from a connector's own process, which moves no family, wakes the asking reader with `journal` alone and the older one by nothing, which the next session write then wakes with `sessions` alone. **Proof**, each seen failing first: the 10 new `EventJournalTests` cases failing as missing rows (the remote number, rewrite and delete case passes before and after, as a guard); the 6 new `ChangeStreamTests` cases as compile reds (no `journal` family, place or parameter); the two new `ChangeStreamHostTests` cases failing with the route's reading of `journal` left out, and passing with it. Each move once in order across the families as its raw transition, with no text in a row; a same-state or note-only write, a mirror seen again and a cap-refused post journalling nothing; a row bounded and the table trimmed by each trigger's own statement; an older build's own statements minting rows and never failing; a store opened alone carrying the table; the journal outside `All`, a default answer never naming it, a journal-only append waking the asker and not the older reader, a state move naming its family to both and the journal to the asker, each journalled write waking the journal beside its family, the place and the floor with a trim and an emptied table. **Ran**, all green: `EventJournalTests` 25 (14 before), `ChangeStreamTests` 55 (49 before), `ChangeStreamHostTests` 7 (5 before), `SaidSettledHostTests`' protocol case, and `ChangeStreamProcessTests` 3 (2 before; the service has no Process settings and the class carries no trait, so it ran filtered with the project's ordinary run); and 468 cases of the store, desk, sync, lock and history classes that reach the three schemas. **Marks asserted elsewhere:** `SaidSettledHostTests`' list moves with the mark; `SessionOpensHostTests` asserts no list. **Not done, the install's:** the `sessions` write's p95 within a tenth and the journal's typical size under 5 MB, and the rows a week journals (§10's row k, §19.6): the p95 now also covers the `quest_log`, `asks` and `channel` writes, which gain one trigger insert each. **Needs you:** none.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-ii part b of 2: the live limit move's record proved through a crash at every phase and a downgrade, and the line's head read before anything is delivered; row f-ii is complete).** Built in the driver. **The proofs** run the live move (`LimitMove.MoveAsync`) in a shell of one run, stopped for good at each boundary the executor passes, which is blocking there as a killed process never leaves it, its run then declared dead by the lease's own test and its records ended as a restarted service leaves a dead shell's; and a shell that comes up, the real chat runner over the real protocol stub, takes the move up and its destination's go-on carries what waits. `ConversationMoves.Cut` is told each boundary as it is passed (`Cuts`: `decided`, `old-ended`, `opening` with the open not made, `answered` with it made and its answer unwritten, `opened`, `marked`, `written` after the door's write returned and before `sent`, `sent` before the take, `taken` before `delivered`); nothing in production sets it. **Row by row at a come-up:** `decided` and `old-ended` fall back `not-opened` with the word still on the old part; `opening`, with or without the open made, is held `unrecorded` and closed; `opened` and `marked` are closed with their words waiting never attempted, carried once by the destination's go-on (the stub hears the word once); `written` is held `undelivered`, the word attempted without `sent` settled uncertain with the `lost` note once, never carried again; `sent` and `taken` close `delivered` by `sent`, the word taken off where it still waited. The key of a move at `opening` is never asked again, and the shell let go after its take-up changes nothing: its claim is gone. **The downgrade run** is the same kill, then an older build's come-up, the runner whose move holder reads every run as alive and so takes nothing up, then this build's return, then a second come-up of this build that finds nothing to do: the older build's statements are the go-on's, which row f-ii did not change, and it writes no `lost` note for a moved word, the killed shell's door recording the word's own event before its write as the real door does. At `answered` the older go-on carries the word once and the return closes the move; at `opened` and `marked` the older go-on carries it to a successor of the destination, so the return finds the head past the destination, reads the word gone without `sent`, *left*, and the hold, `superseded`, names nothing that waits and closes; at `written` the older come-up takes the uncertain word back with no note and the return holds the move `undelivered` naming it *left*; at `sent` and `taken` the older take-back changes nothing and the return closes `delivered` by `sent`. **The line's head** (§13.4 step 5, §19.3) is read before anything is delivered: `IMoveSaid.HeadPastAsync` (a default member answering none, the service's door answering `WordsNever.ContinuedIn` over the records, as the go-on reads a record that went on) and `MoveDelivery.HeldPastAsync`, which the one reading calls first at `opened` to `delivering` where a word still lacks its disposition, and which the live executor calls between `opened` and `delivering`, so nothing is marked or handed to a part an older build has continued. It holds the move `superseded`, naming the person's own waiting words *held* for their take-back and saying at the head, the words gone without `sent` *left*, the attempted ones uncertain, settled and said, and nothing for another author's waiting words, which stay on the destination as claims (`MoveWaiting.Person`); a word with `sent` that still waits is taken off as taken first. The take-up of a dead run's move before its open, whose old part an older build has since continued (a record from it that no key of the move's made, since none was drawn), closes `superseded` rather than falling back. **Readings taken:** (1) *A move found at `opening` is held `unrecorded` and closed, and asks its open again under no key.* The design's re-ask at that phase (§13.4 step 3, §13.5's `opening` row) is the executor's own retry of an answer lost inside its run, after the host's marks are read again, and a taker holds none of what the open's delivery needs: the brief, the refused turns and the walk's account are the dead executor's, so a re-ask could only learn the destination and then hold the move `undelivered` naming none, which is where this take-up already leaves it; and where the open was made, the destination and its moved words are a record of the line's own, which its go-on carries, once, as the `answered` case shows, while a destination an older build deleted needs no answer to be closed. (2) The head is not read at `opening`, where the open's own destination is a successor of the old part by the move's key, and is read before the open only as the take-up reads a record with no key drawn. (3) A hold's note and its offering of the person's words for saying again, and the doors that press it, are not built: no door calls `MoveResolve.PressedAsync`, so the person's take-back and word at the head are made here at the service and the look closes the hold by its one rule; the hold names what each press needs. (4) A refused open (the old part runs again or has a successor) closes the move held `unrecorded` with its note on the line, as part a left it, and not `superseded` or `gone`, since the open's answer does not carry the refusal's code to the move. (5) A later run on the old part, which the table's first row also reads as superseding, is not read: only a record from it is.

**Proof**, red where the case was a change, a guard where the case already held: `MoveDeliveryTests` (4, new) and `LimitMoveStoreTests` (3, new) were seen failing with the head read neutralized (the hold written with no head, the take-up falling back, the live executor handing words to a continued part), and their one guard, a head past a destination whose every word has `sent`, closes `delivered` as before. The crash and downgrade cases hold by part a's design, so they are guards: each was seen to bite with the `sent` write removed, which failed the `sent` and `taken` cases of the crash theory. **Process**, each run once with `process.runsettings`: `LimitMoveCrashProcessTests` 10 (a census and nine boundaries; 20 s), `LimitMoveDowngradeProcessTests` 10 (56 s), `LimitMoveOlderBuildProcessTests` 4 (9 s): an older build's successor of the destination opened before it moved the words, the move held and the person's take-back and word at the head each once, the look closing it; Codex's delayed older move, its copy delivered once on its part after the newer move delivered once on its own, nothing a third time; and an older shell's resume of the old part beside the newer open, in both orders, the word once and the open refused or the resume refused. **Ran**, green: those, `MoveTakeUpTests`, `MoveDeliveryTests`, `MoveResolveTests`, `LimitMoveStoreTests`, `MoveStoreTests`, `MoveStoreCensusTests`, `CourseCensusTests`, `LineCourseTests`, `ChatGoOnTests`, `NoteCodesTests`, the driver's `StructureTests`, `SystemWiringTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests`. **Not run, the parent's:** `LimitWaitsProcessTests`, `LimitGoesOnProcessTests`, `LimitGoesElsewhereProcessTests`, `ChatWordsRestartProcessTests` and the rest of the Process half.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-iii part a: the curve's move keeps its move record in the store, and writes no hand-over box beside it).** Branch iii of Part B's row 1f-ii to 1f-iv, part a; the crash, downgrade and older-build process proofs of the curve's move are part b's. **Decided where it is judged:** `CurveMove.MoveAsync` keeps the box guard (a box an older build left is still taken by the hand-over's own take, `TakeAllAsync` and `Due`), judges the hand-over's own refusals quietly before anything is written (`HandOver.JudgeAsync`, which the box's ask judged before it kept one), then decides through its own `Moves` holder (`ConversationMoves`, over the home's one store file shared with the limit move's holder, so the shell's come-up takes a dead curve move up through `_limit.Moves.ComeUpAsync` unchanged): executor `conversation`, how `brief`, why `curve`, by `daoris`, the account the decision named, the brief's recipe `curve` with no bound, since the curve has no refused turns. A record a move still stands for takes no move and a store that will not take the decision moves nothing, both quiet, the next turn's end judging again. **Taken:** `ChatHandOver` has a take beside the box's that is handed the request the decision made rather than reading a box, and the one body behind both (`TakeCoreAsync`) hands `HandOverTake.RunAsync` the same sequence, so the person's and the agent's hand-overs, whose box and reading stay, are untouched (row f-v). The move's phases are written around that sequence: `old-ended` and `opening` under the key the open is sent with at the end of the old part (a write the store refuses opens nothing, said as the open's refusal), the open keyed with `MovedWords.Asked(id, [], [])` as the limit move asks it where nothing waits, `opened` with whatever the open moved, `delivering` and the prompt's mark before the hand, and, where the line's head has gone on past the new part (an older build), nothing is handed. The prompt's outcome is written as the door's write returns, before the take (`ChatMessage.Delivered` for a turned door; the return of the send for a text door), as `Wire([])` with no word's `sent`, because the curve's composed message carries no word id; the one reading then closes the move `delivered` on that outcome. A move whose hand was not made (the open refused, a refused store write, a new part that took no hand) is ended by its phase in a `finally`, its prompt known unwritten; a turns' hold refused, or a take already under way, because a word started a turn meanwhile, closes it `superseded`, and the take's `TakeEnded` still tells the person's held words to go on. The context's `CurveMoves` dictionary, which carried the decision from the ask to the box's take, is gone with the box: a curve box an older build left is taken with the hand-over's own two lines, as it was where the decision was not held. **Reading taken:** a word the person kept on the old part after its end, which the keyed open moves, waits on the new part never attempted and is carried by that part's own go-on, the move closing `delivered` or held `undelivered` naming none by the delivery's one reading; before, such a word waited on the old record for the hand-over's `TakeEnded`; that case is read from the delivery's rules and is not proved by a process case yet (part b's). **Proof:** `CurveMoveStoreTests` (new, 9 cases and a two-row theory), seen failing first (the file did not compile without the holder, and the census row, the hand-over's unkeyed take and the destination assertion each failed once before the code and stand-ins were right), and `MoveStoreCensusTests` and `CourseCensusTests` extended: the curve's move is a reader of the decision types alone and a named record of row f-iii. `CurveMoveTests` re-points its case to the record; `CurveMoveProcessTests` waits for the record's `delivered` where it waited for a box to go, and `course-cases.txt` names the renamed case.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-iv part a of 2: the driven carry-on keeps its move record).** The third row whose move writes f-i's record, after the live limit move (the curve, row f-iii, is built beside it); part b adds `sent` at each door's write return with the take moved after it, the driven take-up and the Process proofs, and none of that is built here. **The holder** is `Course/ConversationMoves` again, its take-up scope now a parameter (the conversations' by default); the driver's `Moves` is built over the lease holder's scope, executor `driven`, so there is one store, one decision and one reading of a delivery whichever executor writes the move. **The decision** (`CarryOnRecord.Decision`, called in `Driver.RunAsync` once the walk has settled the account and the moved words are named, before the keyed open): executor `driven`, how `brief`, its why and asker the record's own `from` link (`carry-on`, `retry`, `limit` or `curve`), the settled selection's account, the agent the new part opens on. It is written at `old-ended` and not at `decided`: the session a carry-on goes on from has ended, so the design's move from an ended record (§3.3) applies and the old part's end is no phase of its own here. **A carry-on of the quest's hand-over keeps none** (row f-v's). A move already standing for the session it goes on from, or a store that will not take the decision, holds the start for the next look, its sentence saying which; nothing is opened and nothing written. **The phases:** `opening` under a key drawn before the open and handed to it (`OpenSessionMovingAsync`'s `opens`, in place of the key it drew for itself), so a move's `opens` is the key its open was sent with. The open is keyed for every carry-on that keeps a record, with words or without: one that hands none sends the key and asks no word to move, since the host moves the person's words kept since the read wherever it is asked to move any, so an empty ask would have moved words the course never named. Then `opened` with every word the open moved by its id, the line's head read (as the live move reads it), `delivering` and the prompt's mark, all before the take. **Today's take is the delivery here.** The moved words are taken off the new part before its session spawns, into the ask its instruction reads (`HandedMovedAsync`), so each word's `sent` and the prompt's outcome are written where that take returns, then the one reading closes the move `delivered`; a carry-on that hands no word, or whose host moves none, closes on its prompt's outcome once the old record's words are taken off. **What part b changes** is exactly this point: `sent` moves to each door's write return (the native new part's start returning its process; the protocol door's first prompt write, the run's `prompted`, and after a transient failure the episode's try's `OnWire`) with the take after it, and a dead loop's driven move is taken up at the watch's first look. **Ended by its phase** on every way out of the start (`CarryOnRecord.DisposeAsync`), and explicitly once the take is made, so the record never stands for as long as the session runs and the quest's close cannot race its end: before the open, `fell-back` `not-opened`; with the open asked and no answer recorded (a refused open, a lost answer), held `unrecorded` and closed; past the fence with the take not made, its prompt `unsent`, held `undelivered` naming none and closed, the moved words waiting on the new session never attempted for its own go-on. A write the store refuses after the open stops the take of the moved words, which wait on the new session, and says so on the session's console. **Readings taken:** (1) *Until part b's take-up, a loop that dies between the decision and the end leaves a standing move that holds that line's carry-on at each look.* No look takes up a driven move yet, and the hold's sentence names the standing move; every other way out of the start ends the record. (2) *Where the take is before the write, a crash between the take and `sent` reads the word* left*, held, never sent twice,* which is the conservative side the design reads a word gone without `sent`. (3) *A no-word carry-on is now judged by the keyed open's own checks* (the record left stands, is its line's head and is not active), which an unkeyed open never asked: a refusal is the start's refused open, as a carry-on with words already met. (4) *The row's hand-over is told apart by the start's own `handedFrom`,* the quest's hand-over held, never by the why, so a hand-over's `limit` or `retry` is never mistaken for a carry-on's.

**Proof,** red where the case was a change: `DrivenCarryOnStoreTests` (6, new) was seen failing to compile before the record existed; `SessionMessagesTickTests`' three new cases (a fallback's record with its word, a carry-on of no word at the host's marks 2 and 1) were seen failing, the store holding no move for the line, with the decision neutralised. The census (`CourseCensusTests`' `records` row, `MoveStoreCensusTests`' readers) and `course-cases.txt` (which also takes the prompt-on-stdin case AGENTLYNTAI1s-i left unlisted) name the new site. **Ran**, green: `DrivenCarryOnStoreTests`, `CourseCensusTests`, `MoveStoreCensusTests`, `LineCourseTests`, `ContinuationTests`, `CarryOnHandedTests`, `LimitMoveStoreTests`, `MoveTakeUpTests`, `MoveDeliveryTests`, `MoveStoreTests`, the driver's `StructureTests`, `ParticipantWorkReadersTests`, `SystemWiringTests`, `ContinuedReadersCensusTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests`. **Process,** each class once with `process.runsettings`: `SessionMessagesTickTests` 25 (4 min 10 s), `LimitResumeTickTests` with `AccessSwapTickTests` 12 (46 s), `TransientRetryTickTests` with `HandOverLookTests` 12 (58 s), and the tick classes that reach a carry-on through a stand-in service, `AccountRotationTickTests`, `TurnCarryTickTests`, `RetryWorkTickTests`, `MakerTrailTickTests`, `AccountLimitTickTests`, `BroughtForwardLookTests`, `HandOverQuestTickTests`, `StartInSyncTickTests` and `AnswerContinuesTickTests`, 39 together (1 min 38 s). **Not run, the parent's:** the family rehearsal, `LimitMoveCrashProcessTests` and the rest of the Process half; a carry-on's open now carries its key at every host mark, which no rehearsal compares.

**Note, 2026-10-30 (FOUNDATION1f-iii part b of 2: the curve's move record proved through a crash at every boundary, a downgrade and the older build's cases, and the word kept after the old part's end proved; branch iii is complete).** Tests only; no production line changed, since no proof showed a defect. **The boundaries the curve passes** are all nine of `Cuts.All`, which a census pins by running a move to its end and listing what it passed, once each, in the executor's order; the difference from the limit move is what two of them mean. The curve's one message is the brief, which carries no word's id, so `sent` is the prompt's outcome written as the door's write returns (`Wire([])`), not a word's, and `written` is the brief's write made with that outcome not yet kept. A word the open moves is never written by the move: it waits on the new part never attempted and the part's own go-on carries it. `LiveLimitHarness` gained a twin run for the curve (`RunCurveShellAsync`: the conversation's account past its curve, its last turn ended, its agent with a usage question and the clock the curve's own cases hold), over the same kill, come-up and stub, and the harness's shell run now holds either move. **Each boundary is run with the person's word kept on the old part and with none**, because a move that moved nothing is read by its prompt's outcome alone. **Rows at a come-up:** `decided` and `old-ended` fall back `not-opened`, the old part's own go-on carrying the word on it once; `opening` and `answered` close held `unrecorded`, the word carried once by the old part's go-on before the open was made and by the destination's after it, the open never asked again under its key; `opened` closes held `undelivered` naming nothing; `marked` and `written` close held `unrecorded` (the prompt's effect with no outcome, never made again), the brief written once by the dead shell at `written` and never before; `sent` and `taken` close `delivered` by the prompt's outcome where no word was moved, and held `undelivered` naming nothing where one was, since no write of the move's gives that word its `sent` and the destination's go-on carries it. The word is heard once in every row, by the go-on of the part it waited on, and a shell let go finds its claim gone and changes nothing. **The downgrade run** is the same kill, an older build's come-up (the move holder reading every run as alive, so taking nothing up), then this build's return, then a second come-up that finds nothing to do. With no word moved, the older build has nothing to carry and the return reads what this build's own come-up reads. With one moved: at `decided` and `old-ended` the older go-on carries the word on a record that continues the old part, which no key of the move's made, so the return closes the move `superseded` rather than falling back; at `opening` and `answered` the move closes held `unrecorded` as before, the older go-on's successor not read (D212's note on f-ii part b, reading 2); from `opened` to `taken` the older go-on carries the word to a successor of the destination, the return finds the head past the destination, reads the word gone without `sent`, *left*, and closes held `superseded` naming nothing that waits, with the brief written once or never and the word once. **Reading to know:** a word the destination's go-on carried, or an older build's, is named *left* by a hold that reads it after, as the limit move's `opened` row has it since f-ii; the hold is read by no door that offers the person the word again, so nothing is said to the person twice. **The older build's cases** mirror f-ii's: a successor an older build opened before it moved the destination's words (the move held `superseded` naming the person's word, the person's take-back and word at the head once each, the look closing it); Codex's delayed older move (the newer move's word once on its part, the older copy once on its own, nothing a third time); an older shell's resume of the old part beside the newer open, in both orders (the open refused, or the resume refused, the word moved to the new part and carried once by its go-on). **The reading part a took, now proved:** a word the person says on the old part after its end is moved by the keyed open, named in the move's record never attempted, carried once by the new part's go-on, the move closed held `undelivered` naming nothing; the record it left holds nothing, and a second and third come-up send nothing more. **Mutants, each seen failing then reverted** (the crash and downgrade rows hold by part a's design, so they are guards): the prompt's outcome write removed from `Wire` failed the `sent` and `taken` rows of the crash theory (four of them) and of the downgrade theory with no word moved; the open's answer recorded with no moved words failed eleven cases: two of the crash theory (`sent` and `taken` with a word moved), five of the downgrade theory (`opened` to `taken` with a word moved), and four of the older-build class, among them the case for a word kept after the end. **`SessionWords.TakeWaits`** (modules) reads the hand-over box alone to hold the person's words while a take runs and to try them once it is told over. A curve take now keeps no box, so a curve take engages it no longer: a word said on the old part before the open is moved by the keyed open, and one said after it is said at the line's head (the part's own door), where the hold would have kept it behind the brief. The window between the open's answer and the brief's hand is short, and the live limit move has had no box there since f-ii, so nothing is left broken that was not; whether `TakeWaits` should read a standing move for the record (the store's `StandingOf`) so a word said in that window waits for the brief is a later row's, in the modules lane, which this part did not edit. **Process**, each run once with `process.runsettings`: `CurveMoveCrashProcessTests` 19 (a census and eighteen boundary cases; 43 s), `CurveMoveDowngradeProcessTests` 19 (a census and eighteen cases; 1 min 16 s), `CurveMoveOlderBuildProcessTests` 5 (11 s), and the limit move's three classes beside, whose harness this part changed, 24 cases unchanged and green (39 s). **Ran**, green: those, `CurveMoveStoreTests`, `MoveStoreCensusTests`, `CourseCensusTests`, the driver's `StructureTests`, `GitSeedTests`, `ShortWaitsCensusTests` (its census made the new classes wait on `Poll.Patience`), and `tools/partials.mjs`, `read-shares.mjs`, `optional-locks.mjs` and `test-map.mjs --check`. `LineCourseTests` fails on one case, `SessionMessagesTickTests`' native-session prompt case (a table of 19, a suite of 20), which the merged batch brought in and this part does not touch.
